# Decisions

Every decision that shapes Magick Agency, by ID, in one place. Read it when you need to know
whether something is settled, why the code is built the way it is, or what is still waiting on
someone. Source comments cite entries here by ID ("decision B8", "Q6 (Manas, 2026-10-09)"), so
IDs are never renumbered or reused. As of 2026-11-09.

Status words: **settled** (a founding product decision, not reopened here); **default, pending
Manas** (built with the recommended option, not yet ratified); **open** (no default yet; a launch
concern); **decided — keep** (Manas ruled the current behaviour stays); **decided — changed**
(Manas ruled a change, and it is implemented).

## 1. Settled product decisions (approved by Manas, 2026-10-08)

| # | Decision |
|---|---|
| S1 | Magick Agency is a self-contained application: its own server, database, Redis, storage and carrier account. It depends on vendors only, never on another service at runtime |
| S2 | It owns its voice engine (the WebRTC bridge and the VoiceLink adapter) and its own VoiceLink capacity: account, numbers and concurrency limits |
| S3 | It owns post-call analysis and transcripts, and its identity data: users, tenants, accounts, memberships, roles, invites |
| S4 | Sign-in uses Firebase Authentication, in a Firebase project that may be shared with other products; agency verifies ID tokens against Google's public keys and its own `FIREBASE_PROJECT_ID`, with no service account (Manas, 2026-10-10; see §4). A separate identity layer may come later |
| S5 | Super-admins create tenants and add users, with their own login and their own console |
| S6 | No credits, billing or credit enforcement in v1. Metering is an open item |
| S7 | AI calling is out of scope |
| S8 | Stack: Node 22, Fastify, TypeScript, raw `pg`, node-pg-migrate, Vitest; Vite + React UIs; pnpm workspaces |

## 2. Launch decisions

Nine decisions about going live. Where there was a recommended option, it is built and recorded
as **default, pending Manas**.

| # | Question | Default used | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Invites still outstanding on the previous platform at launch | Re-issue them (only token hashes are stored, so they cannot be imported) | default, pending Manas |
| 2 | Existing phone numbers | — (move them to agency's VoiceLink account if VoiceLink allows, or buy new ones; a new caller ID is visible to customers) | open |
| 3 | Inbound calls to agency numbers in v1 | Play a message and hang up. **Superseded for Plivo numbers linked to inbound** by IB1–IB8 (2026-11-09, [`inbound.md`](inbound.md)); unlinked numbers and VoiceLink keep this default | default, pending Manas |
| 4 | Abandon clip | Uploaded audio file only, no TTS | default, pending Manas |
| 5 | Platform API keys | None in v1 | default, pending Manas |
| 6 | Super-admins | Created fresh in agency, not imported | default, pending Manas |
| 7 | Launch style | — (whole environment at once, or tenant by tenant; a tenant must sit wholly in one system) | open |
| 8 | Domain, feature freeze on the previous platform, rollback window | — (7 days proposed for the rollback window) | open |
| 9 | Metering | Not in v1 | open item |

## 3. Build decisions

In one line each:

- **B1** one `tsconfig` per package, tests included, so lint catches a broken test.
- **B2** workspace packages are consumed as TypeScript source; no build step between packages.
- **B3** one Postgres (5436, two databases) and one Redis (6383, db 0 dev / db 1 test) for dev and test.
- **B4** one file per module area for config, routes and background work.
- **B5** the server keeps CommonJS runtime semantics (esbuild `format: cjs`).
- **B6** agency's alert rules and dashboard are a Terraform module in `grafana/`, with its own state (in S3, its own key); routing comes from the Grafana stack's shared policy.
- **B7** two audit tables (`audit_logs` and `platform_audit_log`), written by one process.
- **B8** DNC is one table: an indexed, fail-closed dial-time read and one transactional mark.
- **B9** the campaign-management layer sits on top of the dialer domain and calls its repository in-process.
- **B10** infrastructure used by more than one module area lives in one shared place.
- **B11** cross-module seams are fixed files in `apps/server/src/seams/`.
- **B12** the agency repository (`agency.repository.ts`, a barrel over `src/db/repositories/agency/`) lives in `apps/server/src/db/`, not `packages/db`, to avoid a package cycle.
- **B13** the guard host counts live bridged calls, so self-heal stays armed during calls.
- **B14** one S3 module, one bucket, one client, one config key.
- **B15** roster replace and clear refuse until a supersede primitive exists.
- **B16** the public API layer serves the console's paths and reaches the internal handler instance in-process through `callCore`.
- **B17** Magick Agency is a standalone brand; nothing user-visible links to or names another product. Amended 2026-10-11: the UIs show the logo artwork.
- **B23** UI pieces both SPAs share live in one workspace package, `packages/web-ui`, consumed as TypeScript source.
- **B24** every workspace package has its own semantic version and changelog, released by release-please from Conventional Commit PR titles.
- **B26** signed-in console URLs name their tenant and account, `/t/:tenantId/a/:accountId/…`, as the router's basename.
- **B28** the console has one shell for every signed-in page but the agent's full-viewport ones, with a Settings hub; supersedes the agency/`/app` two-shell split.
- **B28a** a supervisor's Home page (`/agency/home`) and landing: what needs them, the floor, today so far. Redesigned as Live floor (LF1–LF10, B28b), 2026-11-18.
- **B28b** the Live floor's server reads: the account's floor in one read, the promises summary, and who paused a campaign on Home's board.

The full entries:

| # | Decision | Why |
|---|---|---|
| B1 | One `tsconfig` per package covering `src` and `test`, `moduleResolution: Bundler`, `noEmit` | Lint must catch a broken test. Nothing is emitted by `tsc`: the server is bundled by esbuild, the UIs by Vite |
| B2 | Workspace packages are consumed as TypeScript source (package `exports` point at `src/*.ts`) | No build step between packages; esbuild and Vite compile them in place |
| B3 | Dev and test share one Postgres container (5436) with two databases, and one Redis (6383) with db 0 for dev and db 1 for tests | Two ports that collide with no other local stack. The test harnesses refuse any other port, any database not named `magick_agency_test*`, and Redis db 0 |
| B4 | Each module area (platform, agency, voice, analysis) has exactly one config block (`apps/server/src/config/blocks/<area>.ts`), one route plugin (`apps/server/src/api/<area>.plugin.ts`) and one bootstrap (`apps/server/src/bootstrap/<area>.ts`) | Each area's surface is in one place, and config blocks must declare disjoint top-level keys (checked at load and by a test). See `docs/seams.md` |
| B5 | Server modules keep CommonJS runtime semantics (esbuild `format: cjs`) | Server code uses `__dirname` and CommonJS-shaped imports |
| B6 | Agency's alert rules and dashboard live in this repo, in `grafana/`: a Terraform root module with its own state (from 2026-11-13 in the S3 state bucket, key `grafana/terraform.tfstate`; it was local), applied on its own. 20 `agy-*` rules (13, plus `agy-cluster-child-crash` from 2026-11-02, the five `insights` group rules for the rollup and pacing history from 2026-11-04 and `agy-pacing-share-starved` from 2026-11-05 (MC7), all warnings) select the service names `magick-agency(-.+)?` (production `magick-agency`, `magick-agency-Staging`, `magick-agency-Dedicated`) and carry the labels the Grafana stack's shared notification policy routes on, so they page the same PagerDuty service as the rest of the stack. The module manages no contact point, policy or mute timing. `pnpm test:grafana` checks every rule and panel against the metric declarations | Manas, 2026-10-09: "the alerts also should live in agency". The routing labels are a contract with the stack's policy, which this repo does not own (`grafana/README.md`). Metrics agency does not emit yet are listed there under "Known metric gaps" |
| B7 | Two audit tables: `audit_logs` (dialer events, the "Dialer" half of a campaign's activity trail) and `platform_audit_log` (console and admin actions, the "Console" half) | The campaign activity trail merges the two, each with its own retention horizon. One process writes both |
| B8 | DNC is one table, `dnc_entries`. The dial-time check is one indexed read (`idx_dnc_entries_tenant_phone`), widened by scope (tenant, account, campaign), and fails closed on a database error. An agent's mark writes `dnc_entries` in the same transaction as the attempt bookkeeping. No Redis copy, sync or versioning. `agency_dnc_outbox` exists in the schema, unused, reserved for mirroring DNC changes to the previous platform during a launch rollback window | One table and one transactional write leave no window in which a marked number can still be dialled. A database error halts the dial, and a halt aborts the whole claimed batch; both are tested |
| B9 | Two layers in the agency domain: the dialer domain (pure rules in `packages/domain`, the agency repository, DNC) and, on top of it, campaign management (ingest, staffing, activity, agent identity, the spine, campaign config and wire shapes, stats enrichment), which calls the repository in-process | Campaign management depends on the repository, never the reverse |
| B10 | Infrastructure used by more than one module area lives in one shared place: shared repositories in `packages/db`, the feature-flag service in `apps/server/src/feature-flags/`, both audit modules in `apps/server/src/audit/` (see `docs/seams.md`) | One owner per shared piece; no module area keeps a private copy |
| B11 | Cross-module seams are fixed files in `apps/server/src/seams/`, plus the bridge API the dialer runtime calls (`docs/seams.md`) | Module areas talk through fixed contracts that are pinned by tests |
| B12 | The agency repository, `agency.repository.ts` (a barrel over `src/db/repositories/agency/`: one file per repository class plus the SQL leaves they share), and its model live in `apps/server/src/db/`, not `packages/db`; `packages/domain` holds only leaf modules. Wording amended 2026-10-17: the barrel, and no DNC outbox repository (none exists; `agency_dnc_outbox` has no reader or writer) | The repository imports about fifteen `apps/server/src/agency/` modules, several of which import the agency model; putting it in `packages/db` would make db, domain and server cyclic |
| B13 | The guard host reports the bridge's live WebRTC calls as `getActiveCallCount`, so the self-heal poll stays armed while a bridged call holds slots | The bridged call is the only call type; reporting 0 would leave the poll dormant through every call. Lead decision |
| B14 | One S3 module, `apps/server/src/storage/s3.ts`, reading `config.s3`, including `getFileStream` and `headFile` for ingest | One bucket, one client, one config key |
| B15 | Roster replace and clear refuse: `supersedeRoster` throws `unsupported` and writes nothing, so a `mode: 'replace'` ingest fails `replace_unsupported` and `POST /campaigns/:id/roster/clear` refuses. Both are behind `AGENCY_ROSTER_REPLACE_ENABLED`, off by default | The schema has no supersede primitive (no `superseded_at`, no retired contact state, no contact `ingest_job_id`). Building one is three schema changes plus a replace lock: new work, pending Manas. The console does not send either request. When it is built, the replace also clears the owner of every contact it retires, with a history row each (CA11) |
| B16 | The public API layer serves the paths the console calls (`/proxy/agency/...`, `/dnc`, `/phone-numbers`, `/proxy/call-analysis-profiles`, `/super-admin/...`). Its handlers authenticate, check tenancy and RBAC, validate and enrich, then run the dialer's handlers on the internal handler instance in-process through `callCore` | One process, no network hop and no service credentials between the layers. The `/proxy` prefix is cosmetic and can be renamed in one coordinated server and console change. The internal handlers are modules, not URL surfaces |
| B17 | Branding (Manas, 2026-10-09). Magick Agency is a standalone product: nothing in the console, the super-admin console or anything the server sends links to or names another product. Console default brand pack `magick-agency` ("Magick Agency" / "MA", no tagline; the mark was a text tile with no logo file until the 2026-10-11 amendment below); no external docs link; originators `magick-agency-console` / `magick-agency-super-admin`; localStorage keys `magick-agency-*`; server `PLATFORM_BRAND_NAME` default "Magick Agency" (product noun "Magick Agency Dialer"); masked errors say "contact support" with no address; CSV preambles say "Magick Agency". The console's `/app` is its own platform zone (Team, Notifications, Call summaries); the exits to it read "Team & settings" (agency shell) and "Go to settings" (`AgentHomePage`, `WorkspaceExit`). (Superseded by B28: one shell, Settings at `/settings`. `AgentHomePage`'s exit for a viewer or operator now reads "Open the console" and opens the campaign list, B28a.) A guard test in each app fails on any non-comment mention of a parent product's name. Internal `x-mgkvc-*` header names are wire names and unchanged (follow-up). **Amended 2026-10-11 (Manas):** the UIs use the logo artwork, the same mark the parent product used, as the brand logo and favicon. The default pack ships `public/logo.png` (256 px, cropped from `source/logo-1024.png`) and `public/favicon.png`, named by the pack's new optional `logo` / `favicon` fields; the super-admin app carries byte-identical copies in its `public/` (pinned by `brandAssets.test.ts`). A pack without `logo` keeps the text tile. The parent product's name and links stay out, and the console guard still refuses the parent product's old brand pack folder | Manas's ruling: "this shouldn't have any linking present"; amendment: use the product's logo and favicon in the UI |
| B23 | `packages/web-ui` holds the UI pieces the console and the super-admin console share, consumed as TypeScript source like the contracts (B2): the error and format helpers, `parseApiErrorBody` (both API clients), the telephony-provider aliases, the 15 shared components with their CSS modules, `ToastContext`, `global.css`, `useAsyncResource`, `useDialogFocus` (the one focus-trap implementation) and `mapWithConcurrency`. `react`, `react-dom`, `react-router-dom` and `lucide-react` are peer dependencies. It carries its own B17 no-parent-brand scan (`packages/web-ui/test/noParentBrand.test.ts`), because each app's guard scans only its own `src/`. `docker/web.Dockerfile` copies it into the UI build | One copy of each shared piece instead of a console and a super-admin copy that drift apart; no build step, as for every workspace package. Decided in the tidy-up plan Manas approved on 2026-10-16 |
| B24 | Per-component semantic versioning with release-please (`release-please-config.json`, `.release-please-manifest.json`, `.github/workflows/release-please.yml`). Every workspace package is a component with its own version, `<component>-v<version>` tag, GitHub Release and `CHANGELOG.md`; the deployables are `server`, `console` and `super-admin`. Commits are assigned to components by the files they touch; the `node-workspace` plugin gives the dependents of a released shared package a patch release. PR titles are Conventional Commits (`.github/workflows/pr-title.yml`) because squash merging makes the title the commit release-please reads. Pre-1.0, a breaking change bumps the minor. Every component starts at 0.1.0, the state of `main` at `bootstrap-sha`. `tooling/release-please.test.mjs` keeps the config, the manifest and the `package.json` versions in step. Detail in `operations.md`, "Releases and versioning" | release-please has no way to add paths to a component, so the server's and the UIs' versions can only follow `packages/*` if those packages are components too; versioning them is what lets a `fix(db)` or `fix(contracts)` move the server's version instead of shipping inside an unchanged one. Asked for by Manas on 2026-10-23 (server and console with their own versions) |
| B26 | Every signed-in console page is served under `/t/:tenantId/a/:accountId/…` (`apps/console/src/utils/urlScope.ts`, over the shape in `packages/contracts/src/api/platform/console-urls.ts`, which the server's emailed links use too). The prefix is the router's basename (`components/routing/ScopedRouter`, over the history `contexts/UrlScopeContext` owns), so routes and links are written without it; `TenantContext` follows the URL (`localStorage` is only the fallback for an unscoped URL), and a tenant or account switch is a full page load at the new account's home (`utils/pageLoad.ts`; a caller may name the path, as the soft phone does for the station); `components/routing/ScopeGate` scopes an unscoped signed-in URL to the active account and refuses a link to a tenant or account the viewer cannot open instead of opening another. `/login`, `/agency/login`, `/join/:token`, `/agency/join/:token` and `/verify-email` stay unscoped. PostHog receives the prefix as `/t/:tenant/a/:account` (`analytics/redact.ts`). The campaign-completion email links under the campaign's own scope, unscoped when its account does not resolve (`agencyCampaignUrl`, `apps/server/src/notifications/agency-campaign-completion.ts`). Built 2026-11-05 | A link copied from the address bar opens the same tenant and account for whoever it is sent to, instead of whichever one their browser last had open. `/t` and `/a` rather than `/tenants` and `/accounts`, which are API prefixes that nginx and the Vite dev proxy send to the server. A basename keeps the scope out of every existing route, link and `useLocation()` reader |
| B27 | The API can have its own host (`api.example.com`), opt-in. The console is built with `CONSOLE_API_BASE_URL` and calls that origin; the server registers `@fastify/cors` only when `CORS_ALLOWED_ORIGINS` is set (`apps/server/src/api/middleware/cors.ts`): exact origins (normalised at boot; any other origin, or none, gets CORS off for that request), no credentials (bearer auth, no cookies), request headers reflected, `CORS_EXPOSED_HEADERS` exposed, preflights cached 2 h and answered before the rate limiter and the auth layer (so they are charged to no bucket: each is a header-only 204, accepted). Both names reach the same nginx :8080 block, so `TRUST_PROXY_HOPS` is unchanged. The super-admin is never given the API base: it calls its own origin, and :8081 stays the only block that proxies `/super-admin`. Unset, the deployment is single-origin as before | Separate names for the app, the API and the admin, so the mobile app, VoiceLink and the console each point at the API by name and the apex domain stays free for a marketing site. Chosen by Manas on 2026-11-04 over naming an API host while keeping the console same-origin |
| B28 | One console shell and a Settings hub (2026-11-11, Stage 1 of the navigation redesign Manas approved). Every signed-in console page except the agent's full-viewport surfaces (`/station`, `/dialer/*`) renders in `ConsoleLayout` (`apps/console/src/components/layout/`), mounted by `ConsoleShell`. Sidebar: the tenant and account context block (the existing switchers; a plain label when there is one of each), then Campaigns, Inbound (with the missed-calls badge), Reports, Do Not Call and Take calls (a link out to `/dialer`, for whoever holds `agency.station.connect` without `agency.supervise`: a supervisor joins a campaign as an agent from the campaign), then Settings at the foot of the same Main landmark (left out for a dedicated agent while the dialer is on, since `AgentLanding` would send them to `/dialer`) and the version. Each entry's visibility is the union of the gates of the pages it groups (permissions, flags, capabilities, the missed-calls rule), and it is active on all their URLs; "New campaign" is not an entry (the Campaigns page has the button). Grouped pages keep their URLs and get an in-page tab bar, each tab gated as its page, none when one tab is left: Campaigns · Soft-phone calls, Missed calls · Summary, Analytics · Exports (the export list, `/agency/reports`, under the Reports section; amended 2026-11-12: the list is called Exports wherever it is named, in its tab, breadcrumb and page title, and the report-ready email's link says "Open the report in Exports"; "report" is one file, Reports the section, and Home's Today so far links to Analytics as "Open Analytics"). Top bar: breadcrumb (on a phone the current page, with a 44px back arrow up to the nearest crumb above it that is a link; no crumb links to the page it is on once redirects have run, so a campaign's Overview goes back to the Campaigns list and Analytics has no arrow; pages leave their own breadcrumbs out), one search field, profile menu (a disclosure: name, email, tenant and account ids, theme, sign out). The field and ⌘K open `GlobalSearch` on every page: pages (renamed ones by their old names too), Settings entries, campaigns, each campaign's own pages ("Diwali renewals › Call log", by the tab's gate), each campaign Settings section the page has (`…/settings#<id>`, IN14; Agents only with contact assignment or inbound on), Customer history and a phone number's customer history, each under its destination's gate and never a route that would send the reader elsewhere; it replaced the separate "Search a number" popover. A number's refusal is not shown when anything else matched (a campaign called 2026). The campaign list is kept per tenant and account for the session and read again in the background when over a minute old; it, the campaign bar's badge counts and the recent campaigns are cleared on sign-out (in place, not a page load). With nothing typed, search lists the pages, then the reader's recently opened campaigns (up to five, per tenant and account in this browser's storage, remembered as a campaign screen names its campaign, under the names the list has now and without deleted ones), then the Settings pages. "Notifications", "alerts" and "needs attention" find Home ahead of Email notifications: what needs a supervisor is on Home, and the emails are only the two sent as their events happen (no digest words). Escape closes only the topmost layer. Settings at `/settings/*`, grouped by who a setting affects: You (Profile and appearance, Email notifications), This account (Soft phone, Inbound routing, Call summaries), People and access (Team, Accounts); each page says who it applies to (people and access says the account, not the tenant, to a seat scoped to one account, which the server confines to it); `/settings` reopens the entry the viewer visited last (per user, in `localStorage`) while they may still open it, else Team for whoever has it, else the first entry; on a phone the sub-nav is one picker, with Find a setting and the campaign-settings pointer kept and the list shown again with what a search matched (amended 2026-11-12). A work page links to the Settings entry that shapes it, for a reader who passes that entry's gate (`components/common/SettingsEntryLink`): Missed calls, Inbound summary and the inbound queue's empty state to Inbound routing, Soft-phone calls to Soft phone. Every old `/app/<page>` URL redirects (replace) to `/settings/<page>`, keeping the rest of the path, the query and the hash; `/app` itself stays the home decision (`AppHomeRedirect`: `/agency` with the dialer on, Settings with it off, the dedicated agent's dialer-off notice). `AgentLanding` keeps a dedicated agent out of the shell on every page while the dialer is on (Settings, `/app` and, since 2026-11-12 on Manas's ruling, `/agency`): a page with an agent twin sends them to it with the query and the hash (`dialerTwinPath` in `utils/consoleNav.ts`: a report, the reports list, Missed calls, Customer history), any other to `/dialer`; a twin behind a flag that is off (the reports with `agency_reports_enabled`, Missed calls with `agency_inbound_enabled`) sends them to `/dialer` too, since the agent's frame has no way out of a refusal. PostHog's `product` stays `'agency'` on `/agency` and `'ai'` elsewhere. The navigation model is `apps/console/src/utils/consoleNav.ts`. Supersedes B17's "`/app` is its own platform zone" and its "Team & settings" exit; B17's branding rules stand | Supervisors, tenant admins and owners found two shells confusing: four agency sidebar entries (Notifications, Soft phone settings, Inbound settings, Accounts) dropped them into the `/app` shell, whose only way back was a link at the bottom of its sidebar, and the agency shell had no profile menu (sign-out, theme and ids lived only in `/app`'s top bar). The two-shell reasoning (agency is a few destinations used all day, the platform zone a list of sections) no longer held once the platform zone was only settings: settings are one more destination, and a hub grouped by who a change reaches answers "where is that setting" better than a second shell |
| B28a | Home (2026-11-11, part of the navigation redesign B28). `/agency/home`, gated at `agency.supervise` (`HOME_GATE` in `consoleNav.ts`: every read it makes floors there); the route sends anyone else to `/agency`. **Landing:** `AgencyHomeRedirect` sends whoever passes that gate to Home, so a supervisor lands there from `/agency`, after sign-in and after an account switch (a full page load at the new account's home); a dedicated agent (`agent`) lands on `/dialer`; a viewer or operator lands on the campaign list, inside the console (amended 2026-11-12: they used to land on `/dialer` with the agent persona, whose only way out opened Profile and appearance; `AgentHomePage` now offers them "Open the console", to the campaign list; `agencyLandingPath` in `utils/consoleNav.ts`). **Sidebar:** Home is the first entry, active only on its own URL, with a badge in the warning tone counting the dangers and warnings on Home's list: never a campaign that is only waiting, and never the missed calls, which the Inbound entry's own badge (accent tone) already counts, with screen-reader words ("4 need your attention"). **Needs your attention** lists conditions, not notifications (no read state; an item goes on the next refresh after it is fixed): each campaign's top stall in the health strip's words (one diagnosis, as the strip shows); abandonment on a running campaign from 80% of its ceiling, the abandonment meter's warning band (`abandonmentTone`), over the guardrail's 24 hours and only with 25 answered calls or more (IN5), not beside an abandonment pause; a campaign's overdue callbacks (`totals.callbacks_overdue` from the insights allocation, the read the Callbacks tab's badge uses); the account's open missed calls when the reader gets the Missed list (a warning on the list, left out of the Home badge). A campaign that is only waiting for its calling hours or a scheduled retry (the stalls Home reads as information) is not under "Needs your attention": it shows apart, muted, as "Waiting, nothing wrong". **Floor right now:** a row per running campaign, and per paused or stopping one with agents still on it, from `/stats` `agents_by_state` (a call being connected counts as on a call). **Today so far:** one grouped read (`group_by=campaign`, the viewer's local midnight to now) summed into dials, conversations, successes and talk time, with the success rate (successes over conversations, under the Insights rate rules) and a line on what counts as a success; no connect rate, which divides by placed first dials the grouped rows do not carry; no total when the server holds rows back; callbacks kept is left out (it needs a range read per campaign). It is read one at a time, on the minute and on a return to the tab only once the totals are a minute old, and a failed refresh keeps the totals only while they are still today's (after local midnight it shows none, never yesterday's). **Bound:** one request, `GET /proxy/agency/home` (`agency.campaigns.read`, see the Campaigns page below; then the account scope, then the dialer flag): the active campaigns, running first and newest first within a status, at most 25 (`AGENCY_HOME_CAMPAIGN_CAP`; the page says when there were more), each with the fields of `/stats` Home reads (the internal stats handler's payload) and, for a supervisor, what the campaign Overview's attention rules read (amendment (1) below); the server reuses a still campaign's stats (the campaigns home's `needsPolling` rule) for a minute while its floor holds (3), and the insights figures for a minute, all keyed by the campaign's status and `updated_at` (today's figures also by the day in the campaign's zone, so they turn at its midnight with the Health tab's Today). Home asks every 10 s and on return; a tick that falls due while a read is in flight is answered by that read (every read is complete), and a failed read, or a campaign the server could not read, keeps the last good numbers. Home and the badge read one board per tenant, account and role (`useHomeBoard`): one read at a time, on one timer at the fastest period a mounted reader asks for (Home's 10 s while it is open, the badge's minute elsewhere), and a reader that mounts, the timer or a return to the tab reads only while the tab is visible and the board is older than that period, so the badge never adds a request beside Home. **The Campaigns page's Active cards** (amended 2026-11-12, review of the redesign): they read the same board (one request every 10 s while any campaign is active, shared with the badge for a supervisor) in place of a `/stats` read per active campaign (19 running campaigns: 2 requests at first paint and 14 a minute, where it was 20 and 140). The board's route therefore floors at `agency.campaigns.read`, the list's and `/stats`' own, so a viewer or operator, who lands on that page, reads it (never a 403, so the cards never stop asking for them; an agent does not reach the page). The insights parts of the board (`staffing`, `allocation`, `today`, `settings`) are the insights reads', which floor at `agency.supervise`: below that floor the server builds the board without them, serves them null (withheld, not failed) and makes none of their reads, so a viewer's board costs no allocation or range read, and it caches a viewer's board apart from a supervisor's. A card shows the stall, calls in progress, agents on the floor, on a call and available, who was reached of the dials placed, what is waiting to dial, the 24-hour abandonment against the ceiling (Home's floor's rule), the campaign's line share (MC7) and the list-worked bar: the board's stats carry every `/stats` field a card reads (`AGENCY_HOME_STATS_FIELDS`: the attempts, the contact counts and `concurrency` beside Home's), all `agency.campaigns.read`, so a viewer's card is a supervisor's (Manas: a card must not lose information). A moving campaign's stats (running or stopping, live calls, anyone on shift: `needsPolling`, the server's `campaignMoving`) are read on every board, so its card's figures are never a kept read; a still one's are up to a minute old, which covers what changes it without moving it (a roster upload, the account's live calls on its line-share line), where the card used to read them only on return to the tab. The first 25 active campaigns get a card; past that the section links to the Active tab. A campaign the list still shows that a board read since no longer carries has left the active set (a paused, still campaign stopped in another tab, which nothing else would re-read the list for): the list is read again, once per such board read, so its card goes rather than loading for good. The page is the list, with no rail of sidebar entries (its Analytics, Do Not Call and Team shortcuts went); its one action is New campaign for whoever can create, or Take calls (the sidebar's gate) for a viewer or operator. **Amended 2026-11-12 (review fixes):** (1) *The Overview's list, not a subset.* Beside each campaign's stall, Home lists every row of the campaign Overview's "Needs your attention", from the same rule function (`attentionFrom` in `insights/overview.ts`) over facts the board serves from the Overview's own reads: overdue callbacks with who owns them, "No agents online" on a running campaign (so an unstaffed running campaign is on Home), due contacts held by away agents, abandonment against the ceiling in any status (not beside the guardrail's own pause), carrier failures and missing recordings counted over today in the campaign's zone (their rows open Health on `?range=today`, so the number is the page's), and calling hours left at the default. The board's per-campaign `callbacks_overdue` became `allocation`, `staffing`, `today` and `settings` (served null below `agency.supervise`, see the Campaigns page above). Information rows show under "For your information", not "Waiting, nothing wrong". (2) *One on-shift count.* `agents_live`, and the stall's `agents_on_shift` read from it, count the floor's rows that are not `offline`, the console's `agentsOnShift` rule. (3) *A still campaign's floor.* Its memo is also keyed by the floor's signature (open sessions, newest state change, newest join), so an agent joining a paused campaign shows on the next board. (4) *When Home cannot refresh.* Once its reads have failed for more than twice their period, Home says "Couldn't refresh. Last updated hh:mm" with Retry, never "Nothing needs you right now", and marks a campaign whose figures are the last read's ("Not refreshed"); the badge keeps its last count, muted and labelled not up to date (dropping it would read as an all-clear). A 403 stops the asking until Retry or a new scope or role (the role is part of the board's identity in `useHomeBoard`, so a viewer's board and a supervisor's are never one). (5) *Got it.* Each row has a per-viewer "Got it" that hides it and takes it off the badge until it gets worse (its count grows, or its kind or tone changes) or until the viewer's next local midnight, the day Today so far counts (Manas, 2026-11-12: the browser's day, where it was Asia/Kolkata's, which reset rows mid-shift for anyone outside IST), kept per signed-in user, tenant and account in this browser's storage (`utils/agency/homeAcks.ts`, `hooks/useHomeAcks.ts`; without storage it lasts for the page), and forgotten in the tab at sign-out. An automatic pause's kind carries its `paused_at`, so a campaign resumed and paused again the same day comes back; a count that cannot grow (missed calls past 99) is offered no "Got it". Put-aside rows stay behind "N acknowledged · Show"; with only those left Home says "Nothing new needs you right now". One overdue callback lighting the badge all day taught people to ignore it. (6) *Phones.* The top bar's menu button carries a dot, with "N things need you" for a screen reader, while the Home badge is above zero, since the sidebar (and its badge) is a closed drawer there | The supervisor's first question on opening the console is what needs them, and the answer was spread over every campaign's health strip, its Callbacks tab and the Missed list. Thresholds and words are the ones the console already uses, so Home never disagrees with the page it links to. Redesigned as Live floor on 2026-11-18 (LF1–LF10, B28b) |
| B28b | Live floor, server reads (2026-11-18; Home, B28a, becomes the Live floor in the console). **`GET /proxy/agency/floor?from=`** (`agency.supervise`, then the account scope, then the dialer flag, then `from`: an instant with its offset, the viewer's local midnight, at most 36 h back and 5 min ahead; 400 `invalid_floor_query`; `Cache-Control: no-store`): one row per person in the account. The rows are every open session in the account (a campaign session on any of its campaigns, or a soft-phone session, whose `campaign_id` is null; an agent switched to the soft phone is their campaign session on `break/soft_phone`, never a second row) and one row per agent staffed on an active campaign (`running`, `stopping`, `paused`) with no open session in the account (`state: offline`, `session_id` null, `left_at` the end of their last session here when that was at or after `from`, else null: "Not signed in today"). An agent live in another account is such a row here. One live session per agent (OS1) makes one row per person; should two ever be open, the newest joined (then the greatest session id) is kept. Each open row carries its state, `state_since`, liveness (`connected`, the per-campaign floor's `StationRegistry` read, null when unknown), `client_kind`, the break code and its label (a reserved code's own, else the campaign catalog's or the default catalog's), the inbound flags, the wrap-up window the session takes (`wrapup_window_seconds`: on an inbound wrap-up the window captured on its call, IQ52, else the inbound default; otherwise the campaign's; null for a timerless campaign, `wrapup_seconds = 0`, whose wrap-up has no countdown to be past, and without a campaign), and the call in progress: the live calls list's own read keyed by the agent's session (not its route, which refuses while listening is off), with the customer's NAME, never the number (the floor is shown to every `agency.supervise` holder with or without the listening flag), the campaign AHT and `listenable` / `not_listenable_reason` by the live list's rule, computed only for a reader with `agency.calls.listen` while listening is on for the account (off, or a flag store that cannot be read: every call unlistenable, no reason, the floor still served). **`calls_today`** is the agent's conversations dialled in `[from, now)` on every campaign of the account, counted exactly as the grouped stats count `connected` (a redialled conversation once), so it equals the grouped read by agent over the same window; soft-phone and inbound calls are not in it, as they are not there; it is the read's cost (the window's attempts) and is kept 30 s per account and `from`. A reader who varies `from` gets a fresh read each time (each instant is its own entry), so the cache only bounds a well-behaved console; the entries are capped at 1,000 per process, least recently read first out, and every read needs an authenticated `agency.supervise` holder. **Totals** are distinct people: `on_shift` the rows not `offline`; `assigned` the agents staffed on an active campaign together with everyone on shift, so `on_shift <= assigned`. Built as the live list is: an internal handler (`GET /agency/floor`, owner `none`) answers everything but names and listenability, which the public route adds. **`GET /proxy/agency/promises/summary?from=&to=`** (the same gate; two instants, from before to, at most 31 days; 400 `invalid_promises_query`): every promise to pay (PT1) recorded in the window in the account, by when the outcome carrying it was written (`dispositioned_at`, last-write-wins on a same-code replay), on all three call tables (dialler attempts, soft-phone calls, inbound calls), per campaign (the attempt's, the soft-phone call's tag, the inbound call's matched campaign; null for a call with none): the count, how many carry an amount, and their sum, exact (NUMERIC in SQL, a decimal string with two places), no currency (none is stored, PT5); and `promise_campaign_ids`, the active campaigns whose stored catalog has a `requires_promise` entry (none: the console shows Successes). Only campaign catalogs are read: the soft phone's and inbound routes' catalogs refuse `requires_promise` when saved, and an untagged soft-phone call or an unmatched inbound call resolves to one of those or the built-in catalog, so no promise is recorded outside a campaign's catalog. `0050_promise_summary_indexes.sql` indexes each table's promises by account and `dispositioned_at`. **Home's board** gains `status_changed` per campaign: for a paused one, `paused_at` (every pause writes it), the transition's actor (`last_transition_by_*`, cleared by an automatic pause) and `automatic` (`pause_reason` other than `supervisor`); for a stopping one the same actor and `stopping_at`, which `transitionStatus` stamps on the way into `stopping` (`0051_campaign_stopping_at.sql`; first-write-wins, so a second Stop, were one accepted, would not move it; kept on `stopped`), not the Stop's audit row, which the route writes after the transition without waiting for it, so the console's reload straight after Stop could read the board before it existed (null for a campaign that entered `stopping` before 0051: no time is shown and the audit row is not read). The name is the identity lookup's, else the one recorded with the transition. It is read with the board's per-campaign reads, so a transition (which moves `updated_at`) misses the board's memo at once (the reload straight after Stop serves the stop and its time), and it is not withheld below `agency.supervise`: the campaign list already serves `last_transition_by` to every reader. **Phase 3 (LF4, 2026-11-19):** each floor row also carries `help`, the open session's open Need help (`{ raised_at, call_id }`; null otherwise, and on every row while `agency_floor_assist_enabled` is off or unreadable), read by the internal handler from `idx_agency_agent_sessions_help_open` with the 15-minute expiry applied (`0052_floor_assist.sql`); optional in the wire type only so a client written before it compiles. | One read per poll for the whole floor, where the console would otherwise read `/stats` per campaign, and the per-person counts (`on_shift`, `assigned`) a sum of per-campaign `staffed` counts would double. The definitions are the existing reads' (the live list's call and listenability, the grouped stats' conversations, the per-campaign floor's liveness and inbound flags), so the floor never disagrees with the page it links to |

## 4. Rulings on open questions (Manas, 2026-10-09)

Each ruling carries a `Q<n> (Manas, 2026-10-09)` code comment and a mutation-checked test. Q3 was
not ruled on; it is in §5.

| # | Question | Ruling | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| Q1 | Verify the production Postgres TLS certificate? | Yes. When TLS is on (production) the certificate is verified (`rejectUnauthorized: true`). `DB_SSL_CA` takes the PEM **text** of a private CA (literal `\n` allowed; a path or other non-PEM value is a boot error); `DB_SSL_REJECT_UNAUTHORIZED=false` is the only opt-out (strict `true`/`false`). TLS parameters in `DATABASE_URL` (`ssl`, `sslmode`, `sslrootcert`, `sslcert`, `sslkey`, `sslnegotiation`, any case) are refused at config parse, because pg lets them replace these settings | **Decided — changed** |
| Q2 | How many action error codes? | 18. The union in `packages/contracts/src/errors.ts` has 18 members; an older count of 16 was stale. **2026-11-01:** 19, with `not_staffed` (the join's staffing check, `POST /proxy/agency/sessions`) | **Decided — keep** |
| Q4 | Platform audit `actor_type` has no `api_key` | When audit rows are imported from the previous platform at launch, rows with `actor_type = 'api_key'` become `system`, keeping the key name in `details`. Record only; nothing built now | **Decided — keep (launch)** |
| Q5 | Cache invalidation when Redis is down | Revocations retry the Redis DEL (3 attempts) and log at ERROR on final failure. A revocation route that is idempotent on retry answers 503 `cache_invalidation_failed` (the role-change routes, tenant and super-admin); one that is not keeps its 2xx and relies on the error log (both membership-removal routes: a retry 404s before the delete). Non-revocation invalidations keep swallowing. TTLs: membership 30 min, user 20 min, tenant/account records 5 min, local layer 5 s (off by default). No metric fits; none added. There is no tenant/account soft-delete route | **Decided — changed** |
| Q6 | Should bridge-leg and webhook tokens fail open? | Middle option: a missing token when Redis ANSWERED is refused; Redis absent or erroring still accepts (never hard-fail a live call); a wrong token is refused. Kept working explicitly: VoiceLink's late terminal posts (the recording URL) after teardown — the webhook token lives 2 h past the call end instead of being deleted; and a token whose `SET` failed at mint — remembered in-process and still accepted. No other leg relies on the fallback (provider and webhook tokens are stored before the dial; nothing verifies a browser token). The "SET failed at mint" memo is **process-local**, so it is sound only on a single replica: with more than one replica, a token minted (and failed) on one replica and verified on another is refused, so scaling out needs the memo shared or the excuse dropped. **Deployment invariant:** Redis must keep these keys for their TTL (persistence on; `noeviction`, or `volatile-*` with nothing else competing); a key evicted or lost to a non-persistent restart is a refusal that cuts the live call's carrier leg and status webhooks, and each such refusal logs a warning (`WebRTC WS token missing while Redis answered`) | **Decided — changed** |
| Q7 | `trustProxy` for IP-keyed rate limits | See Q9 | **Decided — changed** |
| Q8 | Can an agent act on another agent's session? | No; sealed. The public API layer sends the authenticated actor on every session route; `requireOwnedSession` requires `session.agent_user_id === request.user.id`, else the same 404 as a missing session (no existence leak, checked before the `left_at` 409). A supervisor (`agency.supervise` → `on_behalf`) may act on another agent's session ONLY on `force-available`, the one supervisory session flow; station-token, available, break, break/cancel and leave are the agent's own presence. `requireOwnedAttempt` needed nothing: every attempt route already runs `checkActor`. A station token for session X can only be minted by X's agent, so only X's agent can open X's station socket (the upgrade itself is token-only) | **Decided — changed** |
| Q9 | Client IP behind a load balancer | `TRUST_PROXY_HOPS` (`server.trustProxyHops`, `.int().min(1).default(1)`), never `true`. Passed to Fastify as a hop-count trust function (proxy-addr's `i < hops`), because Fastify 5.12 fails a bare numeric `trustProxy` closed. The same trust makes `request.host` / `request.protocol` proxy-asserted (`x-forwarded-host` / `x-forwarded-proto`); nothing reads them today. **Deployment invariant:** the server must be reachable ONLY through exactly `TRUST_PROXY_HOPS` proxies; bind it to loopback or a private interface, or firewall the port. If it is reachable directly, a client's own `X-Forwarded-For` entry becomes `request.ip` and the IP rate limits can be evaded; if the real chain has a different number of proxies, set the count to match. **Amended 2026-11-01:** per-user bucket. Carrier-grade NAT (Jio, Airtel) puts many agents behind one IP, so the IP is no longer what binds authenticated traffic. `sessionMiddleware` charges a bucket keyed on the VERIFIED user id (`u:<id>`, `RATE_LIMIT_USER_MAX`, default 300/min, platform config block), never on a header. The app-scope limiter, which runs before auth, gives a request carrying `Authorization: Bearer` its own coarse per-IP bucket (`ipa:<ip>`, `RATE_LIMIT_IP_AUTH_MAX`, default 3000/min); a request without one keeps `RATE_LIMIT_MAX` (200) under the plain `<ip>` key. Only the bearer's presence is read there, so a forged one buys the larger coarse bucket and is refused 401 by auth. Webhook, carrier-media and internal buckets and the probe exemptions are unchanged; super-admin routes (own JWT) stay on the IP buckets. Pending Manas's ratification | **Decided — changed** |

Other rulings of 2026-10-09 (not numbered questions):

| Topic | Ruling | Status |
|---|---|---|
| `VOICELINK_RECORDING_HOSTS` | Unset → `recording.app.voicelink.co.in`; set → exactly that list (set empty = every recording fetch and playback refused) | **Decided — changed** |
| Analysis retention | `AGENCY_TRANSCRIPT_RETENTION_DAYS` defaults to 30 days (falls back to `DIALER_TRANSCRIPT_RETENTION_DAYS`), so the purge runs out of the box; `AGENCY_RETENTION_DAYS` (row deletion) stays unset by default; the `RETENTION_MIN_DAYS` floor (30) holds | **Decided — changed** |
| Super-admin audit writes | Stay fire-and-forget, but a failed write is logged at ERROR with action, actor and target instead of being dropped silently | **Decided — changed** |
| B7, B12, B13, B15 | As recorded above | **Decided — keep** |
| B6 | Handled with the OpenTelemetry SDK. Service names `magick-agency` (production), `magick-agency-Staging`, `magick-agency-Dedicated`; agency's alerts page the same PagerDuty service as the rest of the Grafana stack. Everything agency-related (rules, dashboard, agency copies of the liveness, runtime and cardinality rules) lives in this repo's `grafana/`, with its own state | **Decided — changed** |
| Unnamed exporter | With `OTEL_ENABLED=true` but no `OTEL_SERVICE_NAME`, the SDK does not start and the server warns: the fallback name `magick-agency` is the production name alerts page on | **Decided** |
| OTel export path | OTLP push only (http/protobuf, e.g. Grafana Cloud's OTLP gateway); there is no Prometheus scrape endpoint. See `src/instrumentation.ts` | **Decided** |
| pg span parameters | `enhancedDatabaseReporting: true` stays on: pg spans carry query parameter values | **Superseded 2026-10-16** (below) |
| Credentials on spans | Redacted on spans as in logs: `?token=`, `sig=`, `*verify_token=`, signed-URL keys and media-stream path tokens, on server spans and (query values) on outgoing `http` and `fetch` spans | **Decided** |
| Log export | Logs reach OTLP only through the SDK's `instrumentation-pino` bridge (no `pino-opentelemetry-transport`), so no line ships twice | **Decided** |
| Audit partitions | Dropped after 85 days (`AUDIT_RETENTION_DAYS` default, `auditPartitions.retentionDays`) | **Decided — keep** |
| `findActiveSuccessor` | Tenant- and account-scoped (`packages/db/src/repositories/call-analysis-profile.repository.ts`: `dead.tenant_id = $2 AND dead.account_id = $3`, with the caller's scope from the route), so a profile lookup cannot cross tenants | **Decided — keep (verified)** |

Rulings of 2026-10-10:

| Topic | Ruling | Status |
|---|---|---|
| Firebase credential | No service account. The server only calls `verifyIdToken` without the revocation check, which needs the project id alone (`apps/server/src/auth/firebase.ts`). `FIREBASE_SERVICE_ACCOUNT_KEY` / `_PATH` are removed | **Decided — changed** |
| Caller ID | No app-level default: `VOICELINK_DEFAULT_CALLER_ID` is removed, and each campaign carries the `caller_ids` its tenant sets | **Decided — changed** |
| AI providers | Every AI request goes through one client factory, `apps/server/src/ai/`, with one implementation each for `openai_compatible` (OpenAI or any Chat Completions endpoint), `azure_openai` and `gemini`. `openai` is accepted as the older spelling. `OPENAI_API_KEY` is a fallback only when no `POST_CALL_ANALYSIS_BASE_URL` is set, so it never reaches another host | **Decided — changed** |
| Audio analysis | `POST_CALL_ANALYSIS_INPUT=audio` gives the analysis model the recording as well as the transcript (gemini only, refused at boot otherwise). Default `transcript` | **Decided — changed** |
| Recording at call time | An account with no settings row does not record: the bridge reads `allow_recording ?? false`, matching `DEFAULT_ALLOW_RECORDING`. The baseline migration's "NULL inherits true" column comment is superseded (the migration is applied, so it is not edited). The campaign create wizard now has the recording box | **Decided — changed** |
| S3 provisioning | `aws/terraform`: one bucket and one IAM user (Put/Get/List, no delete) per environment, one Terraform workspace per environment | **Decided** |
| Terraform state | `aws/terraform`'s and `grafana/terraform`'s state is in one S3 bucket (`magick-agency-terraform-state`, versioned, S3 lock file), each under its own key, made once per account by `aws/state`, so any machine with AWS access can apply. Was local to the machine that applied (Manas, 2026-11-13: "I want it to work in all computers as long as it has access to AWS") | **Decided — changed** |

Rulings of 2026-10-11:

| Topic | Ruling | Status |
|---|---|---|
| VM provisioning | `aws/terraform` also creates the server VM when `create_server = true` (off by default, so a bucket-only environment gets none): one EC2 instance, a security group (80/443 open, 22 only from `ssh_cidrs` or the EC2 Instance Connect range), an Elastic IP associated separately so it outlives the instance, and a Session Manager role with no S3 access. The instance ignores AMI and user-data changes, so it is only replaced deliberately (`-replace`) | **Decided** |
| VM image and size | Ubuntu 26.04 LTS, looked up from Canonical's public SSM parameter for the instance type's architecture. Default 2 vCPU / 4 GB (`t3.medium`), 20 GB gp3, 2 GB swap. The 4 GB heap limit in `docker/entrypoint.sh` is unchanged and equals that RAM (`status.md`, pre-deploy checklist) | **Decided** |
| Super-admin exposure | Publishing the super-admin UI through the host's TLS terminator on its own domain is allowed, open to everyone to begin with; nginx's :8081 bind stays loopback. Accepted costs: one shared login bucket, the terminator's address recorded for super-admin actions, the password as the only gate. An IP allow-list at the terminator is the next step (`DEPLOYMENT.md` 8.4) | **Decided** |
| Carriers (B18) | Plivo is supported alongside VoiceLink, on the **tenant's own Plivo account** (bring-your-own-carrier, BYOC). VoiceLink stays the service's own account, configured from env. **Amended 2026-11-25 (`carriers.md`, CR1–CR14, pending Manas):** a carrier declares which account modes it supports (the service's own account from env, tenants' own accounts, or both); VoiceLink now supports both, Plivo stays tenant accounts only. Amends S1 and S2: a tenant may dial on its own carrier account; agency still owns the voice engine. Carriers are added as provider modules under `apps/server/src/telephony/`, so a further carrier is a set of registered pieces (adapter, credential spec, carrier module, webhook and recording entries), not branches in the bridge | **Decided** |
| Carrier credentials: storage (B19) | Per-tenant credentials live in Postgres (`telephony_credentials`), never in env and never in Redis. The secret part is sealed with AES-256-GCM under a fresh data key per seal (one per secret version, in `telephony_credential_secrets`), which is wrapped by a master key from env (`TELEPHONY_CREDENTIAL_KEYS`, a versioned keyring; `TELEPHONY_CREDENTIAL_ACTIVE_KEY` names the one new seals use). AWS KMS may replace the env keyring later without a schema change (secret rows record the key id). The seal is bound to tenant, credential, provider and secret version, so a ciphertext copied to another row does not open | **Decided** |
| Carrier credentials: scope and owner (B20) | A credential belongs to one tenant and serves every account in it. Only super-admins create, rotate, verify, disable, enable or revoke one (rows are never deleted; a revoked credential's secrets are shredded once every call pinned to it is terminal and ended more than 2 h ago, the post-call webhook window of Q6), from the super-admin console; the secret is write-only (never returned, only a hint). Every change is audited without secret material. A verify checks the current secret only and records its verdict against that version; a verdict that no longer applies when written (rotated, revoked or status changed meanwhile) is refused, never reported as success, and not audited. A call is pinned to the credential it dialled on; a missing, disabled or unopenable credential fails the call and never falls back to the service's VoiceLink account, and neither does the teardown hangup of a pinned call | **Decided** |
| Carrier credentials: recordings (B21) | A BYOC carrier's recordings are fetched on demand with the tenant's credential (no copy to S3). The credential is attached only for the provider's exact recording hosts and dropped on any redirect. Revoking a credential therefore ends playback and analysis of that tenant's earlier recordings on it. **Amended 2026-11-18 (Manas, after staging refused `aps1.media.plivo.com`):** "exact recording hosts" now reads "recording media hosts": a provider's list may take a host and its subdomains when every name under it serves only recordings (Plivo: `media.plivo.com` and its regional subdomains, PL6), never an API host or an apex whose subdomains include one. The list stays in code, per provider, never from tenant or call data, and the credential still goes with the first request only | **Decided** |
| Carrier credentials: concurrency (B22) | A BYOC call is admitted exactly like a VoiceLink call: the global, account and per-account provider scopes all apply. It is not exempt from the global cap because the bridge, not the carrier, is the shared resource | **Decided** |

Rulings of 2026-10-16 (Manas; trace quota exceeded on 2026-10-15):

| Topic | Ruling | Status |
|---|---|---|
| pg span parameters | `enhancedDatabaseReporting: false`: pg spans carry query text, never parameter values (they put phone numbers, names and other customer data in Tempo). `ignoreConnectSpans: true` too. That option also skips the instrumentation's binding of `pool.connect` callbacks to the caller's context, so a query that waited for a connection joined the releaser's trace. `packages/db/src/connection.ts` restores the binding (`bindPoolConnectContext`). See `apps/server/src/utils/otel-sdk-config.ts`, "Traces" | **Decided — changed** |
| Probe and analytics spans | `/healthz`, `/readyz` (and the readiness check's pg/Redis calls) and outgoing PostHog requests are not traced | **Decided** |
| Trace sampling | Head sampling by env only: `OTEL_TRACES_SAMPLER=parentbased_traceidratio`, `OTEL_TRACES_SAMPLER_ARG` 0.5 production and dedicated, 0.1 staging. Unset keeps every trace | **Decided** |

Rulings of 2026-10-16/17 (tidy-up). Manas asked on 2026-10-16 for the whole tidy-up plan to be
implemented. The first table is what the approved plan decided; the second is what the
implementation chose where the plan left a choice, built and awaiting ratification.

| Topic | Ruling | Status |
|---|---|---|
| Shared UI package (B23) | `packages/web-ui`, consumed as TypeScript source; see B23 | **Decided** |
| Migration 0003 | `0003_agency_attempt_indexes.sql` (0002 is the telephony credentials): `idx_agency_attempts_live` redefined with the predicate `state <> 'ended'`; new `idx_agency_attempts_answered` (partial, `answered_at IS NOT NULL`) and `idx_agency_contacts_connected (id) WHERE state = 'connected'`; `idx_agency_attempts_reporting` dropped (a strict prefix of the keyset index). Plain `CREATE INDEX`, which blocks writes to those tables while it builds; acceptable because migrations run at container start, before the server. `EXPLAIN` evidence from seeded data is in `packages/db/BASELINE.md` | **Decided** |
| One transaction shape | `withTransaction` (`packages/db/src/utils/transaction.ts`) is the one BEGIN / COMMIT / ROLLBACK / release shape; with `client` it joins the caller's transaction (B8). A client whose ROLLBACK failed is destroyed, not returned to the pool, at every site on the helper (and in the membership repository's own transactions); error mapping runs after the release. Other raw `BEGIN` sites remain (status follow-ups) | **Decided — changed** |
| Agency repository layout (B12) | `agency.repository.ts` is a barrel over `apps/server/src/db/repositories/agency/`; callers and `vi.mock` use the barrel path | **Decided** |
| Route families | A large route plugin is a family: `<family>.routes.ts` plus `<family>/`, registered on the same Fastify instance; source-text guards read the whole family. `forwardCore` / `forwardCoreVia` live in `core-dispatch.ts` (`vi.mock` cannot intercept calls inside one module, so tests use `coreDispatchDouble`). `parseOr400` keeps the three 400 dialects distinct (`agency`, `platform`, and the internal instance's `Validation failed`) | **Decided** |
| Internal export fast path | `callCore`'s typed `provenCampaignId` sets the internal `x-mgkvc-proven-campaign` header, `<tenant>:<account>:<campaign>`; the header is stripped from every forwarded header set, and the internal spine handler honours it only on an exact tenant, account and campaign match. Exactly one caller (the public spine export's page drain, `api/routes/proxy-agency-campaigns/spine.ts`), pinned by `core-dispatch.test.ts` | **Decided** |
| Campaign summaries | New internal route `GET /agency-campaigns/summaries` (internal instance only; at most 100 ids; gated; tenant- and account-scoped). The staffing `my-*` routes label assignments with it, in reads of at most 100 ids | **Decided** |
| Console wire types | `packages/contracts/src/api/agency/*` re-export or derive from the core contract. `AGENCY_STATION_CLOSE` is one `as const` (4401 / 4404 / 4408 / 4409); `AGENCY_STATION_ERROR_CODES` removed (no producer); `api/platform/audit.ts` deleted (no read route); `zod` removed from the contracts package. The `AgencyActionErrorCode` union is declared in `errors.ts`, so Q2's wording is accurate | **Decided — changed** |
| Campaign config defaults | The contracts hold `AGENCY_DEFAULT_DISPOSITION_CATALOG`, `AGENCY_DEFAULT_DISPOSITION_RETRY`, `AGENCY_OUR_FAULT_REDIAL_BOUND`, `AGENCY_DEFAULT_OUR_FAULT_REDIAL_DELAY_MINUTES`, `AGENCY_OUR_FAULT_RETRY_DEFAULT` and `AGENCY_UNANSWERED_RETRY_DEFAULTS` (the server's default rows for a call nobody picked up, which New campaign says as the default), read by the server and the console; and the rate floors `AGENCY_CAMPAIGN_MIN_RATE_DENOMINATOR` (25, the console's campaign rates) beside `AGENCY_ROSTER_MIN_RATE_DENOMINATOR` (20, the server's `rates_reportable`) | **Decided** |

| Topic | Default used | Status |
|---|---|---|
| Pacing shutdown | Closes §5's "should `stop()` await an in-flight pacing tick?". `stop()` waits for in-flight ticks up to `TICK_DRAIN_TIMEOUT_MS` (5 s; then logs and carries on) before draining completion notices. `mayDial` is re-checked after `attempts.create`, and `executeDial` asks `stillAuthorised` once the dial is prepared, before the agent's lease is extended, the attempt is stamped `dialing` and it is registered live; a dial abandoned there ends its attempt `orphaned` with `dialed_at` NULL and no `agency_attempt_hold_seconds` observation (the agent is released `reservation_expired`). A stop inside the two writes after that check (lease CAS, `dialing` stamp) does not call the dial off. The 45 s `stop_grace_period` stays | **default, pending Manas** |
| Dialer settle on a database failure | If the `answered` row write fails, the abandon branch still runs and the answered (denominator) counter still increments. If the `ended` row write fails (or an abandoned dial's), the agent is still told and released, with `requires_disposition: false` and no wrap-up, and nothing writes the contact; the dialer hands the attempt id to the reaper, whose next sweep ends the row `orphaned` and requeues the contact on the our-fault ledger whoever holds the agent's station (the station-owner arm is skipped for these). `WrapupManager.enter` writes the attempt with `only_from: ['ended']`, so it never ends a row. If the contact write fails after the row was ended, a contact left `in_flight` is requeued on the our-fault ledger by the reaper's stranded-contact sweep once it has had no live attempt and no write for 5 min (migration `0004_agency_contacts_in_flight_index.sql` indexes `in_flight` contacts for it), and not dialable before `dialed_at` + 15 min when any of its dials (a campaign attempt or a soft-phone call) was sent, never answered and not ended by a carrier answer (`no_answer`, `busy`, `invalid`): the same statement reads them, so an outcome-unknown hold whose own write failed is not cut to 5 min; one parked `connected` at the bridge keeps its wrap-up, and the disposition route or the lapsed-wrap-up sweep releases it. A throwing `WrapupManager.enter` leaves no entry or timer and does not release twice | **default, pending Manas** |
| Unstored WS token memo (Q6) | Capped at 10 000 entries (`UNSTORED_WS_TOKEN_CAP`), oldest evicted (one per insert); expired entries swept at most every 10 s (`UNSTORED_WS_TOKEN_SWEEP_MS`); a webhook that would have used an evicted entry is refused | **default, pending Manas** |
| `dialedAt` | The in-process `dialedAt` is the same instant as the `dialed_at` column (one timestamp) | **default, pending Manas** |
| Ingest totals | Each chunk locks its campaign row (`FOR NO KEY UPDATE`) first, so `contacts_total` and the reported total are exact under concurrent chunks; a retry child's `contacts_total` is incremented by the seeded count, not recounted | **default, pending Manas** |
| Occupancy carried-in scan | Bounded by `MIN(joined_at)` of the window's sessions (assumes the database clock does not step backwards) | **default, pending Manas** |
| Batch claims | `claimDialable`, `failStaleActive`, `claimRunnable`, `recoverStale` and `promoteRecordingReady` pick their rows in a `MATERIALIZED` CTE, so each claims at most `limit`. Fixes a pre-existing bug: a nested-loop semi-join plan re-ran the locking subquery and claimed the whole eligible set (dials with no agent, `agency:agent:undefined:state` leases) | **default, pending Manas** |
| Console display locale and rounding | One locale, `en-IN` (`DISPLAY_LOCALE`), for dates and numbers on the analytics surfaces (lakh grouping "1,00,000", "19 Aug", "09:05 am"). Live timers round down; aggregate durations (AHT, averages, percentiles) round, so the roster cell and band agree (59.6 s reads "1:00" / "1m"). The surfaces still on the reader's locale are in `status.md` follow-ups | **default, pending Manas** |
| Rate floors | Campaign 25 and agent 20 keep their values, both now in the contracts; whether they should match stays open (§5). `rowConnectRate` (by-campaign agent rows, console and mobile) prints the served per-campaign rate, withheld in the roster's wording ("Not enough calls") where the row's served `rates_reportable` (20 first dials) is false | **default, pending Manas** |
| `talk_seconds` analytics | `agency_hangup_requested` and `agency_attempt_released` measure `talk_seconds` on the offset-corrected clock | **default, pending Manas** |
| Super-admin error messages | Built in the console's order (details / issues before `message`); a masked login error shows its request id | **default, pending Manas** |
| Super-admin lists | The phone numbers page filters client-side over the full inventory (the Deleted filter fetches `status=deleted`); the accounts list reports every account `unavailable` if its single allocation read fails | **default, pending Manas** |
| CSV download filenames | Spaces and non-ASCII kept (`filename*`), capped at 120 code points; `..` still collapsed | **default, pending Manas** |
| Analytics emitters | The `trackX` names stay as one-line aliases over a typed `track<K>()` and `EventCatalog`; `captureEvent` accepts only catalogued names | **default, pending Manas** |
| Super-admin role enum | The membership role enum follows `ROLES` order (only the order in zod's error message changes) | **default, pending Manas** |

Roster upload, choosing the duplicate column (2026-11-02; ClickUp "Roster upload: let the operator choose the column used to find duplicates"). The ticket's open questions, built with its own recommendations:

| Topic | Default used | Status |
|---|---|---|
| Duplicate key rules | One column (no combinations). Values compared trimmed and case-insensitive (`LN-001` = `ln-001`). A blank value is never a duplicate: the row is kept, unchecked. Only an accepted row claims its key, so a first row rejected for a bad number does not cost a later row with the same key. Naming the phone column is the phone dedupe, on the normalised number | **superseded 2026-11-10** by "Combined duplicate key" below; the value rules stand |
| Combined duplicate key (2026-11-10, Manas: "up to three primary keys … so that no false removals are happening") | Up to three columns (`AGENCY_MAX_DEDUPE_COLUMNS`; `dedupe_columns`, `0031_ingest_dedupe_columns.sql`). A row is a duplicate only when EVERY key matches an earlier accepted row, so each column added can only drop fewer rows. A row with ANY key blank is kept, unchecked (the conservative reading: a missing part is not evidence of a repeat). The phone column may be one of the keys and is then compared as the normalised number, rejected `duplicate_key`; the phone alone stays `duplicate_phone`. One column is the rule above, unchanged. The default key is the phone number alone; nothing is combined unless the operator adds a column, and "Combine with another column" adds a column, not the phone | **decided 2026-11-11, Manas** ("apply current defaults"; "nothing, start with just phone number field") |
| DNC row claims its key (found 2026-11-10; true of one column since 2026-11-02) | Kept as built. The CSV pass claims a row's key before the DNC check, which runs per batch in the service's `onBatch`. So a row on a DNC number holds its key, and a later row with the same key on a callable number is rejected `duplicate_key`: that loan is never called. The phone dedupe is not affected (the later row is the same number, suppressed anyway). Pinned as the ruled behaviour in `agency-ingest.service.test.ts`. Changing it would move the DNC check ahead of the key claim (a batched pre-check the CSV pass awaits, or claims that wait for the batch's DNC answer), which touches the fail-closed DNC path | **decided 2026-11-11, Manas** ("apply current defaults"); **superseded 2026-11-15 by IM4** (pending Manas: a Do Not Call row no longer claims its key) |
| Rows differing only in an ignored column | Unchanged and now stated in the console: the roster refuses an exact copy of an earlier row at insert by `row_fingerprint` (number, kept columns, timezone), so two rows that differ only in an ignored column become one contact even with "Don't remove duplicates", or when a key part is blank. Counted in `core_rejected_duplicate_rows`. Map the column as Detail to keep both | **decided 2026-11-11, Manas** ("apply current defaults") |
| Combined key across a rollback (2026-11-11, PR review) | The server still writes 0007's `dedupe_column` for a one-column key (NULL for a combination or no key), so a server rolled back past `0031_ingest_dedupe_columns.sql` keeps that key. The 0031 Down fails every pending or running job whose key combines columns (`interrupted`, "Upload the file again") rather than run it on one column, which would drop more rows than the operator chose; a finished combination keeps its first column for display. That status change moves `updated_at`; the Down's copy does not. A job the Down fails gets no `roster_upload_failed` activity row (a migration cannot write one) | **default, pending Manas** |
| Ignored key column | Refused, for any key column: the console blocks the mapping and `POST /ingest/jobs` answers 400. An ignored column is not in the contact's `context`, so not in `row_fingerprint`, and two loans differing only there would collapse into one contact at the roster insert | **default, pending Manas** |
| One live call per number | `claimDialable` skips a contact whose number (digits) is `in_flight` or `connected` on another contact of the same campaign, and claims at most one contact per number per tick. `connected` counts as live because a bridged call with a disposition catalog parks there; it also covers wrap-up, so on existing rosters a second household contact on one number now waits for the first one's write-up | **default, pending Manas** |
| Soft phone and the guard | Unchanged. The soft phone's pre-dial rule still refuses only an `in_flight` match (SP rulings, `softphone.md` §5), so a tagged manual call to a number whose contact is `connected` goes ahead; and a manual claim racing a pacing claim on two rows of one number can slip through in a window of milliseconds (the claim's busy check is a read, not a lock). See §5. Since 2026-11-05 a tagged dial also refuses the number `in_flight` on any campaign of the account (MC4) | **open** |

Rulings of 2026-10-30 (Manas):

| Topic | Ruling | Status |
|---|---|---|
| Invite claims for admin seats (B25) | Every role gets a single-use invite token by email, and the token binds whatever Firebase identity presents it, with a different address confirmed on the join page and audited (`email_matched: false`). From `account_admin` up (`account_admin`, `tenant_admin`, `tenant_owner`; `inviteRequiresMatchingAddress` in `packages/contracts/src/rbac.ts`) that is refused: `POST /invites/:token/claim` answers 409 `address_required` (with the invited address, which `GET /invites/:token` already shows the token holder) unless the signed-in email equals the invited address, trimmed and case-folded. A missing email is a mismatch. `email_verified` is not required: a password account made on the join page at the locked invited address is unverified by construction, and holding the mailed token is the inbox proof. The rule reads the membership's current role, not the role the invitation was issued for. The refusal binds and spends nothing (the same link still works for the invited address), logs a warn with tenant, invitation and role (no addresses), and counts `invite_claims_total{result="address_required"}`, alerted in `agy-invite-claim-identity-conflicts` at 3 per hour; it writes no audit row, like every other claim refusal. `GET /invites/:token` reports `requires_matching_address`, and the console's join pages show "this invitation must be accepted with <address>" and sign the other account out instead of offering to continue with it. Agents, viewers and operators keep the flexible claim | Before, a forwarded or leaked invite mail was a bearer credential for an admin or owner seat: anyone signed into any Google account could claim it after one confirmation. For agents the flexibility is the point (a personal Google account must still get in); for a seat that can invite people, change roles and supervise calls it is not worth the risk | **Decided — changed** |

Rulings of 2026-10-30 (soft phone, [`softphone.md`](softphone.md)):

| # | Topic | Ruling | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| SP1 | DNC on soft-phone dials | Checked on every dial (tenant, account, tagged campaign). A hit refuses `dnc_listed`; the agent may call anyway with an override note (10–500 characters), stored on the call with the DNC entry id and audited as `agency_manual_call.dnc_overridden`. A lookup failure refuses `dnc_unavailable` and cannot be overridden | **Decided** |
| SP2 | Calling hours | A call logged to a campaign whose window is closed (contact timezone, else the campaign default) refuses `outside_calling_hours`; override with a note, audited as `agency_manual_call.hours_overridden`. An untagged call has no window | **Decided** |
| SP3 | VoiceLink cannot cancel a ringing call | Accepted. Hang-up while ringing is hold-to-confirm and the console warns that the phone may keep ringing | **Decided — accept** |
| SP4 | Who gets the soft phone | Every agent in an account where `agency_softphone_enabled` is on (`agency.softphone.dial` at the agent floor); settings need `agency.softphone.manage` (account admin) | **Decided** |
| SP5 | Metering | Manual calls are counted in usage counts at the agency rate (`agency_calls.origin = 'manual'` is in the agency scope) | **Decided** |
| SP6 | Roster contact | A tagged call to a number on that campaign's roster claims the contact and its outcome settles it, forward only (a terminal contact is never reopened; suppress always applies) | **Decided** |
| SP7 | Switch from a running campaign | An agent on a campaign can switch to the soft phone (a `soft_phone` break, queued while busy) and return; both are audited (`agency_session.softphone_started` / `softphone_ended`) and shown in the campaign activity trail *Addendum 2026-11-20 (agent station redesign, AS1):* the station's rail is gone; the soft phone is reached from the station's ⋮ More ("Dial a number myself") and has its own screen in the station's frame (see [`agent-station.md`](agent-station.md)). | **Decided** |

Rulings of 2026-11-01 (agent mobile app, [`mobile.md`](mobile.md)):

| # | Topic | Ruling | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| M1 | A phone silent during a call | A mobile station silent past its 10 s grace on a call the agent can see is dropped by the sweep (`terminate`, not `close`, which waits 30 s on a dead transport), and the bridge's 8 s deferred hangup decides: the phone back in time resumes the call, otherwise it ends. Before this, no station was closed mid-call and the customer waited on silence until TCP gave up. A pre-bind dial keeps its own grace; the web console is unchanged. A silent phone NOT on a call is terminated too: a `close` would hold the agent dialable for those 30 s, and every pacing tick would write an orphaned attempt against it. **Amended 2026-11-01:** it covers a live soft-phone call too (a phone only; the web console is still never closed), where the terminate meets the bridge's rule for a manual leg: an immediate hangup before the answer, the 8 s deferred hangup after it | **Decided** |
| M2 | The soft phone on the app | The app has the agent's soft phone ([`softphone.md`](softphone.md) §7, "The agent app"): the Phone tab, the station in place (SP7), and the agent's own soft-phone calls in My calls. The supervisor's lists, Settings → Soft phone, recordings and DTMF are not on the app. The station core hosts either session kind on its one socket, audio engine and on-duty service, since the server allows one live session per agent per tenant (across every tenant since OS1). Opening is an explicit "Open the soft phone" tap that asks for the microphone and notifications first; an existing session, or a campaign on the soft phone with an unsettled call, is resumed without one. The on-duty service runs for the soft-phone session's whole life ("Soft phone"). A session in a sibling account is named, with the account switch offered; it is never switched in place | **Decided** |
| M3 | When the Phone tab shows | When the soft-phone bootstrap answers 200 and `agency_softphone_enabled` is on, or when there is a session or call to finish (finish mode), or when the phone hosts a soft-phone session or a campaign on the soft phone. A 403 (no `agency.softphone.dial`) hides it, so the app keeps no role matrix of its own. My calls' "Soft-phone calls" shows where the tab shows, or when a one-row read finds any of the agent's soft-phone calls | **Decided** |
| M4 | The tab rule with the soft phone | A live soft-phone call takes the screen and hides the bar, like a campaign call. An ended call owing its outcome holds the agent on its screen (the Phone tab, or the station for a campaign on the soft phone) with the bar shown and every other tab disabled with the reason; a sign-out waits for it. An idle soft-phone session holds nothing, gets no `device_away` (it is never `available`), and does not keep the screen awake: on a soft-phone session the wake lock is on only while a call is live or owes its outcome | **Decided** |
| M5 | Ringback and hang-up on the app | The ringback is made natively in `agent_audio` (`setRingback`), to the console's tone and cadence, mixed into the playout frames after the jitter buffer: a Dart timer pushing frames through the jitter buffer would glitch and is fragile in the background. While ringing, Mute is "Mute ring" (the ringback only); the microphone's mute is separate. In the app hang-up is hold-to-confirm, with the SP3 warning while ringing; CallKit and the notification's Hang up act at once | **Decided** |
