| M6 | Resuming a soft phone on the app | The app resumes on its own only the session this phone hosts (remembered per workspace, forgotten when the session ends or another device takes it over with 4409). A session open on another device, the console mid-call or another phone, is never taken silently: the Phone screen says so and offers "Use it on this phone", because taking it moves the station socket and any live call's audio to the phone. A resume put off while the app was in the background asks the server again on return | **Decided** |

Rulings of 2026-11-02 (scaling for 500 simultaneous calls; [`status.md`](status.md), "Scaling for
500 simultaneous calls"). Manas approved the plan; the values below are the defaults chosen in
building it:

| Topic | Default used | Status |
|---|---|---|
| Console call audio | `media=alaw8` (binary A-law 8 kHz) by default; `pcm16` per tenant with `agency_console_alaw8` off (the only default-on flag: it is a way back, not a capability), and automatically after a 1008 `invalid_media` or JSON media on an `alaw8` socket. The console's A-law code is console-local (`apps/console/src/utils/alaw8/g711.js`), pinned to the mobile app's checksums | **default, pending Manas** |
| WebSocket frame bound | `maxPayload` 64 KiB on every WebSocket route (`WS_MAX_PAYLOAD_BYTES`, never below the largest legitimate frame). An oversized carrier frame now closes the call's stream with 1009, where the bridge used to drop it | **default, pending Manas** |
| Send back-pressure | Media dropped past ~3 s of backlog (128 KiB `pcm16`, 24 KiB `alaw8`, 40 KiB to the carrier); control frames never dropped; a station past 1 MiB is closed with 4408 `station_send_backlog` (reused: both clients re-mint and reconnect on it) and terminated 5 s later if still open; the carrier stream has no hard cap, since closing it hangs up | **default, pending Manas** |
| VoiceLink timeouts | Login 10 s, `add_lead` 15 s per request. An outcome-unknown `add_lead` (timeout, or a 200 with a non-JSON body) fails the dial and releases the agent and slots at once, is never retried, and defers the contact's redial by 15 min (`DIAL_OUTCOME_UNKNOWN_REDIAL_DELAY_MS`), charging no attempt to it: the dial's own failure path settles it, once, and the dialer's `ended` arm leaves the bridge's outcome-unknown end to it. The hold holds whichever arm settles the attempt and in either order: an end that races the dial (the agent hangs up, or the station goes, during a slow `add_lead`) settles the row and releases the agent at once, and its contact waits, claimed, for the dial's answer. **The hold is counted** (default, pending Manas): the same statement charges `our_fault_attempts` (never `attempt_count`), so the shared our-fault bound (`AGENCY_OUR_FAULT_REDIAL_BOUND`, 3, with a campaign's stricter `canceled` / `agent_disconnected` cap when a teardown raced the dial) retires the contact `exhausted` instead of redialling it every 15 min for ever (`agency_our_fault_retirement_total{outcome="dial_outcome_unknown"}`). The soft phone applies the same hold and charge to a claimed contact (`manualNoOutcomeSettlement`; the call keeps `dialed_at`, so it counts as a dial, SP5); a late carrier media connect for it is refused, which hangs that call up. The reaper applies the same rule to an attempt it ends that was dialled and never answered or bridged (a process killed mid-dial: the startup reap, a dead media worker's slot reap, the leak sweep): its contact is requeued no earlier than 15 min after `dialed_at`, and so is the claimed contact of a soft-phone call the orphan sweeps end in the same shape (`dialOutcomeUnknownNotBefore`, one function for both). Not to an attempt whose end the dialer saw but could not write (`reapUnrecordedEnds`): its contact is dialable at once, unless the dialer reported that dial outcome-unknown (the bridge's `TELEPHONY_INIT_OUTCOME_UNKNOWN`, or the dial's own outcome-unknown failure). Every reap requeues a contact only while it is still the reaped attempt's: one taken again since (a newer attempt, a soft-phone claim, or already back on the roster) is left alone | **default, pending Manas** |
| Dial failed before the carrier | A dial the bridge fails before the carrier is asked (no `ended`: the call row INSERT, the carrier tokens, the carrier request's inputs such as the webhook base or the provider's adapter) ends the attempt `failed`, charges neither ledger, and puts the contact back no earlier than 1 min on (`PRE_CARRIER_FAILURE_REDIAL_DELAY_MS`, `agency-dialer.ts`). Before, it was dialable at once, so a failure that repeats redialled the same contact on every pacing tick (16 to 17 dials per contact in about 4 s on a three-contact list with four agents, `voicelink-bridge-setup-failure.test.ts`). A delay and not an our-fault charge: nobody was called, so the repeat-dial ceiling that ledger enforces has nothing to protect, and its bound (3) would let a systemic failure (every INSERT failing, a config error) retire the whole roster within seconds, permanently; the delay caps each contact at one dial a minute and loses nothing when the fault clears. Such a throw in the carrier request's inputs is no carrier refusal (`BRIDGE_SETUP_FAILED`, not `TELEPHONY_INIT_FAILED`), so it never spends the contact's `failed` allowance; nor is a dial the carrier adapter refuses before sending anything (no webhook base for the carrier, a callback URL off it, a malformed number or caller ID, no max duration: `CarrierDialNotSentError`, rejected `carrier_dial_not_sent`, row `CARRIER_DIAL_NOT_SENT` or `CARRIER_DIAL_INVALID_NUMBER`), which takes the same path, except that a contact number the carrier will never dial (`CARRIER_DIAL_INVALID_NUMBER`: it passed the pre-dial E.164 gate but not the carrier's own check) is suppressed `invalid`, uncharged, as the pre-dial gate's `phone_invalid` is, rather than held a minute on every pass; the soft phone answers it `503 dial_failed`. A dial called off before the bridge (the agent's socket or lease went, pacing stopped) still returns its contact at once, and so does an admission refusal (a concurrency slot not available): back-pressure, not a failure, already bounded by pacing's account cap; held a minute, it stalled a floor whose capacity came back within seconds (`concurrency-cap.test.ts`) | **default, pending Manas** |
| Redis client bounds | 2 s command timeout, 1 retry per request, 2 s connect timeout. A timeout is a rejection, so Q6 tokens fail open on it; the agent lease renewer stops only on `lost` | **default, pending Manas** |
| Ceilings | `MAX_CONCURRENT_CALLS` 1000 (a slot is held from admission until the leg is finalised: ~860 at 500 agents); `DB_POOL_MAX` 30, per process (the server process, or the primary with workers; worker pools in the D-rows below) | **default, pending Manas** |
| Rate limits (amends Q9's numbers) | `RATE_LIMIT_MAX` 2000, `RATE_LIMIT_IP_AUTH_MAX` 20 000, `RATE_LIMIT_USER_MAX` 600, `RATE_LIMIT_WEBHOOK_MAX` 5000, `RATE_LIMIT_CARRIER_MEDIA_MAX` 2000, `RATE_LIMIT_STATION_MAX` 3600, per minute, sized for 500 agents and 50 supervisors behind one IP (arithmetic in `api/middleware/rate-limit-defaults.ts`). The public invite routes keep 20/min per IP | **default, pending Manas** |
| Analysis worker | A slot pool, `DIALER_ANALYSIS_CONCURRENCY` 8 (1–256), refilled as each job finishes; `DIALER_ANALYSIS_MEMORY_BUDGET_BYTES` 512 MiB (at least 2 × `DIALER_MAX_RECORDING_BYTES`), reserved before a claim; a vendor rate-limit breaker (30 s doubling to 15 min, ±20 % jitter, one probe, closed only by a completed job). Rate-limited jobs still never burn an attempt | **default, pending Manas** |
| Pacing skips and caches | A campaign whose last full tick could reserve nothing is skipped until an invalidation event or the 1 s safety tick (`PACING_SAFETY_TICK_MS`); the account limit and live-call count are read once per account per round and shared through a headroom budget (since 2026-11-05 split between the account's running campaigns instead: MC1–MC2) (the guard's Redis count is not used); the campaign row and account limit are cached in pacing with a generation guard, event invalidation and a 2 s TTL. Events travel on `PacingInvalidationBus` (JSON, in-process; an IPC bus replaces it for worker processes, never Redis pub/sub, per Manas 2026-11-02) Since CR15 (`carriers.md`) the tick also caches an account's carrier shares and a carrier account's own cap (the same 2 s generation TTL), dropped by `account_limit_changed` and the new `credential_limit_changed`; a campaign held by a full carrier account is woken by `account_capacity_freed` from any account of its tenant | **default, pending Manas** |


Rulings of 2026-11-02 (multi-process, [`scaling.md`](scaling.md)). Manas asked for the whole
multi-process build (phase B); these are the design's recommendations, built as the defaults and
ratified by Manas on 2026-11-02 (D5 and D12 as amended, D9 as proposed):

| # | Topic | Default used | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| D1 | Topology | Media workers are dialer-runtime shards: each owns its agents' station sockets, their in-process runtime state (live attempts, wrap-up, breaks, the soft phone's live call) and both legs of their calls. The primary keeps HTTP, the public API layer, pacing, the reaper and every leader-only sweep, and sends dials to the owner over IPC. Analysis runs in its own processes | **Decided** (Manas, 2026-11-02) |
| D2 | Session ownership | Fixed by `fnv1a(sessionId) mod MEDIA_WORKERS` (`cluster/roles.ts`): the station socket, every session-owned internal handler and every dial for that agent go to the same worker, and a reconnect lands where its live call is. A worker crash costs ~1/N of the floor their live calls; they reconnect in `break` | **Decided** (Manas, 2026-11-02) |
| D3 | Carrier routing | The worker's slot rides in the carrier's media and webhook URLs as `&w=<slot>` (`core/bridge/carrier-urls.ts`), fixed before `add_lead`; routing only, the token is still verified by the owner. No `w`, or the slot down: the primary handles the post with a session-less bridge (the late-terminal path) | **Decided** (Manas, 2026-11-02) |
| D4 | Crash policy | A child that exits is re-forked with backoff (1 s doubling to 30 s). A media slot that exits more than 5 times in 5 minutes is a crash loop: the primary exits 1 and the container restarts. Analysis children are re-forked without a crash-loop exit | **Decided** (Manas, 2026-11-02) |
| D5 | Readiness and slow workers | `/readyz` is 200 while the primary is not draining and at least one media worker answers its 1 s health ping and is not draining; 503 when none does or the primary is draining. The body names each worker's state (`ok`, `draining`, `not_answering`). The trade-off: the ALB health check and the uptime check point at `/readyz`, and on one host a 503 takes all traffic away, which is worse than losing the 1/N of the floor a slow or restarting worker serves (a restart can sit in backoff for up to 30 s); the cost is that a dead worker does not fail the probe, so it is seen through `agy-cluster-child-crash`, the *Processes* panels and the body instead. (The first default, every worker required, made one slow worker flap the whole host.) A slow worker is measured and alerted on, not shed | **Decided** (Manas, 2026-11-02) |
| D6 | Guards with Redis down | In cluster mode each worker's degraded-mode local limit is `ceil(limit / MEDIA_WORKERS)`, so N workers cannot each admit the whole limit | **Decided** (Manas, 2026-11-02) |
| D7 | Metric series identity | With workers, every process exports under its own `service.instance.id` (`<id or hostname>:<role><slot>`, stable across restarts), forced on; series multiply by the processes that emit each metric | **Decided** (Manas, 2026-11-02) |
| D8 | Config placement | `MEDIA_WORKERS` in the base block (new top-level key `processes`); `ANALYSIS_WORKERS` in the analysis block (`dialerAnalysis.processes`). Both default 0, which is the single process exactly as before | **Decided** (Manas, 2026-11-02) |
| D9 | Station token mint on a failed `SET` | With Redis configured, a `SET` that fails at mint answers `503 station_token_unavailable` (`StationTokenUnavailableError`, `agency/station-token.ts`) on every route that mints (the campaign join, `POST /sessions/:id/station-token`, the soft-phone session), and the public layer lets that reviewed 503 through the error mask. Before, the token was kept in-process, where `verifyAndConsume` never looks while Redis is configured, so the client got a token certain to be refused 4401. The console retries it on its own wherever it mints (the station's re-mint ladder, the campaign join and the soft-phone open, `retryOnStationTokenUnavailable`); the app retries a re-mint on its own (`_scheduleRetry`) and shows its join screen's retry on a join (no app change was made). Without Redis at all the in-process store still serves both mint and verify | **Decided** (Manas, 2026-11-02) |
| D10 | Vendor rate-limit pause | Shared: one analysis process's breaker opening is relayed by the primary over IPC (not Redis) to every other, which pauses to the same moment and escalation (`VendorRateLimitBreaker.applyRemotePause`); each sends one probe when it ends | **Decided** (Manas, 2026-11-02) |
| D11 | `agency_agent_sessions.owner_replica` | Left as the joiner's replica id (the primary's, with workers); informational, nothing reads it | **Decided** (Manas, 2026-11-02) |
| D12 | Postgres connections with workers | Each process has its own pool: the primary `DB_POOL_MAX` (30), each media worker `MEDIA_WORKER_DB_POOL_MAX` (10: it writes only its own calls' rows), each analysis worker `min(DB_POOL_MAX, DIALER_ANALYSIS_CONCURRENCY + 2)`. At boot with workers, before any worker is forked, the primary compares the sum plus 5 with `max_connections − superuser_reserved_connections`; past it, it **refuses to start** (exit 1, the message names both numbers and the settings to change), so a host that would be refused connections at its first peak fails at deploy instead. When it fits, `db_connections_planned` / `db_connections_allowed` and `/readyz?detail=1` carry the numbers. Limits that cannot be read give no verdict and the boot goes on. (The first default logged an error and started anyway.) | **Decided** (Manas, 2026-11-02) |
| D13 | Readiness detail and probe cost | `/readyz?detail=1` (pids, loop numbers, the Postgres budget) needs `Authorization: Bearer <READYZ_DETAIL_TOKEN>`, compared in constant time; unset, the detail is off; otherwise 401 before anything is probed. A static token rather than the super-admin login: the detail must answer while Postgres is the problem (the super-admin check reads the database), and a read-only, scoped token is what a script or `curl` should hold. Not IP-gated (`TRUST_PROXY_HOPS` makes `request.ip` proxy-asserted). The plain body keeps the verdict and each worker's state name (D5). One readiness answer (Postgres, Redis, a `health.ping` to each worker) serves every probe for 1 s (`READINESS_CACHE_MS`), concurrent probes share one run; a not-ready verdict is at most 1 s old. Each process samples its loop over a fixed 5 s window on its own timer (`LOOP_SAMPLE_WINDOW_MS`), and readers never reset it | **default, pending Manas** |
| B16 (amended) | Public API layer → internal handlers | Public handlers still call `callCore` in-process. With media workers, an internal handler that reads per-session runtime state is forwarded to the session's worker (`api/core-owners.ts` classifies every agency route; an unclassified one fails at boot). B16's "one process, no network hop" becomes "no network hop" | **Decided** (Manas, 2026-11-02) |

Wrap-up redial, 2026-11-03 (built as asked; the choices the ask left open made as below). An agent in wrap-up after a connected call, before saving the outcome,
redials the same customer and is connected to them again (`POST /proxy/agency/sessions/:id/redial`,
`apps/server/src/agency/wrapup-redial.ts`), on campaigns with `allow_wrapup_redial` on (default
off, migration 0008):

| # | Topic | Default used | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| WR1 | What closes the redialled call | It keeps its own outcome (`connected`: the call happened, so stats and talk time stay true) and gets the reserved system disposition `redialed` (`AGENCY_REDIALED_DISPOSITION_CODE`, refused in a campaign's catalog) and `wrapup_resolution = 'redialed'`. The redial is a new attempt row linked by `redial_of_attempt_id`, numbered by `redial_number` | **default, pending Manas** |
| WR2 | Limit | `AGENCY_WRAPUP_REDIAL_LIMIT` = 2 redials per original call (3 calls at most), one constant in the contracts, enforced on the server; the wrap-up frame's `redial_remaining` is what the clients render | **Decided** (ask) |
| WR3 | Retry budget | A redial does not spend the contact's `attempt_count` (`max_attempts`): it continues one conversation; the hard limit WR2 bounds it. Neither the retry nor the our-fault ledger is charged | **default, pending Manas** (the ask's open question; the recommendation) |
| WR4 | Which wrap-ups offer it | One that owes an outcome (a non-empty catalog), after a call that bridged, or after a redial whatever its outcome. A campaign with no catalog releases the customer at the end of the call, so there is no one held to redial. A paused or stopped campaign refuses it | **default, pending Manas** |
| WR5 | A redial that does not connect | The customer stays held (`connected`, uncharged) and the agent is back in wrap-up on the redial attempt, owing the outcome there, with the redials its number leaves. A redial that has not bridged is dispositionable only once it has ended; one that bridged, mid-call like any conversation. The settle-time park never re-parks a contact a disposition already released (`parkForWriteUp`: only `in_flight`/`connected` with no disposition on the attempt; paced calls use it too). A refused redial (gates, the agent no longer in wrap-up) leaves or reopens the wrap-up on the original with a fresh window. When wrap-up cannot be reopened (no station, or `WrapupManager.enter` threw) the agent is held out of the pool: `offline` with no station, else `break` (`AgencyDialer.releaseOwingOutcome`); the outcome stays owed on the row and the lapsed-wrap-up sweep closes it. Never back to the pool with an outcome owed | **Decided** (ask) |
| WR6 | Pre-dial checks | The pacing tick's gates (calling hours, DNC, number validity, all failing closed) run before anything is written; a refusal is a named code (`redial_blocked_dnc`, `redial_blocked_calling_hours`, `redial_blocked`) and leaves the agent in wrap-up with the countdown running | **Decided** (ask) |
| WR7 | Binding and caller ID | A redial is always early-bound (the agent sees the ringing panel, whatever `FF_AGENCY_LATE_BINDING` says) and dials from the caller ID of the call it redials while that number is still in the campaign's pool (since DN4: the contact owner's dedicated number first, and "in the pool" means in the rotation) | **default, pending Manas** |
| WR8 | Notes | The notes on the redialled attempt are copied onto the redial when it starts, so the write-up continues where it was | **default, pending Manas** |
| WR9 | A dial whose outcome is unknown | A redial the carrier may have queued (`add_lead` timed out) is not held back 15 minutes like a paced one: the customer stays parked `connected`, which pacing never claims. The reopened wrap-up offers no further redial (the carrier may still ring the customer); when the call ended while the dial was still in flight, the offer is withheld until the dial answers and restored only if its outcome is known | **default, pending Manas** |
| WR10 | Crash mid-redial | The startup reaper ends a non-terminal redial `orphaned` and requeues the customer `pending` on the our-fault ledger, as for any attempt; the outcome owed on the conversation is then lost. The lapsed-wrap-up sweep treats an un-dispositioned redial like a connected call (`no_disposition`, routed as `connected`) | **default, pending Manas** |
| WR11 | While a redial is in flight | From Redial until wrap-up reopens (set-up, ring, call), `/available`, supervisor force-available and `/leave` answer 409 `agent_on_live_call` (`AgencyDialer.hasRedialInFlight`, the WrapupManager's set-up mark). A station reconnect during a wrap-up this process holds comes back `wrapup`, not `offline`/`break`, so Redial works on the first press | **default, pending Manas** |
| WR12 | Stats | A conversation counts once, at the attempt that carries its outcome (the last of the chain, bridged or not: `CONVERSATION_ATTEMPT_SQL`), in the connect buckets, conversions, the series' and agent stats' `connected`/`successes`, and the floor's `calls_handled`; `redialed` is in no breakdown or conversion. Redials are counted as `attempts_redialed` (campaign) and `redials` (floor row), not in `attempts_retried` or `attempts_connected`, and in no connect rate: the connect rate is human connects ÷ placed first dials across `/stats`, the agent record and Insights (`HUMAN_CONNECT_ATTEMPT_SQL` / `PLACED_FIRST_DIAL_SQL` in `sql-fragments.ts`, IN10), so a queued attempt never placed is in no denominator either; they stay in `attempts_total` and the series' `attempts`. Talk time and AHT are per bridged leg; `redialed` wrap-ups stay out of `avg_wrapup_seconds` | **default, pending Manas** |
| WR13 | Media workers | A redial is dispatched in the worker that owns the station (`session_param`), through its `LocalDialDispatcher`, as the soft phone's dials are: not through the primary's `IpcDialDispatcher` and its leadership grant, which authorise pacing, not an agent calling back a customer already in conversation. It is outside the channel's `maxDialsInFlight` back-pressure; an agent has at most one redial in flight | **default, pending Manas** |

Contact assignment, 2026-11-03 (built as asked, phases 1 and 2; the choices the ask left open made
as below). A supervisor assigns roster contacts to agents; on a `strict` or `preferred` campaign
each agent works their own queue; an agent can reopen or call their not-reached contacts; an
outcome in the campaign's catalog can grant the contact an override. Migration
`0009_contact_assignment.sql`; wire `packages/contracts/src/api/agency/assignment.ts` and
`agency/assignment.ts`; flag `agency_contact_assignment_enabled` (default off); permission
`agency.contacts.own` (floored at `agent`). Inbound routing is out of scope.

| # | Topic | Default used | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| CA1 | Modes | `assignment_mode` per campaign: `pool` (default; pacing ignores the owner, behaviour unchanged; an owner still has the contact in My contacts and may call or reopen it, and a reopened contact goes back to the shared queue), `strict` (an agent is paired only with contacts assigned to them; unassigned contacts are never dialled and, being open, keep the campaign from completing; the supervisor sees them as `unassigned_open`), `preferred` (own due contacts first, then unassigned ones; never another agent's). Pairing is by agent, one statement per tick (`claimForAgents`, a `LATERAL ... LIMIT 1` per agent on `idx_agency_contacts_assignee`); the engine re-checks the owner of every claimed row and hands back a mis-paired one | **Decided** (ask) |
| CA2 | What an operation moves | "Open" is `pending` and not DNC. Assignment operations (`random_split`, `count`, `filter`, `range`, `reassign`, `rebalance`, `unassign`) never move a live contact (`in_flight` / `connected`: counted `skipped_live`). Same-number contacts move as one group (the claim's digit projection); a live member stays and the rest of its group moves; a number with no digits is its own group. Roster order is `created_at, csv_line_number, id`. `count` takes the next open unassigned contacts (a reached, exhausted or DNC row is nothing to work), passes over live ones rather than counting them, and takes a group that starts before N whole (at most one group over). `random_split` / `filter` with `only_unassigned = false` take pool contacts in any state and owned ones that are `pending`; `unassign` and `range` silently skip ids that are not open, live or on the campaign (a drag-selected page never moves another agent's reached contacts). `reassign` moves the source agent's open contacts. Rebalance: every listed agent staffed; targets floor/ceil of the mean, the ceil to the heaviest; givers give their newest never-attempted, non-callback contacts, whole groups only | **default, pending Manas** |
| CA3 | Nothing due | On `strict` / `preferred`, an agent with nothing due is not reserved. In `preferred` mode the pool's due contacts go, one each, to the agents without own work due in pacing's fairness order (longest idle first), and only those agents read due: with k due pool contacts the rest read not due (`waiting_for_retry`, or `outside_calling_hours`, with their own `next_due_at`) until the next tick, so the frame says due exactly when the tick reserves. The station is told why by a `queue` frame (`due`, or `outside_calling_hours` / `queue_empty` / `callbacks_later` / `waiting_for_retry`, with `next_due_at`), sent only on a change and once again after each `ready` (the leader forgets the session's last frame on `station_ready`); the join bootstrap, `ready` and the agent's My contacts summary carry the same reading (`agency/pacing/agent-queue.ts`, one rule for all four; those three read one agent alone, so a due pool contact is theirs, as for the agent first in line, and the station's next frame corrects it). Due outranks a closed window (the pre-dial gate defers a contact outside its own window); `next_due_at` is null while due. On `pool` there is no per-agent queue; since agent station BE-5 every agent on a `pool` campaign is sent the campaign's shared queue on the same frame, the bootstrap and `ready` (`readPoolQueueState`, one probe per campaign, not one per agent), and the dialling is unchanged | **default, pending Manas** |
| CA4 | Callback ownership | On `strict` / `preferred`, an outcome that books a callback (`requires_datetime`) on a contact in the pool assigns it to the agent who took the call (history reason `callback`), even when a supervisor files the outcome; an owned contact keeps its owner. Only the first disposition write of an attempt does this | **default, pending Manas** |
| CA5 | Callback grace and fallback | `callback_owner_grace_minutes` (default 30) and `callback_fallback` (`pool` default, or `wait`). A contact is a callback while its last disposition booked one AND that call is still its latest outcome (`isCallbackSql`): a callback dialled and not answered is not reached like any other. A running owner-mode campaign's callback due longer than the grace, whose owner has no live session on it, loses its owner (`callback_fallback`, actor none, one audit row per batch of up to 1000); `wait` leaves it. The pacing leader sweeps each campaign every 30 s, in the background (the tick does not wait for it; one sweep per campaign at a time; a move wakes the campaign for its next tick). In `strict` mode a fallen-back callback is then an unassigned open contact, not dialled until a supervisor assigns it | **default, pending Manas** (the strict-mode consequence is flagged) |
| CA6 | Unstaff | `on_unstaff`: `return_to_pool` (default) moves the agent's `pending` contacts on that campaign to the pool, never a live one; `keep` moves nothing. On all three unstaff paths (a supervisor removing the agent, a tenant admin removing the membership, a super-admin revoking it), one statement over every campaign closed. Audited only where the agent held an open or live contact; history actor the supervisor, or none on the membership paths. A contact live at the unstaff settles back still owned by the former agent; the pacing leader's owner sweep (every 30 s, in the background as CA5's, on a running `strict` / `preferred` campaign with `return_to_pool`) returns the `pending` contacts of every owner without an active staffing row (history reason `unstaffed`, actor none, one audit row per batch of up to 1000, `cause: sweep`) | **default, pending Manas** |
| CA7 | Agent retry and the agent's own call | Eligibility is one SQL definition (`agentRetryBlockSql`): not-reached or exhausted, not suppressed, the campaign's `allow_agent_retry` on, a round left (`agent_retry_rounds`, default 1, max 10), the cooldown since the last attempt passed (`agent_retry_cooldown_minutes`, default 30) unless a retry-delay override is held; an open contact already due is not retryable (it is in the queue). Reopen: eligible contacts go `pending`, due now, one round spent; foreign or missing ids are dropped silently. Call: one contact now on the campaign's normal path (`agent_manual` attempt, caller ID, recording, outcome pad, wrap-up redial), allowed for a due contact, a held retry-delay override (spent) or a retry round (spent; only this one needs `allow_agent_retry`, else `409 contact_not_retryable`). Calling hours and DNC always apply. A reopen needs the campaign `running` or `paused` (else `409 campaign_not_running`; the summary's `retry.allowed` says the same); the call needs it `running` and the `agency_dialer_enabled` kill switch on (`403 feature_disabled`), re-read on every dial as pacing does. Every reopen and call is audited, ids only (a reopen names its contacts, up to 500) | **default, pending Manas** |
| CA8 | Overrides | A catalog entry may carry `override`: `extra_attempts` (1–5), `retry_delay`, `calling_hours`; at least one; unknown keys refused; refused on an entry that suppresses or is terminal, and in the soft phone's catalog. Recording it REPLACES the contact's grant, every time (a repeated granting outcome grants again, so a contact that keeps promising stays dialable); outcomes without one leave a grant alone. `extra_attempts` is spent when a settlement or disposition would mark the contact `exhausted` for `max_attempts`: it stays `pending` on the rule's delay, at most once per attempt (`override_applied`). `retry_delay` and `calling_hours` are spent by the agent's own call; hours open a closed window only, with a 10–500 character note on the attempt. DNC wins over every override | **default, pending Manas** |
| CA9 | Flag | `agency_contact_assignment_enabled` gates the surface, never the runtime: every new route answers `403 assignment_disabled`, and a campaign create, PATCH or retry that sets an assignment setting or an `override` to something other than its default AND other than what the campaign (for a retry, the parent) already stores answers `403 feature_disabled` (details by field), so a campaign set up while the flag was on still saves after it is turned off, and putting a setting back to its default or dropping an override is always allowed. Pacing reads the stored mode whatever the flag | **default, pending Manas** |
| CA10 | Mode changes | `assignment_mode` changes only while the campaign is `draft` or `paused` (`409 mode_change_requires_pause`); a change is audited `agency_campaign.assignment_mode_changed` `{ from, to }`. The other settings patch at any status | **default, pending Manas** |
| CA11 | Roster replace | Roster replace (B15) is not built, so "superseded contacts lose their assignment" has nothing to act on; uploaded rows land unassigned. When B15 is built, its replace clears `assigned_user_id`, `assigned_at` and `assigned_by_user_id` on every contact it retires and writes one `agency_contact_assignment_events` row each in the same transaction, under one batch id, with a new reason (e.g. `superseded`: a CHECK change in a new migration). A retry campaign may copy owners (`keep_assignments`, history reason `inherited`): only owners still actively staffed on the parent; staffing is not copied, so the supervisor staffs the child with them before starting it, or CA6's sweep returns their open contacts to the pool once it runs | **Decided** (B15 stays open) |
| CA12 | Audit | `agency_contacts.assigned` (one per committed operation: batch, kind, moved, skipped live, to pool, per agent), `agency_contacts.unassigned_on_unstaff`, `agency_contacts.callback_fallback`, `agency_contacts.agent_retry_reopened`, `agency_contact.agent_dialed`, `agency_campaign.assignment_mode_changed`; each contact move is also a row in `agency_contact_assignment_events` under the operation's `batch_id`. Ids only | **Decided** (ask) |
| CA13 | Preview and commit | Every operation is previewed and then committed with the preview's token: a hash of the normalised operation (seed included), the counts and an order-independent hash of every move. The commit locks the rows, re-plans, and writes only when the new plan matches the token contact by contact, or, for a preview moving 100 or more contacts, when its move and to-pool counts are within 2% of the preview's (`commitDrift`: a running campaign's claims and settlements change rows every second; the commit then writes the plan as it stands and reports what it moved); otherwise `409 preview_stale` with the fresh preview (also for a lock timeout or a deadlock). A random split must send back the preview's seed. The token is not signed: a token that matches the current roster commits exactly what a preview would show | **default, pending Manas** |
| CA14 | Agent reads | The agent's routes take the subject from the session, never a parameter, and answer `403 not_staffed` off an active staffing row, the same answer for a campaign that is missing or in another tenant or account (one helper, `staffedCampaign`, for the reads and the actions); with no `X-Account-Id` the flag is read again for the campaign's account. The list is in due order (keyset); hero values that are blank are left out and `q` searches hero columns only. A contact of another agent is the same 404 as a missing one on the per-contact call | **default, pending Manas** |
| CA15 | Soft phone on an owned contact | On a `strict` campaign an agent's tagged soft-phone call refuses a number whose matched contact is assigned to another agent (`409 contact_assigned_elsewhere`, no override), judged on the contact the soft phone would pick (oldest pending, else oldest), before hours and DNC, and not re-checked under the claim's lock (a reassignment in between, or a number shared by contacts of different owners, can slip through; same-number grouping makes the second rare). A supervisor may call it; the pool and the caller's own contacts are fine; `pool` and `preferred` campaigns do not check (only `strict` promises an agent that nobody else calls their contacts) | **default, pending Manas** |

Dedicated caller ID per agent, 2026-11-06 (ClickUp "Dedicated caller ID per agent allocation").
Caller IDs were campaign-wide: `agency_campaigns.caller_ids`, rotated round-robin. A supervisor
can now dedicate one of a campaign's caller IDs to an agent staffed on it, and every dial to a
contact that agent owns on the campaign (`agency_contacts.assigned_user_id`, contact assignment
above) goes out from that number. Migration `0018_agent_caller_ids.sql`; wire
`packages/contracts/src/api/agency/agent-caller-id.ts` and `caller_id` on
`AgencyMyContactsSummary`; the rule `resolveCallerId` in `agency/pacing/caller-id-pool.ts`; the
routes `api/routes/proxy-agency-assignment/agent-caller-ids.ts`. Out of scope: retry campaigns
copying dedications, the mobile app's UI, inbound routing.

| # | Topic | Default used | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| DN1 | Scope and data | One table, `agency_campaign_agent_caller_ids` (`tenant_id` FK `tenants` CASCADE, `account_id` the campaign's, `campaign_id` with no FK as the staffing's, `user_id` FK `users` CASCADE, `caller_id VARCHAR(20)` stored trimmed and non-empty, `set_by_user_id` FK SET NULL, `set_at`), `UNIQUE (campaign_id, user_id)` and `UNIQUE (campaign_id, caller_id)`: one number per agent and one agent per number on a campaign; the same number may be dedicated on other campaigns. `user_id` is FK'd, unlike `assigned_user_id` (0009 left that one without because a user delete would scan a million-row roster; this table has one row per staffed agent per campaign, and the staffing row it follows cascades the same way). An index on `user_id` serves the cascade and the unstaff release. A row counts (is EFFECTIVE) only while its agent has an ACTIVE staffing row on the campaign and it was set at or after that staffing row's `assigned_at`: one SQL definition (`EFFECTIVE_DEDICATION_SQL`, `agent-caller-id.repository.ts`) applied by every read (the dial paths' map, the supervisor's list and its rotation, the My contacts summary, the PATCH guard). A row an unstaff left behind (a release that failed, or one that raced a PUT) is never dialled, listed or guarded, never blocks a PUT (which deletes it), and never revives when the agent is staffed again | **default, pending Manas** |
| DN2 | The rule | One pure function for every dial path, `resolveCallerId`, fed by one read of the campaign's dedications, `DedicatedCallerIdCache` (`agency/pacing/dedicated-caller-ids.ts`; the agent's own call and the wrap-up redial ask its `callerIdForContact`, pacing and the soft phone apply the rule to its map themselves): the contact's OWNER has a dedicated number that is still one of the campaign's usable caller IDs (`usableCallerIds`, trimmed) → that number; otherwise the rotation. On a campaign pinned to a tenant's own carrier account "usable" means dialable there too (PL13's addendum): the pool is `dialableCallerIds`. The owner decides, not the dialling agent: in `strict` they are the same; in `preferred` an agent's pool contacts use the rotation; in `pool` an owned contact still goes out from its owner's number whoever takes it | **default, pending Manas** |
| DN3 | The rotation | The usable caller IDs minus every dedicated number still usable, in stored order, picked round-robin by `pickCallerId`; when that leaves nothing (every number dedicated), the whole usable list, so dedications never halt a campaign. Null only for an empty pool, as before (the caller halts or refuses, never throws) | **default, pending Manas** |
| DN4 | Dial paths | (1) Pacing: the claimed rows carry `assigned_user_id`; the dedicated map is read once per dialling batch, BEFORE any agent is reserved or contact claimed, never on a tick that plans no dial and never per contact. Every dial path reads it from a per-campaign cache (`DedicatedCallerIdCache`, the pacing engine's generation-guarded TTL cache, 2 s; one indexed query on a miss: `uq_agency_agent_caller_id_agent` joined to the campaign's active staffing, DN1), invalidated by `dedications_changed` on the pacing bus, which the PUT, the DELETE and every unstaff (all three staffing-close paths, `returnContactsOnUnstaff`) publish after their commit; it caches only while the process's pacing engine listens to the bus, so a media worker reads through. A failed read dials nothing and takes nothing: the tick records the idle reason `dedications_unavailable` (the metric's own; the pacing history counts it as `idle_no_reservations`) and logs, throttled per campaign. (2) The agent's own call (`my-contact-call.ts`). (3) The wrap-up redial: the owner's dedicated number wins; otherwise WR7's "the same number while it is still in the pool" now means still in the ROTATION, so a number dedicated to someone else since is not reused for a contact that is not theirs. (4) The soft phone tagged to a campaign: a requested caller ID outside the campaign's usable caller IDs is refused as before (`caller_id_not_allowed` with the set); an in-set request goes through `resolveCallerId` as its sticky number, as the redial's previous number does: when the number matches a contact of the tag (the contact the soft phone would pick, as CA15) whose owner has a dedicated number still on it, the call goes out from that number WHATEVER in-set caller ID was requested; otherwise the requested number while it is in the ROTATION, else the rotation's pick, so another agent's dedicated number is never dialled for an unmatched or unowned number or another owner's contact (the mobile app sends the campaign's first caller ID by default and cannot change in this work, a console hand-pick or redial can send any in-set number; when every number is dedicated the rotation is the whole list, DN3, and the request is kept); the response and the call row's `caller_id` report what was dialled; with no caller ID requested, the owner's number, else the rotation's pick. The match is read before the caller-ID step but its refusals keep their place (a caller ID outside the set is still answered before `contact_in_flight`). Untagged calls are unchanged | **default, pending Manas** |
| DN5 | Supervisor routes | Beside the contact-assignment routes, with their guards (`agency.supervise`, id shape, the ownership probe; the flag on the PUT only, DN9): `GET /campaigns/:id/agent-caller-ids` (the dedications by agent name, `in_campaign`, the rotation, `all_dedicated`); `PUT /campaigns/:id/agents/:userId/caller-id` (only an agent with an active staffing row, else 404 `agent_not_staffed`; the number trimmed and one of the campaign's usable caller IDs, else 409 `caller_id_not_in_campaign` with `details.caller_ids`; held by another agent → 409 `caller_id_taken` with `details.user_id`, `agent_name`; an agent who holds a number has it replaced in one statement; setting the number they already hold writes nothing; a unique-violation race is `caller_id_taken`, never a 500; 400 `caller_id_invalid` per field). The staffing and the campaign's caller IDs are checked inside the write's transaction: a per-(campaign, agent) advisory lock, the campaign row `FOR SHARE`, the agent's active staffing row `FOR SHARE`, so an unstaff closing the staffing waits for the PUT (and its release then sees the row) or is seen by it, and a PATCH dropping the number waits for it or is seen by it. `set_at` is the `clock_timestamp()` after those locks. `DELETE …/caller-id` (204, also when none was set; audited only when an effective number was released) | **default, pending Manas** |
| DN6 | Campaign PATCH | A `caller_ids` that would drop a dedicated number the campaign still has → 409 `caller_id_dedicated`, `details.caller_ids` the numbers it would drop (after every other refusal); the supervisor releases or moves the dedication first. The check and the write are one transaction holding the campaign row `FOR UPDATE` (`updateLocked`), with the effective dedications read after the lock, so a concurrent PUT (DN5) is either seen or waits and then refuses the dropped number: a dedication of a just-dropped number cannot be stored and later reactivate when the number is re-added. A direct write that drops one only stops it being used (DN2's "still usable") | **default, pending Manas** |
| DN7 | Unstaff | On all three unstaff paths (a supervisor removing the agent, a tenant admin removing the membership, a super admin revoking it), the agent's dedicated number on each campaign whose staffing closed is released, whatever `on_unstaff` says (`keep` keeps contacts, not numbers), in CA6's one statement (`returnOnUnstaff`). Audited `agency_campaign.agent_caller_id_cleared` with `cause: 'unstaff'` (and the unstaff's own cause, `unstaff_cause`) only where a number was released. The release skips a campaign the agent has been staffed on again by the time it runs (a number set on the new staffing is theirs), and it reports (so audits) only a row that was effective for the staffing that just closed (set at or after its `assigned_at`); an older row a failed release left behind is deleted silently | **default, pending Manas** |
| DN8 | Callbacks and reassignment | No code of their own: the number follows the contact's owner at dial time (the owner comes from the contact row the dial claims; only the dedication map is cached, DN4), so contacts reassigned from A to B dial from B's number, and a callback CA4 assigns to the agent who took the call dials from theirs | **default, pending Manas** |
| DN9 | Flag | `agency_contact_assignment_enabled` gates setting a number (`PUT …/caller-id` answers 403 `assignment_disabled`), never the runtime: stored dedications are honoured whatever the flag (CA9's pattern). Reading and releasing work whatever the flag (`GET /campaigns/:id/agent-caller-ids`, `DELETE …/caller-id`), on CA9's principle that putting a setting back to its default is always allowed: with the flag off, pacing still dials from a stored dedication and the campaign PATCH still refuses to drop it (DN6), so a supervisor must be able to see and release it | **default, pending Manas** |
| DN10 | Audit | `agency_campaign.agent_caller_id_set` `{ campaign_id, user_id, caller_id, previous_caller_id }` (`previous_caller_id` is the number the write replaced, right also for two first-time PUTs, which DN5's advisory lock serialises) and `agency_campaign.agent_caller_id_cleared` `{ campaign_id, user_id, caller_id, cause }` (`supervisor` or `unstaff`), filed under the campaign's account, in the platform audit catalog and the campaign activity vocabulary (group Staffing). Ids and the number only | **default, pending Manas** |


Automatic outcomes for unanswered calls, 2026-11-07. For calls nobody answered, a campaign
files an outcome chosen in advance, so whoever reads the call later knows what to do with it
(for example busy → "Retry later", no answer → "Callback"). It is set in the campaign builder
and settings ("Unanswered calls"). Migration `0022_outcome_dispositions.sql`. Wire:
`packages/contracts/src/agency/outcome-dispositions.ts`, `outcome_dispositions` on the campaign,
and `auto_disposition_code` on the attempt row, the station's prior attempts and the
customer-history timeline. The rule is `agency/auto-disposition.ts` on `packages/domain/src/auto-disposition.ts` (shared with the console); the write is
`settleContact` (`agency/dialer/contact-settlement.ts`); the validation is
`validateOutcomeDispositions` (`agency/campaign-config.ts`), shared by both layers. Out of scope:
the soft phone; Insights breakdowns, filters and retry selectors by automatic outcome; My
contacts and the roster (they read the contact, OD4); and the mobile app, whose decoders read
fields by name and never read `auto_disposition_code`.

| # | Topic | Default used | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| OD1 | What an automatic outcome does | A label only. The mapped code is stamped on the attempt. The contact is routed exactly as without a mapping: same state, next attempt time and attempt count, by the campaign's outcome retry policy. The mapped entry's own `retry`, `requires_datetime` (no callback time is booked) and `override` (nothing is granted) are not applied. Repeat-dial limits are unchanged | **Decided** (Manas, 2026-11-07: "Label only") |
| OD2 | Which outcomes | `no_answer`, `busy`, `failed`, `invalid`, `abandoned` (`AGENCY_AUTO_DISPOSITION_OUTCOMES`). Not `connected` (the agent writes it up). Not `agent_disconnected`, `orphaned` or `canceled` (our own faults and teardowns, charged to the our-fault ledger; they say nothing about the customer). Not `machine` (never produced). VoiceLink reports a switched-off handset as `busy` (`classifyVoicelinkOutcome`: Q.850 16 with SIP 200 on an unanswered call), so "switched off" is mapped through Busy. A declined call arrives as `busy` (the same signature) or as `no_answer` (a rejection or SIP 487, which `classifyAttemptOutcome` files as `no_answer` unless we ended the call), depending on what the carrier reports | **default, pending Manas** |
| OD3 | Which catalog entries | Any entry of the campaign's catalog except one that `suppress`es or is `terminal`: such a label claims a consequence ("Do not call", "closed") that an automatic outcome never has, since the retry policy alone decides what happens to the contact (dialled again, exhausted, or, for `invalid`, always suppressed). A `requires_datetime` or `retry` entry may be mapped and is a label like any other (OD1). The label is stamped whatever the routing, so the attempt that exhausts a contact carries it too | **default, pending Manas** |
| OD4 | Where it is stored | On the attempt only, in its own column (`agency_call_attempts.auto_disposition_code`, CHECK: only on those five outcomes). `disposition_code` and the contact's `last_disposition` keep meaning "a person recorded this", so success and connect rates, agent outcome counts, the disposition route's replay rule, the wrap-up sweeps, the My contacts buckets, assignment filters, retry selectors and Insights are unchanged. A person may still write up the attempt; the two codes sit side by side | **default, pending Manas** |
| OD5 | When it is stamped | When the attempt settles: the code the campaign mapped as the dialler last loaded it (the campaign row the dial was placed with, which a campaign PATCH invalidates for the next dial, not for a call already ringing). It is re-checked against the catalog (a code no longer there, or now suppressing or terminal, stamps nothing). One guarded statement (`stampAutoDisposition`: ended, same outcome, none stamped yet), so a replayed settle cannot restamp with a since-edited map. Editing the map never rewrites past attempts. Best-effort: a failed write is logged and never holds up routing the contact. Its own guarded UPDATE after the ended-row write, run beside the contact's charge (not before it, so it adds no round trip to the agent's release), rather than a column of that write: the dialer only knows which path settles the contact (settle, outcome-unknown hold, redial park) after the row is ended. An unanswered dial already writes its row about four times; this is one more, only on a campaign with a mapping, and it marks the Insights bucket those writes already mark | **default, pending Manas** |
| OD6 | Validation | Keys: the five outcomes only, refused with the reason otherwise. Values: a code of the effective catalog that is neither suppress nor terminal. The internal handler checks every create, PATCH and retry override against the stored campaign (or the parent), so a catalog edit that removes a mapped code, or makes it suppress or terminal, is refused (400, keyed `outcome_dispositions.<outcome>`) until the mapping changes. The public layer checks the shape, and the cross-field rule when the body carries both (the console's save always does). The PATCH audit row names the field when it changes (IN8) | **default, pending Manas** |
| OD7 | Which dials | Campaign dials that settle through the dialer's charged path: paced dials and the agent's own calls on their contacts (CA). Not the soft phone (its own catalog and settlement), not wrap-up redials (the conversation's write-up is still owed), not outcome-unknown dials (we do not know what happened), not the reaper's orphans, and not a dial that failed before it reached the carrier (`abandonBeforeDial`, a pacing or agent-dial dispatch failure: our failure, uncharged and redialled after a minute). So a `failed` mapping labels only the failures the carrier reported | **default, pending Manas** |
| OD8 | Retry campaigns | The map is inherited with the catalog it names (`RETRY_INHERITED_CONFIG_KEYS`) and may be overridden in `config_overrides`, checked against the parent's catalog | **default, pending Manas** |

Campaign insights, 2026-11-04 (ClickUp "Campaign insights page"; the data mapping and IN1–IN8
signed off by Manas on 2026-11-04). One Insights tab per campaign, every number opening a list
whose total is that number. Migrations `0011_insights_foundation.sql`, `0012_insights_rollup.sql`,
`0013_insights_pacing_history.sql`; wire `packages/contracts/src/api/agency/insights.ts`; design
and API in [`insights.md`](insights.md). IN9–IN12 are the build's own calls where the mapping left
a definition open.

| # | Topic | Default used | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| IN1 | Where it lives | A tab of the campaign shell at `/agency/campaigns/:id/insights`, gated on `agency.supervise`; it replaces Overview and Performance, whose URLs redirect to it. Agents, Contacts, Call attempts, Activity and Settings stay. Split into five tabs by IN13. | **Decided** |
| IN2 | Pacing history | What was Prometheus-only (idle reasons, calling-hours deferrals, concurrency hits, pre-dial suppressions, our-fault retirements, redials refused at the limit) is persisted per campaign per minute (`agency_campaign_pacing_minutes`); it starts the day it ships | **Decided** |
| IN3 | Target date | None in v1; "pace vs needed pace" is dropped | **Decided** |
| IN4 | Callback timing | On time: the follow-up dial is by `callback_at` + 15 min (inclusive; an early dial is on time); late: after that; missed: never dialled and the contact closed first (whatever `callback_at` says), or `callback_at` passed, or the campaign ended. A closed contact's callback is never an owner's open callback | **Decided** |
| IN5 | Small samples | One minimum-sample floor, 25, for every rate on the page (`AGENCY_INSIGHTS_MIN_RATE_DENOMINATOR`) | **Decided** |
| IN6 | Attempts per contact | Counts every placed dial, wrap-up redials included (`agency_contacts.dials_placed`, kept by 0011's trigger) | **Decided** |
| IN7 | Calling hours | The launch checklist warns while the window and days are still the column defaults (09:00–20:00, Mon–Fri) | **Decided** |
| IN8 | Order | The defects found in the mapping and the start-recording writes ship ahead of the page | **Decided** |
| IN9 | Waiting buckets | Pending contacts split three ways that sum to the pending count: due now (`next_attempt_at <= now`, a callback whose time has come included), callback (later, waiting on a booked callback), retry (later, not a callback). One definition, `insights-sql.ts`, read by the counts and the Contacts list's `waiting=` filter | **default, pending Manas** |
| IN10 | Connect rate | One definition across the product (`/stats` `connect_rate_pct`, the agent record's roster, scorecard and grouped cells, and Insights): human connects (conversations coded neither voicemail nor `no_disposition`) ÷ placed first dials (`dialed_at` set, not a redial). Before this, `/stats` counted never-placed queued rows in the denominator and the agent record divided conversations by every dial. `rates_reportable` floors placed first dials. `by_campaign[]` rows carry `human_connects`, `first_dials`, the rate and its `rates_reportable` per campaign, which the agent page and the mobile app print as served (never `connected / attempts`); `/stats` serves `first_dials_placed`, so the campaign Performance card prints the rate as its own fraction ("N people answered, of M first dials placed") | **default, pending Manas** |
| IN11 | Who an allocation dial belongs to | The allocation table's contact columns are by owner; its dial columns (redials, retries, overrides, last activity) by the agent who placed the dial, so each opens a Call attempts list of exactly those dials. In `strict` mode the two agree | **default, pending Manas** |
| IN12 | "Lifetime" | From the campaign's `created_at` to now, at most 92 days (`AGENCY_INSIGHTS_MAX_RANGE_DAYS`, the limit on every range read); an older campaign's Lifetime serves the last 92 days and the page says so | **default, pending Manas** |
| IN13 | One tab per question | Insights is split into Overview, Calls, Callbacks and Health, panels of the detail page beside Agents (`/agency/campaigns/:id/{overview,calls,agents,callbacks,health}`), all gated on `agency.supervise` except Agents' floor and staffing. Overview carries the status headline, six figures marked "now" or the range, a "Needs your attention" list (`utils/agency/insights/overview.ts`) and the roster; Agents gains the leaderboard, allocation and overrides; Settings gains the lifecycle and upload history. `…/insights` redirects to Overview. The range is carried between the analytic tabs. The Callbacks tab carries a danger badge with the overdue callbacks as of now, on every campaign screen. Print prints every tab. The analytics event `agency_campaign_tab_viewed` reports `overview`, `calls`, `callbacks` and `health` where it reported `insights` | **Decided** (Manas, 2026-11-07) |
| IN14 | A grouped bar, still one tab per question | The campaign bar (`utils/agencyCampaignTabs.ts`) shows three labelled groups with a divider between them, never menus: Monitor (Overview, Calls, Agents, Callbacks, Health), Data (Contacts, Call log), Set up (Settings); on a phone it scrolls sideways and the labels hide. *(Amended by NV2: the labels sit above their tabs, the divider went, and the labels stay on a phone.)* Each tab keeps its gate, a group with no tab left is dropped (a viewer sees Monitor with Agents), and Agents stays the landing tab below `agency.supervise`. Call attempts is now Call log (URL `/attempts`); Soft-phone calls is its second view, a segmented control on both list pages ("Dialler calls | Soft-phone calls", `/manual-calls`, behind `agency_softphone_enabled` as before), and Call log is the current tab on both and on `/attempts/:attemptId`. *(Amended 2026-11-22 by CL11: Call log is one list with type chips, "All · Dialler · Soft phone"; the segmented control is gone and `/manual-calls` redirects to `/attempts?type=soft_phone`. Soft-phone calls stays in `CAMPAIGN_TABS` with `group: null`, now pointing at that chip, so search still finds it. [`call-log.md`](call-log.md))* Activity is the campaign header's History button (`audit.read`, URL `/activity`), on the Monitor tabs and on Settings, which since 2026-11-12 carries the same header (name, status, lifecycle controls, History); its page is titled History, marks the button as current and links back to the role's landing tab. Both stay in `CAMPAIGN_TABS` with `group: null`, so `agency_campaign_tab_viewed` still names them. The top bar's breadcrumb names the campaign ("Campaigns / <name> / <tab>") through `usePageCrumbs` (`components/layout/PageCrumbs.tsx`), and is the only breadcrumb: the pages no longer render one of their own. Campaign Settings is split into anchored sections with an in-page nav (`utils/campaignSettingsSections.ts`; `…/settings#<id>` opens at one): General, Caller IDs (with the carrier), Calling hours, Dialling and retries (retry policy, abandonment limit), Wrap-up and outcomes (the outcome catalog, unanswered calls, wrap-up), Recording (and the call summary), Agents (contact assignment and inbound sticky, only while one is on or assignment settings are left over), Lifecycle and uploads (id `roster-uploads`, `agency.supervise`); still one form and one Save, no field changed. The Save is a bar stuck to the bottom of the view while anything is unsaved ("N unsaved changes · Discard · Save changes", one per changed setting), and leaving with unsaved edits asks first (links, Back and Forward, a reload); a name or caller ID taken away is said on its field and holds Save. New campaign asks the same before leaving input it has not saved | Ten tabs in one row read as a wall, and two of them were not questions about the campaign: soft-phone calls are more calls, and the activity trail is who changed what. Grouping keeps IN13's one question per tab | **Decided** (Manas, 2026-11-11, navigation redesign) |

Abandonment ceiling in the console, 2026-11-05 (ClickUp "Campaign builder: let the supervisor set
the abandonment ceiling by dragging a line (default 3%)"). The server already took
`abandonment_ceiling_pct` on create and PATCH; the console only displayed it. The builder's "How
agents work" step and the settings page's Behaviour section now show a meter with a draggable
ceiling line and a number field (`CampaignBehaviourSection`); Insights draws the same meter, on the
same scale, against the measured rate. AC1–AC3 answer the ask's "To decide".

| # | Topic | Default used | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| AC1 | Range | The console offers 0.5–10% in steps of 0.1 on a fixed 0–10% scale (`utils/agency/abandonmentCeiling.ts`). The server still accepts `> 0` and `<= 100` (the column CHECK and `validateAbandonmentCeiling`), so a value set through the API outside the console's range (25, or 7.25 with two decimals) loads and prints as stored and is kept as stored: it does not block saving other edits, and it is sent back unchanged. Only a change has to land in 0.5–10 with one decimal (`ceilingFieldError`, the form's `abandonmentCeilingStoredPct`). The editable meter stays on 0–10% (a stored 25 pins at the right-hand end, labelled 25%); the read-only Insights meter grows its scale to the next multiple of 5 when the ceiling or the rate is above 10% | **default, pending Manas** |
| AC2 | Raising it above 3% | Allowed, with an amber note that 3% is the limit most dropped-call rules set. No extra role: every campaign write already needs `agency.campaigns.write` (account admin and above), so a role gate above 3% would leave only tenant admins able to raise it | **default, pending Manas** |
| AC3 | Recording the change | No new write: a PATCH that changes it already writes `agency_campaign.config_changed` with `abandonment_ceiling_pct: { from, to }` and the signed-in actor (IN8), shown on the campaign's Activity page | **default, pending Manas** |

Abandonment grace, 2026-11-24. On 23 Nov two Staging campaigns were auto-paused 87 times over
their ceilings, and 9 of their 11 abandoned calls had reached an agent and been talked through:
VoiceLink's audio stream connected 1.06–1.25 s or about 5 s after its answer event, past the 1 s
grace the predicate allowed. Asked whether it should be one value or a setting, Manas answered
"Configurable, but default to 3" (2026-11-24); AG2–AG5 are the choices the build made for that,
pending Manas's ratification.

| # | Topic | Ruling | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| AG1 | When a bridged call counts as abandoned | A call bridged more than its grace after the carrier's answer is abandoned. The grace is **configurable per campaign, default 3 s** (Manas: "Configurable, but default to 3"; `DEFAULT_ABANDONMENT_GRACE_MS` = 3000, the one constant was 1000). The change from 1 s to 3 s was read at query time, so every figure changed with that deploy, history included, and 0074 rebuilt the Insights rollup; a campaign's own setting applies to new calls only (AG3). The ceiling (AC1–AC3) is unchanged | **decided — changed** (Manas, 2026-11-24) |
| AG2 | The setting: where, and its range | Per campaign, beside the abandonment ceiling: `agency_campaigns.abandonment_grace_ms` (0075), whole seconds from 1 to 5 (1000–5000 ms, a CHECK of exactly those five values), 3000 by default. Whole seconds so every allowed value is a bucket boundary of `agency_answer_to_bridge_seconds` and the share past any campaign's grace is exact. Settable on create and by PATCH at any status, refused with a 400 naming the field otherwise (`isAllowedAbandonmentGraceMs`), and filed in the campaign's activity as any setting change is (AC3's `config_changed`). The console offers a select of 1–5 seconds inside the sentence "Count a call as abandoned if the agent's audio connects more than [3] seconds after the customer answers", next to the limit: New campaign's Agents & timing › Advanced and campaign Settings' Dialling and retries. 1 s is the old constant; past 5 s the call has waited long enough that a customer may well have hung up | **default, pending Manas** |
| AG3 | Which grace a call is judged by | The campaign's grace at the moment the attempt row is created, stamped on the attempt (`agency_call_attempts.bridge_grace_ms`, 0075) by the INSERT itself, read from the campaign row: the pacing dial, the agent's own dial of their contact and the wrap-up redial (the redial takes the setting as it is now, not the redialled call's). Never rewritten. So a change of the setting applies to calls placed from then on and never rewrites the figures of calls already made, and `ABANDONED_ATTEMPT_PREDICATE_SQL` stays a predicate over one attempt row with no join (its independence as an audit of `agency_abandoned_total`). The settle site's in-process twin reads the same stamp (`DialCommand.bridgeGraceMs`), so `bridge_late` uses it too. Rows from before 0075 hold 3000, the grace AG1 counts them with. The rolling 24-hour rate can then hold calls judged by two graces for a day after a change | **default, pending Manas** |
| AG4 | Retry campaigns and templates | A retry campaign inherits the grace as it inherits the ceiling (`RETRY_INHERITED_CONFIG_KEYS`). A campaign template's `safety` section holds it beside the ceiling (`AGENCY_CAMPAIGN_TEMPLATE_SECTIONS`); a template saved before it that sets `safety` reads 3 s, the built-in value of a field its section leaves out (CT2) | **default, pending Manas** |
| AG5 | The dashboard | The Answer → Audio Connected panel draws the default 3 s (its line, its `le="3"` share, its title "against the default 3s grace") and says that a campaign set to another value is judged by its own: that campaign's late calls are `bridge_late` on Abandonment Causes by its own grace. No per-campaign series (series cost); a campaign's figure is the attempts table | **default, pending Manas** |

A warning before Resume, 2026-11-24 (Manas: "Add a warning"). On the same 23 Nov the guardrail's
87 pauses were each resumed by a supervisor 4–60 s later (`agency_campaign_lifecycle_action`), so
dialling went on over the ceiling: the console offered Resume for the guardrail's pause like any
other and never said the campaign would be paused again. A warning with an explicit confirmation,
never a block; the server's `/resume` still does not read the rate.

| # | Topic | Ruling | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| AW1 | Resuming a campaign the guardrail paused | Every Resume of a campaign paused with `pause_reason = 'abandonment_ceiling'` asks first, whatever the rate is now: "Resume after an automatic pause?", the rate the guardrail froze at the pause (`pause_abandonment_rate_pct`) against the campaign's ceiling, the rolling 24-hour rate now from `/stats` (`abandoned_24h` ÷ `answered_24h`, with both counts), that the dialler will pause the campaign again within about a minute while still over (the guardrail runs every 60 s), and "Get more agents on shift before you resume". Cancel or **Resume anyway**; nothing is sent until confirmed, and confirming always resumes. The preflight re-reads the row and `/stats` together, so a pause made after the page last painted is caught; a failed stats read, or one slower than 3 s, never holds Resume (the warning is then said from the row alone). When the row itself cannot be re-read (Live floor's card holds only the board's status, and the board carries no pause reason), Resume still asks, "Could not check this campaign", saying the console could not check why it is paused, rather than resume a guardrail pause unasked. A rate over the ceiling is said on enough decimals to read as over it ("3.03%, over the 3% limit", never "3%"). One place for every Resume offered: `useCampaignLifecycle` (the campaign header on the Monitor tabs and Settings, Live floor's campaign cards); words in `utils/agency/resumeWarning.ts`. Why it was paused is said once per campaign screen: the health strip's `auto_paused_abandonment` diagnosis where the screen reads stats, otherwise a line under the header ("Paused automatically at 11:03 AM: abandonment over the last 24 hours reached 4.2%, over the 3% limit.", or "on 23 Nov at 11:03 AM" for an earlier day; Settings, and any screen whose stats read failed). The dialog, the header line and the strip all say "the dialler" for what pauses it and "the 3% limit" for its ceiling | **decided — default kept** (Manas, 2026-11-24) |
| AW2 | Resuming any other pause while over the ceiling | Also warned: a campaign paused by a supervisor or its carrier account whose rolling 24-hour rate is over its ceiling now ("Abandonment is over the limit", the rate with its counts, "the dialler will pause the campaign again within about a minute"). "Over" is the guardrail's own rule, `abandonmentBreachesCeiling` in `packages/domain/src/abandonment-predicate.ts` (above the ceiling, not a single abandoned call in a sample too small to measure, `singleAbandonSampleFloor`, moved there from the guardrail, whose `breachedRows` a unit test holds equal to it), not the Insights 25-answered floor (IN5): the copy promises a pause, so it follows what pauses. Under the ceiling, or with no stats, Resume goes straight through as before | **decided — default kept** (Manas, 2026-11-24) |
| AW3 | Recording it | `agency_campaign_lifecycle_action` and `agency_campaign_lifecycle_failed` gain `resume_warning` (`abandonment_pause`, `over_ceiling`, `unchecked`, or null when none was shown), and the action's `confirmed` is true for a confirmed warning; a new `agency_campaign_resume_warning_cancelled` (`campaign_id`, `resume_warning`, `from_tab`) records Cancel. Console only: the agent app has no campaign lifecycle. No server write: the resume's audit row is unchanged | **decided — default kept** (Manas, 2026-11-24) |

Several running campaigns per account, 2026-11-05 (ClickUp "Allow multiple running campaigns per
account within the account's concurrency limit"). An account could run one campaign at a time
(`uq_agency_campaign_running`, Start answered 409 `another_campaign_running`). Dropping the index
alone left the account's `max_concurrent_calls` to whichever campaign's tick drew on the round's
headroom budget first (`PacingRound.takeHeadroom`, first come first served), and let two campaigns
ring one customer at once. MC1–MC7 replace it:

| # | Topic | Default used | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| MC1 | Allocation rule | Each free slot goes to the running campaign with the **fewest live calls** that still has an agent ready and a contact due (max-min fair share, equal weights). A campaign that cannot use its slots leaves them to the others in the same round; one that ran above an equal share while the other was idle keeps its calls (no hang-ups) and gets no new slot until it is back at or under the others as calls end. Ties go round-robin across rounds. With one running campaign on the account the rule is exactly `MAX(0, MIN(limit − live, idle))`, as before. A campaign whose dials are slow to place gets a slightly smaller share: while its tick is still dialling it is not in the split, so lines freed meanwhile go to its sibling (modelled at about 4.5 against 5.3 of 10 lines, at 1.5 s against 50 ms per dial; never above the limit). Weights and a per-campaign cap set by the supervisor were weighed and left for later: equal share needs no new setting, and a cap can be added as a nullable column without changing the rule | **default, pending Manas** |
| MC2 | Where the split is decided | In the pacing round (`pacing/round.ts`), which already reads each account's limit and live calls once per round. **Members:** every campaign a scheduled round starts joins its account's split before any tick runs (a skipped, quiescent campaign is not a member). It joins NOT contending when it is drain-only (`stopping`) or its last full tick found it not running; before its first full tick it contends. The account is contended when two or more members contend (a member that reports demand counts as contending). Each tick states its demand (available agents with work, bounded under contention by the contacts actually due and by MC5's cap) and its own live calls, or drops out (a tick that fails or returns early withdraws in `finally`). **The split:** once every member has reported or withdrawn, the account's headroom is split by MC1 among those that reported. If some have not reported 1 s (`PACING_SHARE_WAIT_MS`) after the account's FIRST report, the split runs among those that have, and stays OPEN for the rest. **One open split per account:** a split opens when the account's first member joins in a round and closes when every member has reported or withdrawn, or at its deadline, `max(PACING_SPLIT_DEADLINE_MS` (5 s), the wait): a split open that long is closed at the start of the next round with the warning log "Pacing split open past its deadline — closing it". While an account has an open split, a round starts no tick for its campaigns; the first round after the close starts them. So a fast campaign's next ticks wait until its slow sibling reports. **Late reporters:** a member reporting after the split ran but while it is still open gets `min(demand, leftover)` at once, first come first served (not by fewest live calls), and puts it in the ledger, so a consistently slow sibling is served from the leftover rather than starved. A report after the split CLOSED gets 0: the tick is held back by the share (not quiescent, idle `no_slots`, counted in `agency_pacing_share_withheld_total` when `min(demand, headroom) > 0`) and takes part in the next split. **Pending dials:** a tick's dials can outlive its 250 ms round (each attempt is inserted, then the carrier is awaited before the next), so the engine keeps, per account, a hold per tick on the dials its split granted that are not yet attempt rows (the pending-dial ledger, `PacingEngine.pendingDials`, a set of holds per account): the grant goes in right after the report, each inserted attempt row takes one out, and the rest is released as soon as the tick knows it will insert fewer (checked per pair) and at the latest when the tick ends. Each round's account live read is `countLiveForAccount` plus the ledger, the ledger snapshotted before the query, so a row inserted in between is counted twice (a one-round under-dial, never an over-dial); the ticks' recorded `occupied` includes it. A hold with no progress (no grant or insert) for `PACING_DIAL_HOLD_MAX_MS` (60 s, about twice the worst healthy gap between inserts: VoiceLink login 10 s plus `add_lead` 15 s, each with its own timeout) stops counting against the account, with one warning per hold ("A pacing tick has held granted dials with no progress past the bound — no longer counting them against the account"); the hung tick's own `finally` still releases it. The cost: should that tick recover, the account can briefly exceed its limit by what it still inserts, and the account concurrency guard refuses the excess. **Why nothing is granted twice:** no two splits of an account are open at once; every grant (the split's, and each leftover grant while open) is handed out synchronously and goes into the ledger in the microtasks right after, before any later round can read, and a closed split grants nothing more, so every later read includes every grant already made. A campaign alone on its account is unaffected: its own holds are gone before its next tick, and its grant is `MAX(0, MIN(limit − live, idle))`. **Exempt:** a forced `tickOnce` (its own round, no production caller) neither waits for nor holds back a scheduled split; racing one it could grant the same headroom twice, and the account concurrency guard refuses the excess. No Redis state: on this single-replica deployment one engine leads every campaign of an account (MC3 for two processes) | **default, pending Manas** |
| MC3 | Two processes leading one account | Leadership stays per campaign (Redis lease, 15 s). While two processes lead campaigns of one account (a leader failover, or a deploy overlap; an old leader learns it lost a lease only at its next renewal), each splits only among its own campaigns, and each splits the headroom it reads from the database, so the account's live attempt ROWS can exceed `max_concurrent_calls`. Calls are still bounded: the account concurrency guard refuses the excess at bridge admission. This is the same exposure one campaign already has in split-brain (two processes leading it). The one open split per account and the pending-dial ledger (MC2) and the per-account claim lock (MC4) are per process too. An account-level lease or a Redis reservation of the account's slots would close it; neither is built, and whether to is open with Manas (§5). It ends when only one process leads the account's campaigns | **default, pending Manas** |
| MC4 | One live call per number, across campaigns | The claim's busy probe (`numberLiveSql`) is widened from the campaign to the account: a number `in_flight` or `connected` on any campaign of the account is not claimed (a retry child and its parent, or two rosters sharing a number, never ring one customer twice). Claims for campaigns of one account run one at a time in the engine (a per-account claim lock), so the read probe is enough against this process's other pacing ticks, as it was within one campaign. The agent's own dial of one of their contacts (`my-contact-call.ts`, `claimForAgentDial`) uses the same account-wide probe in its claim. The soft phone's tagged dial checks the account too: the number `in_flight` on any campaign of the account refuses `contact_in_flight`, naming that campaign's contact, as an untagged dial already did; `in_flight` only, so a number `connected` elsewhere is not refused (`softphone.md` §4.1). Backed by a partial index on the account's live contacts by number. What remains is §5's: the soft phone's checks and the agent's own dial run outside the engine's claim lock, so each can race a pacing claim of another row of the number by milliseconds | **default, pending Manas** |
| MC5 | Quiescence and claim shortfall | A tick held back by MC1 while the account had headroom is not quiescent: it ticks every round until it is served, so a campaign capped by the share cannot sleep through slots another campaign leaves idle. So is a tick that reports after its account's split closed (MC2). **Claim shortfall** (`demandCap`): after a contended tick claims `c` contacts of a grant `g` with `c < g`, that campaign's demand in later contended rounds is capped at `max(1, c)`, so slots it was granted and could not fill go to the others. The cap lifts once it claims its whole grant, on an uncontended tick, on a tick that finds it not dialling (paused, stopping), and when this process gives up its leadership; a tick that reserved no agents leaves it as it was. The floor of 1 keeps it probing, so it recovers once its contacts become claimable, at a cost of at most one slot a round: while an owner-mode campaign's due contacts stay busy on a sibling campaign, that is one line idled every round, which on a 2-line account is half its lines. It covers what demand cannot see: an owner-mode campaign's demand counts its agents with work due, not numbers busy on the account's other campaigns, and a `pool` campaign's count of due contacts is read before its claim, which can then find fewer (a number live elsewhere on the account, a contact taken in between) | **default, pending Manas** |
| MC6 | Schema and API | Migration `0015` drops `uq_agency_campaign_running`; Start and Resume no longer answer 409 `another_campaign_running` (the code is retired from `packages/contracts`). Agent exclusivity is unchanged: an agent is live on one campaign at a time (the session-join conflict check, `uq_agency_agent_live_tenant`; across tenants since OS1, `uq_agency_agent_live_agent`). Contact claims are per campaign (a tick claims only its own roster), but the number busy check behind them spans the account (MC4); DNC, retries and contact ownership stay per campaign | **default, pending Manas** |
| MC7 | Visibility | Campaign stats carry `concurrency`: the campaign's live calls, its equal share of the account limit while other campaigns run (what each would get if every running campaign were busy: neither a cap nor a guarantee, and left out of the console's line when it rounds down to 0), the account limit and live calls, and how many campaigns are running; the console shows it on the campaign cards and the detail page.
