Metrics: `agency_campaign_slots_in_use` and `agency_campaign_slots_allocated` (gauges, per campaign), set only after a contended report to the split and cleared on every other outcome: no agent available, nothing due, a contended demand of 0 (`no_contacts`), a tick that did not plan, any uncontended tick, and when this process gives up its leadership; and `agency_pacing_share_withheld_total`, `min(demand, headroom) − grant` for every campaign whose demand exceeded what it got while the account had headroom, a report after the split closed (granted 0) counting its whole `min(demand, headroom)`. That counter measures contention, not starvation: under fewest-live-first a busy campaign is withheld routinely. The starvation signal is withheld while its slots in use read 0 (`agy-pacing-share-starved`). On the agency dashboard | **default, pending Manas** |

Account creation, 2026-11-05 (Manas: "Both super admin and tenant admin should be able to create
more accounts through UI"). Before this a tenant had exactly one account, the `Default` that tenant
creation writes; no route made another. The console's Accounts page (`/app/accounts`, now `/settings/accounts`, B28) and the
super-admin tenant page's "New account" button now do, through one write
(`api/routes/helpers/create-account.ts`). An independent review on the same day found that a new
account's default call capacity let a tenant admin add capacity without a super admin; Manas chose
zero capacity until a super admin allocates it (AA4).

| # | Topic | Default used | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| AA1 | Who | Tenant admins and owners (`account.create`, floor `tenant_admin`, the one account-administration permission in the contract) through `POST /accounts`, and super admins through `POST /super-admin/tenants/:id/accounts`. Not `account_admin`: that role runs one account. The console route also requires a tenant-wide membership: a `tenant_admin` seat scoped to one account is refused 403 `tenant_wide_membership_required`, because `GET /accounts` confines it to its own account and the account it made would vanish from its switcher. The console sends the create without `X-Account-Id` so the tenant-wide seat is the one checked. The console's sidebars and command palette link the Accounts page for `account.create` | **Decided** (who); **default, pending Manas** (the tenant-wide requirement) |
| AA2 | Name | Trimmed, 1–100 characters, no control or invisible format characters (`\p{Cc}`, `\p{Cf}`, line and paragraph separators: tabs, zero-width spaces, bidi overrides); unique among the tenant's live (not deleted) accounts ignoring case, Unicode compatibility forms and repeated spaces, else 409 `account_name_taken`. A deleted account's name and slug can be used again | **default, pending Manas** |
| AA3 | Slug and concurrent creates | The slug is the server's, never typed: the name with accents dropped, its ASCII letters and digits joined by dashes (40 characters at most, `account` when the name has none), with a random six-hex suffix when another live account holds it. The whole create is one transaction that first locks the tenant's row `FOR NO KEY UPDATE`, so creates in one tenant run one at a time: a double-submitted name ends as one account and one 409 even when the two would have picked different suffixes. The lock mode does not block the foreign-key checks of other inserts under the tenant. A deleted tenant is not found (404); a suspended one can still be given accounts, as it can still be given members | **default, pending Manas** |
| AA4 | Call capacity of a new account | An account a tenant admin creates gets an `account_settings` row with `max_concurrent_calls = 0`: it can be set up (campaigns, people) but places no call until a super admin sets its limit on the Service tab. Without it the account would read the platform default of 5, and with no tenant-wide cap a tenant admin could add capacity (and calls outside any provider breakdown) by adding accounts. An account a super admin creates writes no settings row and takes the default, since the same person sets limits. Everything else needs no row: recording and analysis default off, untagged numbers serve every account in the tenant, feature flags fall back to the tenant's. The console's Accounts page shows each account's call limit ("Not set yet" for zero). There is still no rename or delete (`account.update` / `account.delete`, Q3e) | **Decided** (Manas, 2026-11-05: zero until allocated) |
| AA5 | Audit | A tenant admin's create writes `account.created` to `platform_audit_log`, filed under the new account (`account_id`, `resource_type` `account`), so its own trail opens with its creation; a super admin's writes `create_account` to `super_admin_audit_log` | **default, pending Manas** |
| AA6 | Account order | `GET /accounts`, `/accounts/mine`, the session settings map and the super-admin list are oldest first (`created_at`, then `id`). The console selects the first account when it has no saved choice, so the tenant's original account stays where a fresh sign-in lands; newest first would move every such sign-in into whatever account was created last | **default, pending Manas** |
| AA7 | The new account's first person, and an invite's account (2026-11-12, review of the navigation redesign) | After New account, the Accounts page shows a panel ("<Name> is ready. Next, invite the person who'll run it. You stay in <current account> while you do.") with Email, Role (starting on Supervisor, `account_admin`) and the account shown, not chosen: the invite goes into the new account only; "Send invite" and "Later" (Team's invite button says "Send invite" too: the server emails every invite it can, and the hand-off says plainly when it could not). It shares Team's request, analytics and hand-off (`pages/team/inviteForm.tsx`). Team's invite form no longer defaults to a tenant-wide seat: every role an invite can carry is an account's role (the server takes `account_admin` and below), so the Account field starts empty and the invite cannot be created until an account, or "All accounts (whole organisation)", is picked; an account-scoped inviter is offered only their own account, filled in, as the server allows nothing else (`pages/team/inviteScope.ts`; the invite is sent without `X-Account-Id`, so the server holds an inviter with no tenant-wide row to their oldest account row: with exactly one account that one, with several the field offers nothing and says to ask an admin with access to the whole organisation, never every account). Neither invite closes while it is being sent (Team's Cancel, overlay, Escape and close button; the new account's "Later"): when the server could not email it, the hand-off is the only place the link is shown. The console calls `account_admin` "Supervisor" in lists and on the invite claim page and "Supervisor (account admin)" where a role is picked; the super-admin console keeps "Account admin". **Roles described, not merged** (Manas, 2026-11-12): wherever a role is picked (the invite's Role select and Change role) it carries one line saying what it can do (`ROLE_DESCRIPTIONS` in `pages/team/teamRoles.ts`), read from the contract's permission table and pinned to it by `teamRoles.test.ts`, and Team's guide says the same. Viewer and Operator are currently identical in the dialler and everywhere else: no permission floors at `operator`, and taking calls (`agency.station.connect`, the attempt permissions) floors at `agent`, so both read campaigns, results and the Do Not Call list, both can take calls and neither can change anything; the guide used to say only operators take calls. Operator's line says it is the same as Viewer; the two roles stay (no wire value changes) until one of them is given a permission of its own. The whole tenant is "the organisation" in user copy, never "workspace" or "tenant": the console (the organisation switcher, "Organisation ID" in the profile menu, the scope gate's "You can’t open this organisation", the Do Not Call list's "organisation-wide entry", the invite claim's "Organisation invitation", "Organisation admin" and "Organisation owner"), the agent app (the same sentences, and "Organisation" on the invite), the invite emails, the server's messages to signed-in people, the customer-history export's "Whole organisation" and the audit log's "Organisation" product filter. Wire values (`tenant`, `tenant_admin`, the `workspace` join door, `workspace_invite_*` events) keep their names, and the super-admin console, the operator's own tool, keeps "tenant". `organisationWording.test.ts` reads every console string for the old words | An owner inviting a new account's supervisor who forgot the optional Account field handed them every account in the organisation; the rest of the console already said supervisor. People picking a role could not tell what it allowed, and the guide promised a difference between Viewer and Operator that the permission table does not have | **Decided** (Manas, 2026-11-12, with the landing of viewers and operators in B28a) |

Customer history, 2026-11-06 (ClickUp "Customer history: search one phone number and see every
interaction across the tenant's campaigns"). One phone number searched across every campaign and
the soft phone in the accounts the caller can see, with its owners, DNC status, open items and one
timeline. Manas's five decisions on the task are CH1–CH5; CH6–CH14 are the build's own calls,
revised the same day after a review (DNC scoping, keyset paging per source, the server-served
country default). The full table, the API, the measured performance and the v2 key-column design
are in [`customer-history.md`](customer-history.md). Migrations `0016_customer_history_indexes.sql` and
`0017_customer_history_contacts_index.sql`;
wire `packages/contracts/src/api/agency/customer-history.ts`; number rule
`packages/domain/src/customer-search-number.ts`; hero fields `packages/domain/src/context-hero.ts`;
permission `agency.customer_history.read` (floored at `agent`).

| ID | Decision | Status |
|---|---|---|
| CH1 | Everyone can search: `agency.customer_history.read` floored at `agent` | **Decided** (Manas) |
| CH2 | Scoped by account inside the query; a tenant-level member sees every account; other accounts' rows are left out, never counted. A campaign DNC mark (stored with no account) belongs to its campaign's account; only an entry with neither an account nor a campaign is tenant-wide; a roster import belongs to its campaign's account | **Decided** (Manas) |
| CH3 | v1 by phone only; the request is `{ by, value }` and the page does not assume a phone key (v2 design in `customer-history.md` §6) | **Decided** (Manas) |
| CH4 | Full numbers only; never a suffix search. Validity is judged on length (and a leading 0), not on a mobile prefix | **Decided** (Manas) |
| CH5 | The box explains the input (country picker, helper text, live preview, refusals that say what to do); one rule for preview and server, one shared test table. The picker starts on the platform default, served by `GET /proxy/agency/customers/options` (`DEFAULT_PHONE_COUNTRY_CODE`), +91 until it answers | **Decided** (Manas) |
| CH6 | The number never in a URL or a log: `POST` with the number in the body, a 30-minute `search_id` bound to the tenant and the user for paging and export | **default, pending Manas** |
| CH7 | A retry chain is one contact row; household lines stay separate rows | **default, pending Manas** |
| CH8 | Notes and call fields need `agency.campaigns.read` (or the caller's own call); the call page needs `agency.supervise`; DNC who/why need `agency.dnc.read` in the account the entry belongs to (a tenant-wide entry: the caller's highest role) | **default, pending Manas** |
| CH9 | Every read on an index (0016 adds the soft-phone destination index, 0017 the roster index that walks a number's newest rows per account); each timeline page is one keyset query per source with the cursor and `LIMIT n + 1`; asserted with `EXPLAIN` on analysed bulk data with nothing disabled, and measured on a 1M-contact tenant (a search, summary and first page, 15 ms median for a tenant-level owner) | **default, pending Manas** |
| CH10 | Every successful search and export audited (`agency_customer_history.searched` / `.exported`, with the E.164, result count and account context), zero results included; refusals, timeline pages and the options read are not; best-effort like every platform audit row | **default, pending Manas** |
| CH11 | 30 searches and exports a minute per user (`AGENCY_CUSTOMER_SEARCH_PER_MINUTE`), its own bucket; the server's 429 says "You've searched a lot of numbers in a short time. Try again in N seconds." | **default, pending Manas** |
| CH12 | DNC removals are not shown (hard delete, the audit row has no number); the upload file is inferred from the import window; no contact is ever "removed from roster" (B15); the ingest keeps a bracketed trunk `(0)` the search drops, so such a row is not found (pinned `it.fails`) | (b)–(d) **default, pending Manas**; (a) removals and (e) the ingest trunk **open** |
| CH13 | The CSV export is the console's only in v1: the agent app has no file-sharing plugin, and adding one is a dependency change not made while the app could not be built | **default, pending Manas** |
| CH14 | The history covers a number's newest 2,000 roster rows (summary, totals, timeline, export and audit count alike), chosen by an index walk per visible account that stops at the cap, so a placeholder on 200,000 rows costs what 2,000 do; past it the clients say "This number is on more than 2,000 roster rows. This history covers the 2,000 most recent." Soft-phone calls dialled to the number are all still included | **default, pending Manas** |

Plivo on tenants' own accounts, 2026-11-07 (steps 3 and 4 of B18–B22; [`status.md`](status.md),
"Plivo, on tenants' own accounts"). The build's own calls, made where B18–B22 left a choice; none
has run against a real Plivo account yet (step 5). How a call flows: [`architecture.md`](architecture.md),
"The voice engine"; enabling it and what the pauses mean: [`operations.md`](operations.md),
"Enabling Plivo for a tenant".

| ID | Decision | Status |
|---|---|---|
| PL1 | A 403 on create-call is a plain refusal of that dial (Plivo's answer for a destination its geo permissions do not allow), not an auth failure: the call ends `telephony_init_failed` and nothing is paused. Only a 401 says the credential is refused. To confirm on a real account in step 5 | **default, pending Manas** |
| PL2 | A dial the carrier refuses with 401 pauses the campaign on the first one, pause reason `carrier_auth` (migration 0019), audited `agency_campaign.auto_paused` with that reason; the contact goes back at once, uncharged. The refusal also marks the credential `invalid` (pinned to the refused secret version, so a rotation in between is never marked), audited `telephony_credential.carrier_refused`; start and resume are refused until a super-admin rotates or verifies it back to `active`, then a supervisor resumes | **default, pending Manas** |
| PL3 | A dial refused because the pinned credential cannot dial (disabled, invalid or revoked since the campaign started; `telephony_credential_unavailable`, before the carrier) pauses the campaign on the first one, pause reason `carrier_account_unavailable` (`0020_carrier_account_unavailable_pause.sql`), audited the same way; the contact goes back at once, uncharged. Start and resume answer 409 `telephony_credential_not_active` while the credential is not `active`. A soft-phone dial refused this way (or by a carrier 401) answers `409 carrier_account_unavailable`, not the retryable `dial_failed` (copy: contact support, or use another caller ID), and pauses nothing **Addendum (review of PR #56):** a keyring fault is not the account: a credential that cannot be opened because this server has no keyring (`store_unconfigured`) or lacks the master key its secret is sealed under (`unopenable`), the kinds `isKeyringFaultKind` names and the recording fetch already treats as operator-fixable, is a bridge failure (`ByocCredentialUnavailableError.keyringFault`): the paced dial holds the contact a minute (`return_delayed`), pauses nothing and is not counted by the breaker (PL11), and the soft phone answers the retryable `dial_failed`. A deploy that dropped a key would otherwise auto-pause every running campaign on a tenant's account saying the account is inactive. Start and resume read only the credential's row (`campaignCredentialStartRefusal`, `campaignDialBlockers` open no secret), so a keyring fault never reads there as an inactive account | **default, pending Manas** |
| PL4 | A number synced from a tenant credential is offered as a caller ID (campaign caller IDs, the soft phone's list and defaults, the console's number list, the super-admin's routed-number count) only while that credential is `active`. The number row is untouched, so re-enabling the credential offers it again. A campaign pinned to a credential may use only that credential's active synced numbers | **default, pending Manas** |
| PL5 | Number sync is a super-admin action on an `active` credential (`POST /super-admin/telephony-credentials/:id/sync-numbers`, audited). Synced numbers are pinned to the credential and assigned to its tenant untagged, so every account of the tenant may use them; a number already registered elsewhere is reported as a conflict, never taken over; a number gone from the carrier account is retired, never deleted. A tenant-credential carrier's numbers cannot be added by hand, and a synced number is assignable only to its credential's tenant, enforced in the assignment insert itself (`tenantPhoneAssignmentRepository.assign`, `PhoneNumberOtherTenantError`) so every path is held to it **Addendum (review of PR #56):** one exception to "never taken over": a number pinned to a REVOKED credential of the same tenant, provider and carrier account (`account_ref`, Plivo's auth ID) is re-pinned to the syncing credential and made `active` (reported as `taken_over`; a deleted one stays a `deleted` conflict). That is the same account registered again after its old credential was revoked, and without it the account's numbers could never be synced again. Anything else stays a conflict: a disabled or invalid credential (one carrier account has one live registration, so such a credential is always another account's), another tenant's, another account's, or the service's own. Only the number moves: calls placed on the old credential keep it (`agency_calls.telephony_credential_id` is pinned at dial time), so their recordings are fetched on it until its crypto-shred (no call on it within the post-call hold), after which they cannot be fetched (B21, no fallback); the shred reads calls, not numbers, so a takeover neither delays nor hastens it | **default, pending Manas** |
| PL6 | Plivo recordings are fetched from `media.plivo.com` only, matched exactly, with HTTP Basic on the first request only; `api.plivo.com` is deliberately excluded (a forged `recording_url` there would turn a credentialed GET into a read of account data). For every carrier a redirect hop carries nothing but `Range`. A refused access is `404 recording_unavailable` on playback and a non-retryable analysis failure; a keyring fault is retried. **Amended 2026-11-18 (Manas asked for it after staging refused `aps1.media.plivo.com`):** an account may store its recordings on its regional media host, and Plivo gives that host as the recording URL itself (the first URL, not a redirect): from 2026-11-17 01:11 UTC staging failed 33 analysis jobs for good and refused playback of 4 calls on `aps1.media.plivo.com`. Plivo's list is now `media.plivo.com` and any subdomain of it (`aps1.media.plivo.com`, `usw1.media.plivo.com`), matched exact-or-subdomain on a dot boundary (`recordingHostMatches`; Plivo's access no longer sets `exactHosts`), on the first URL and on every redirect hop, with Basic auth on the first request only, as before. Still refused: `api.plivo.com`, `plivo.com`, `evilmedia.plivo.com`, `media.plivo.com.evil.com`, a host in the userinfo, path, query or fragment, a host with an empty label, and http; VoiceLink's rule is unchanged. Everything under `media.plivo.com` is Plivo's media zone, so the credential still reaches only Plivo's recording hosts. The jobs those refusals failed are re-queued by an operator script once this is deployed (`operations.md` "Re-analysing calls a recording-host refusal failed") | **default, pending Manas** |
| PL7 | New calls a second are limited per Plivo account, default 2 (Plivo's account default, `PLIVO_CALLS_PER_SECOND`), in a sliding 1000 ms window in Redis that every process shares (no 1000 ms span holds more). A dial waits up to `PLIVO_DIAL_RATE_MAX_WAIT_MS` (5 s) for a slot, then is refused unsent (`carrier_rate_limited`); Plivo's own 429 is the same refusal. It is back-pressure: the contact goes back at once, uncharged. A create-call that times out may have placed the call: never retried, its contact deferred 15 min as VoiceLink's. So may one that fails on the network after the request may have been written (a reset, a socket closed under the response): an unknown outcome, the same deferral, not a plain refusal. One whose connection failed before the request was written (DNS, the TCP connect, the TLS certificate) placed nothing: a dial not sent (`carrier_dial_not_sent`, uncharged) | **default, pending Manas** |
| PL8 | A teardown before the answer cancels the dial by its `request_uuid`; after the answer it hangs up by the `call_uuid`. Neither is ever sent with an empty or malformed id (an empty hangup id would address every call on the account). A call with neither its answer nor its terminal seen 30 s after the carrier's own ring timeout (`PLIVO_RING_TIMEOUT_SECONDS`, declared by the adapter as `capabilities.ringTimeoutSeconds`; a carrier that declares none, VoiceLink, keeps only its max duration) is ended here: the carrier is asked for the call's state when it has named the call, one it reports ended is settled as it ended with nothing sent (`ring_deadline_carrier_ended`), and anything else is ended `no_answer` (`ring_deadline_passed`) and cancelled or hung up as above | **default, pending Manas** |
| PL9 | A Plivo callback is accepted only with both the purpose-bound token in its URL and a V3 signature from the account the call was placed on (the current token, or the previous one inside the 24 h rotation grace), over the URL the dial handed Plivo: the full `PLIVO_WEBHOOK_BASE_URL` as written plus the route, the call id and the request's query (`plivoCallbackPublicUrl`, through the dial's own `carrierCallbackUrl`), never a `Host` header and never the path the request arrived on. Each signed nonce is accepted once per call (recorded in Redis past the webhook token's life; a repeat is refused as a replay; a callback that failed to process gives its nonce back; a Redis fault fails open, logged). A refused answer callback is answered with hangup XML. With media workers a callback runs in the worker its `w` names; a forward that fails or misses its 10 s deadline is hangup XML (answer) or 503, never a 200 for a post that was not processed | **default, pending Manas** |
| PL11 | A campaign pinned to a tenant carrier credential is paused `carrier_refusing_calls` (`0021_carrier_refusing_calls_pause.sql`) after 5 carrier refusals in a row with no call placed in between (`CARRIER_REFUSAL_BREAKER_THRESHOLD`, a constant, not configurable): the carrier refusing a dial with a 4xx other than 401 and 429 (`telephony_init_failed` with a known outcome whose adapter error carries the status, `CarrierDialRefusedError.httpStatus`: a 403 destination not enabled, no credit, a caller ID it rejects), or a dial not sent for the carrier's configuration (`carrier_dial_not_sent`, reason `configuration`: no webhook base, a callback URL off it, a malformed caller ID). A 401 and an inactive credential pause on the first one (PL2, PL3); a rate limit, an unknown outcome, the contact's own malformed number and the API unreachable do not count. Nor does a 5xx or a refusal that carries no status (addendum, review of PR #56): a 5xx is a fault on the carrier's platform, not the tenant's account, and counting it would pause every campaign on every tenant's account of that carrier through one outage; such a dial is charged to its contact as before (held a minute, then retried under the campaign's policy). Any placed call resets the count; a dial a teardown stopped before it was sent (at the calls-per-second gate, or while the bridge prepared it) is not a placed call and resets nothing: the bridge resolves it with no carrier handle on the record (`dialWasSent`, addendum, review of PR #56). The count is per campaign in Redis with a 15-minute TTL (every process shares it), counted in-process while Redis errors; the pause clears it. A placed call's clear (one `DEL`) is skipped when it cannot matter (addendum, review of PR #56): in one process (`MEDIA_WORKERS=0`), which dials every campaign and so sees every refusal, the breaker remembers per campaign whether a refusal has been noted since it last cleared, and clears on the first placed call after a start (a streak a previous process left); with media workers each worker dials its own agents' calls and counts into the shared key, so every placed call clears it A supervisor resumes the campaign once the account is fixed at the carrier; nothing about the credential blocks the resume. Audited `agency_campaign.auto_paused` (actor `system:carrier-refusals`); left out of the abandonment health's auto-pauses. Trade-off: the refusals before the fifth are charged to their contacts as carrier refusals (one `failed` attempt each, under the campaign's retry policy), because a single refusal cannot tell an account problem from a refusal about that contact; a dial not sent is never charged | **default, awaiting ratification** |
| PL12 | A carrier can dial from this server only when a carrier module answers to it and its webhook base is configured (`telephony/carrier-dial-readiness.ts`, decided per module and env, never by name; the bridge refuses every dial without one, `carrier_dial_not_sent`). While a tenant-account carrier cannot (`PLIVO_WEBHOOK_BASE_URL` unset), `GET /proxy/agency/carriers` offers none of its accounts, and start and resume of a campaign pinned to one answer 409 `carrier_not_ready` with a vendor-free message. The service's own carrier is not judged (its base is required at load in production); a campaign already running keeps dialling into refusals until the breaker pauses it (PL11) | **default, awaiting ratification** |
| PL10 | The console offers a campaign carrier choice only when the tenant has more than one carrier account, named by its alias plus the account's label, never a vendor name; with one, it uses that one. An untagged soft-phone call dials on the carrier account of the chosen caller ID's number; a tagged one on its campaign's. The carriers list (`GET /proxy/agency/carriers`, labels and ids only) needs `agency.campaigns.read`, not `.write`, so a viewer is shown which carrier a campaign dials on | **default, pending Manas** |
| PL13 | A campaign on a tenant's own carrier account dials only from its stored caller IDs that are still `active` on that credential and available to the campaign's account (the write rule), read at the dial (pacing, the agent's own dials, wrap-up redial, a tagged soft-phone call and its list) and cached 15 s per campaign, cleared by a number sync, retire, delete or unassign. A retired number stays in `caller_ids`: a save on the same carrier account judges only the caller IDs it adds, so a rename is never refused over one, and the console shows it as "No longer available" with Remove. With none left a running campaign dials nothing (health strip `no_dialable_caller_ids`) and start and resume answer 409 `campaign_caller_ids_unavailable`; no pause reason is written. The number sync lists the live campaigns still naming the credential's retired numbers. A service-account campaign is unchanged (no read). **With dedicated caller IDs (DN2, merged 2026-11-07):** the dialable pool is the pool DN2 decides over, on every dial path: a dedicated number is used only while it is dialable (a retired or revoked one falls back to the rotation), and the rotation is the dialable pool minus the dedicated numbers. One place per path: pacing hands `resolveCallerId` the tick's dialable pool, `DedicatedCallerIdCache.callerIdForContact` (the agent's own call, the wrap-up redial) reads `dialableCallerIds` itself, the tagged soft phone narrows the tag before `selectCallerId`. The supervisor's caller-ID routes see the same pool: the list shows only dialable numbers (a dedicated one retired since reads `in_campaign: false` and leaves the rotation), the PUT refuses a retired number with 409 `caller_id_not_in_campaign`, and My contacts stops naming it. Removing a retired number that is still dedicated is refused by DN6 like any other dedicated number (`caller_id_dedicated`, naming it; the settings page says to release it with "Use shared rotation" on the Agents tab first): the dedication is never dropped silently **The cache (addendum, review of PR #56):** a read that started before a clear stores nothing when it returns (`GenerationTtlCache`, per-key generations; a clear moves them all), so a retired number is never put back for the 15 s; the dial that read it uses it once, as it would with no cache With media workers each worker keeps its own cache (the agent's own call, the wrap-up redial and the tagged soft phone dial there): every clear made in the primary is sent to every worker over IPC (`dialable.invalidate`), so a retired number leaves every process's cache at once; a lost message costs at most the 15 s | **default, awaiting ratification** |
| PL14 | An account whose concurrency is split by carrier (`provider_breakdown`) and gives a campaign's carrier no allocation (no row, or 0) cannot dial on that carrier: start and resume of such a campaign answer 409 `carrier_capacity_unallocated` ("contact support to add capacity for this carrier"), a running one is named on the health strip (`no_carrier_capacity`), and the super-admin's tenant Service tab warns of every breakdown account with no allocation for a carrier the tenant has an active carrier account on. It applies to every carrier, the service's own included; an account on a single total is never affected | **default, awaiting ratification** |
| PL15 | Console copy about something only the service's operators can change (a carrier account's credentials, enabling or re-enabling it, which numbers are synced or assigned to the tenant) tells the operator to **contact support**, B17's phrase, never "ask your administrator": a tenant's admins have no screen that shows or fixes a carrier account. The server's carrier refusals say the same. Copy about what a tenant admin can change (team, invites, account capabilities) keeps naming the administrator | **default, awaiting ratification** |
| PL16 | Carrier names in the super-admin console: the alias, then the vendor in parentheses, "Pravah (Plivo)", on every screen that names a carrier (credential column and picker, phone numbers and their picker, tenant numbers, the concurrency tables), through `operatorCarrierName` (`apps/super-admin/src/utils/carrierName.ts`). Operators need the vendor to find an account's Auth ID or a number in the vendor's own console, and the alias first keeps the two consoles saying the same name. The vendor is named plainly only where an operator acts in the vendor's tooling: a credential field's help ("Plivo console → Overview"), its format errors and the carrier's own refusal in `last_error`. Our own copy (placeholders, sentences) names no vendor. The tenant console, the mobile app and server messages that reach them show the alias alone. Guarded by `apps/super-admin/src/__tests__/branding/carrierNames.test.ts` | **default, awaiting ratification** |

Reports, 2026-11-08 (agent, campaign and account exports as PDF and Excel, ad hoc or on a
schedule; [`reports.md`](reports.md)). The design was reviewed the same day by an independent
reviewer and revised (the server assembles the data, the renderer never reads the database). The
full table, with each ruling's wording, is RP1–RP16 in [`reports.md`](reports.md) "Decisions".
Migration `0023_reports.sql`; wire
`packages/contracts/src/api/agency/reports.ts` and `packages/contracts/src/reports/`; renderer
`apps/report-renderer`; flag `agency_reports_enabled`.

| ID | Decision | Status |
|---|---|---|
| RP1 | The server assembles each report's data with the reads the screens use; the renderer (a Lambda, or in process) never reads the database | **default, pending Manas** |
| RP2 | A window is at most 30 days, or exactly one local calendar month, for every kind | **default, pending Manas** |
| RP3 | Campaign reports in the campaign's zone; agent and account reports in the zone the request names (no account zone setting yet) | **default, pending Manas** |
| RP4 | Scheduled recipients are tenant members with `agency.supervise`, re-checked when each report is sent | **default, pending Manas** |
| RP5 | An agent may export their own review, without floor comparisons, call analysis or recording columns; subscriptions are supervisors' only | **default, pending Manas** |
| RP6 | The email carries a link to the console, no attachments | **default, pending Manas** |
| RP7 | Phone numbers in detail sheets masked to the last four digits in an agent's own copy | **default, pending Manas** |
| RP8 | Files kept 90 days, datasets 7 days (S3 lifecycle); the run is then shown as expired | **default, pending Manas** |
| RP9 | Rates are floored as each source floors them (agent 20, campaign 25) until the open "Rate floors" item is settled | **default, pending Manas** |
| RP10–RP16 | Build calls: reports inside the agency area (B4); occupancy against the floor is the roster's; 100,000-row detail sheets; the attempt-fenced render callback and the tick's reconcile; one run per subscription period; the renderer as its own app; ad hoc emails only on request | build |

Inbound calls, 2026-11-09 (designed, reviewed by an independent reviewer and revised the same day;
Manas accepted all eight recommendations the same day). **P1 and P2 are built** (answering every
call and following it up; callers put through to agents on console stations, one process), reviewed
independently (engineering, product, QA) and fixed in two rounds; **P3's mobile app frames are
built** (2026-11-10: callers put through to a phone whose station declares `caps=inbound`, the
app's station and its Missed calls, reviewed twice); **Mode B** is built on the server and
Android (2026-11-12: an away agent's phone rung for a caller; iOS designed only, nothing run on a
phone); **P0** (the live Plivo checks, needs a Plivo
account), the rest of **P3** (pickups from a break and the soft phone, the media-worker handover,
inbound in reports, Insights and customer history, voicemail analysis, "press 1 for a callback")
and **P4** are not built. The design, the build plan and its status are [`inbound.md`](inbound.md)
(§12). Migrations `0024`–`0030` (Mode B `0036`); flags `agency_inbound_enabled` and Mode B's `agency_inbound_ring_enabled`. The design decisions are IB1–IB8
below; the build's rulings are IQ1–IQ24, the fix pass's decisions IQ25–IQ45 and the mobile app's
IQ46–IQ47 and the pilot rulings IQ48–IQ57 (next four tables), and Mode B's rulings IQ58–IQ74 (IQ75–IQ77 from its second review, IQ78 from its refactor, IQ79–IQ82 from the app's fixes)
with the plan review's amendments N1–N5 (ringing an away agent's phone, after the one-station
table). The inbound rulings carry the `IQ` prefix so they never
collide with this file's own Q1–Q9 (§4 above).

| ID | Decision | Status |
|---|---|---|
| IB1 | Available agents are auto-connected with a distinct chime, as for outbound; a Take call prompt only for pickups from break or the soft phone (P3) | **Decided** |
| IB2 | Sticky routing: `loose` by default, 30 s wait, 30-day lookback; signals owner, last call from the number rung, last agent spoken to; last dialled (unanswered) off. `strict` / `off` per route | **Decided** |
| IB3 | When nobody answers, a callback follow-up by default; voicemail optional per route | **Decided** |
| IB4 | An inbound call reopens a contact whose retries are exhausted, as an inbound callback; a terminal outcome is never reopened | **Decided** |
| IB5 | Live inbound calls count in the account's live total (outbound pacing shrinks); never refused for the account limit below the route's own cap | **Decided** |
| IB6 | Only super-admins link numbers to inbound (it writes the tenant's Plivo account) | **Decided** |
| IB7 | A recording notice plays by default whenever the route records, before the bridge | **Decided** |
| IB8 | Connecting agents (P2) runs single-process; cross-worker handover is P3, gated on the live Plivo check | **Decided** |

Inbound P2 build rulings, 2026-11-09 (the P2 plan's open questions, decided as recommended; IQ24
added at the build). Code comments cite them as `IQ<n>`. A ruling a later decision changed says so;
the fix pass's decisions are the next table.

| ID | Topic | Ruling | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| IQ1 | Taking a sticky agent from wrap-up | At the wrap-up's end, through the release hook (`releaseAgent` → CAS `wrapup → reserved`), never mid write-up, so pacing never sees an `available` instant and WR5 holds. The station heartbeat renews a `wrapup` lease this process vouches for (P2 review fix) | build |
| IQ2 | What `inbound_waiting` subtracts | From the idle-agent side (candidates), not from `limit − occupied`, which already counts the waiting call (IB5's third arm). Held back per campaign: an over-hold across several campaigns is accepted, bounded by `n`. As built, a `strict` caller whose sticky agent is not away and a caller promised by a hand-over hold no team agent back | build |
| IQ3 | The reservation's shape | A separate Redis `inbound` field on the agent hash (with its guard) and a separate `inbound_call` frame, not a variant of the attempt-keyed `reserved` frame | build |
| IQ4 | Connect timing | Server-timed: the bind at `connects_at`, 1.5 s after the offer frame (`AGENCY_INBOUND_CONNECT_DELAY_MS`), no client acknowledgement; the bind refuses a socket that is not OPEN | build |
| IQ5 | Preamble before the search | The greeting and the recording notice play whole, then the caller queues and the search starts; no agent is bound during the preamble (`onPreambleDone`). **Amended by IQ29:** a caller nobody could take is decided at the answer, before any greeting | build; amended |
| IQ6 | `strict` with the sticky agent away | An agent offline, on a break, on the phone app or on another account sends the caller to the team at once, under `strict` too; `strict` holds only while the agent is working (on a call or finishing one). **Amended by IQ46:** "on the phone app" is now an app build whose station did not declare `caps=inbound`; a phone that did is a console's equal. **Amended by IQ63 (Mode B):** a sticky agent who is away and ringable (`away_ring`) is rung for one window first, then the team takes over, under `strict` too | build; amended |
| IQ7 | The in-flight clash on a carrier that cannot cancel ringing | VoiceLink: nothing is cancelled and the caller queues normally | build |
| IQ8 | The clash's bookkeeping | The superseded dial's attempt `canceled` with the reserved system code `inbound_call`, its contact back to `pending` uncharged, released with reason `inbound_call` (a contract literal) | build |
| IQ9 | The matched contact | No claim: the busy probe's inbound arm covers live and unsettled calls. Settled forward only (SP6): an `exhausted` contact is reopened only by a callback outcome, a success closes it, and a pending inbound callback is cleared when the call connected. **Amended by IQ32:** a lapsed or supervisor-closed outcome settles it too | build; amended |
| IQ10 | Catalog for a matched campaign that is completed or stopped | Still the campaign's, as the soft phone's `call_campaign` is | build |
| IQ11 | Inbound wrap-up | The soft phone's rule: an outcome always owed, no auto-return, a 30-minute lapse to `no_disposition`. **Replaced by IQ32** (the matched campaign's auto-return rule) | **Superseded** (IQ32) |
| IQ12 | Gating | Agents are connected only with `agency_inbound_enabled` on for the call's account (staged rollout), else P1's answer; the finish routes are never gated. **Amended by IQ36:** the flag off is the kill switch (the closed message) | build; amended |
| IQ13 | Media workers | With `MEDIA_WORKERS > 0` P2 keeps P1's answer (nobody is connected); placing calls on a worker is P3. **Amended by IQ38:** no longer a single log line but a deployment invariant, made loud | build; amended |
| IQ14 | Voicemail | Recorded from our stream after the prompt and a generated 400 ms beep (not TTS), at most 120 s, a WAV in the bucket on the call-data retention; analysis of voicemails in P3; `AGENCY_INBOUND_AVAILABLE_ACTIONS` widened to `voicemail`. The prompt was the action's own clip (no new slot). **Amended by IQ25 and IQ27:** a slot with no recording plays our standard voicemail prompt; a withheld or DNC caller hears the neutral one | build; amended |
| IQ15 | Recording | Plivo's Record API at the bind (its callback shapes unverified until P0, so the reader accepts both); the notice in the preamble; the route's `record_calls` and summary profile apply, not the matched campaign's; `allow_recording` still caps it | build |
| IQ16 | Maximum duration | The answer's stream timeout and the slot TTL are the parked budget plus the maximum duration; the bridge's timer is re-armed at the bind | build |
| IQ17 | A station lost after the bind | The 8 s deferred hangup as outbound, then the call ends `answered` with the outcome owed; a caller who has spoken to an agent is never requeued | build |
| IQ18 | Shutdown with callers live | Outcome `failed` (our side), Missed reason `failed`, with the follow-up and callback. **Amended by IQ35:** the startup sweep writes `failed` too (it kept `abandoned_in_queue`) | build; amended |
| IQ19 | The soft phone and a live inbound number | A number whose customer is on a live, or bridged and unsettled, inbound call to the account is refused; built as `customer_on_inbound_call` (not `contact_in_flight`: an unmatched caller has no contact to name). The number stays busy while the outcome is owed (inbound.md §6) | build |
| IQ20 | Mobile agents | Excluded from the pool and never sent the new frames; the Dart wire types are P3. **Amended by IQ43:** enforced where frames are sent. **Amended by IQ46:** a phone whose station declares `caps=inbound` is a console's equal; every other phone stays excluded | build; amended |
| IQ21 | Which account's agents | The call's account is the matched contact's (it can differ from the route's); the pool is that account's live campaign sessions, and `pool_campaign_ids` applies only when the two accounts are the same | build |
| IQ22 | Call fields | Notes, callback and outcome only; the soft phone's call fields come later | build |
| IQ23 | The supervisor's queue tile | Polled every 5 s on the agency home and the Missed calls page, plus the floor's marker; no push over a socket | build |
| IQ24 | An unset hold slot | The built-in generated hold tone (no TTS), never silence. Kept by IQ25, which gives every other unset message a recorded voice | build |

Inbound fix pass, 2026-11-09, two rounds (the independent engineering, product and QA reviews of P1
and P2, then a review of the first round; Manas approved fixing every finding and approved the
built-in recordings and the upload). Code and docs cite the findings each change closes:
`SWE-M1`, `SWE-m*` and `SWE-n*` (engineering, first review), `PM-M*` (product), `QA-F*` (QA),
`SWE-F*` / `SWE-N*` and `PM-N*` (the reviews of round 1). The row that names a finding is where it
is recorded; where it is described: [`inbound.md`](inbound.md), section in brackets.

| ID | Decision | Amends | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| IQ25 | **Standard recordings.** Seven recorded messages in British English: closed, busy, call-back, recording notice, please hold, voicemail prompt and the neutral voicemail prompt (IQ27). Synthesised offline with Piper, voice `en_GB-southern_english_female-low` (dataset CC BY-SA 4.0, OpenSLR 83; attribution in `apps/server/assets/inbound/README.md`), committed as WAVs under `apps/server/assets/inbound/` and shipped in the image, drop-in replaceable by recordings of the same names; no live TTS (sibling to launch decision 4). The words are one contract constant, `AGENCY_INBOUND_STANDARD_WORDS`, shared with the carrier's `<Speak>` (§3.4). PM-M1, PM-M2 | IQ14 (prompt), IQ24 (kept for hold) | **Decided** (Manas: built-in recordings); listening and licence sign-off open (§5) |
| IQ26 | **No inbound caller is put down in silence.** Where the route has no recording, the queue's exit (the call-back message), the voicemail prompt, the recording notice (whenever the call records and the notice switch is on: IB7 honoured) and "please hold" (no greeting) play our standard recording; a message no stream will carry is spoken by the carrier with the same words. The silent overflow, its log line and its route warning are gone (§3.4, §6). PM-M1, PM-M2 | P1's silent overflow | **default, pending Manas** |
| IQ27 | **Withheld and DNC callers are never promised a callback.** In opening hours (nobody free, at the answer or from the queue) they hear the busy words, never "we're closed" (`messageSlotFor`); a `closed` plan keeps the closed words and over capacity is busy for everyone. Their voicemail prompt is always the neutral recording ("Please leave your name, your number and a short message after the tone."), so they can always leave one (§3.4, §7). PM-N1 | round 1's "DNC callers hear the closed words" | **default, pending Manas** |
| IQ28 | **Inbound recordings.** Account admins (`agency.inbound.write`) upload them from the route editor: WAV, MP3 or OGG, at most 5 MB and 120 s, decoded whole before anything is stored, stored per account (`inbound-audio/<tenant>/<account>/…` and an `audio_files` row), audited; previewable (each recording and each standard one has a Play, `Cache-Control: private, no-store`); deletable only while no live route or active announcement uses it (`inbound_clip_in_use`, naming the routes). An upload naming no account is refused `400 account_scope_required` before anything is read or stored; without audio storage it is refused 503 and the editor says so (§8.4). PM-M1, SWE-N5 | — | **Decided** (Manas: an upload); details **default, pending Manas** |
| IQ29 | **A caller nobody in their pool could take gets the route's overflow action at once.** "Could take": a console station (**amended by IQ46:** or a phone whose station declared `caps=inbound`) on a campaign in the pool (the route's pool for a caller of the route's account, IQ21), whose agent is an active member who sees the call's account, in `available`, `reserved`, `on_call` or `wrapup`; or a station closed less than 15 s ago (`INBOUND_STATION_REJOIN_GRACE_MS`) whose agent was then working or available. Decided at the answer, before the greeting (no "please hold", nothing recorded), and asked again while they wait whenever presence changes and when a rejoin grace ends; written `overflow`, counted `agency_inbound_overflows_total{reason="no_agents"}`; the queue tile shows `no_agents_available`. Membership is re-read at every search, so an agent removed or deactivated while a caller waits is never offered them (§5.1, §6). PM-M6, SWE-N4, SWE-F1, QA-F5, QA-F10 **Amended by IQ50:** a `device_away` pause less than 30 s old also counts as could take. **Amended by IQ68 (Mode B):** a ringable agent (`away_ring`) counts as could take too, except one this caller has already declined or missed. | IQ5; the design's "hold to the maximum wait" (§5.1) | **default, pending Manas** |
| IQ30 | **An agent's hang-up of an offered, unbound call is a decline.** The offer is cancelled as a lost station's: the caller back at the head of the queue (`queued_at` kept), never offered to that session again, the agent back in the pool, nothing recorded as abandoned (no Missed row, no callback); with nobody else who could take them, IQ29. A bind the bridge refuses for any reason but the call ending (`isCallEnding`) is handled the same way. Past the maximum wait the agent is released and answered first, then the exit runs on the call's chain (§6). QA-F3, SWE-F3, SWE-m4 | — | **default, pending Manas** |
| IQ31 | **Settling an inbound call never lifts an agent off a break or out of offline.** The outcome save, the lapse and the supervisor's close return the agent only from the state the call holds them in (`wrapup`, or `reserved` / `on_call` naming the call); an agent on a break or offline stays there. The connector's `session_busy` give-back parks the agent on `break`, re-claimed for the owed call, also when the spine cannot be read (§6). SWE-M1 | — | **default, pending Manas** |
| IQ32 | **Inbound wrap-up follows the matched campaign's auto-return rule** (`wrapup_auto_return`, `wrapup_seconds`, as `wrapup-manager.ts`); an unmatched call takes 120 s with auto-return (`INBOUND_DEFAULT_WRAPUP`; there is no account-level setting); the 30-minute lapse (`AGENCY_INBOUND_OUTCOME_LAPSE_MS`) is only the backstop; the lapse sweep runs every 5 s. A lapsed or supervisor-closed outcome (`no_disposition`) settles the matched contact as a `none` outcome: the inbound callback is cleared (the customer was reached) and a contact that callback reopened from `exhausted` (`callback_reopened`, 0029) goes back to `exhausted` (§6, §9.2). SWE-F2 **Window replaced by IQ52:** the route's wrap-up window (30 s by default) for every inbound call. | **Replaces IQ11**; amends IQ9 | **default, pending Manas** |
| IQ33 | **Supervisor force-available on an agent owing an inbound outcome** closes it as `no_disposition` (the contact settled as IQ32, the claim cleared, the panel told), returns the agent and is audited `agency_inbound_call.closed` ("Inbound call closed by a supervisor", `details.via: force_available`); refused `409 inbound_call_live` while the call is offered or connected. The console's "Close and make available", for an agent on a break too (§6, §8.4). SWE-N6 | force-available's `409 inbound_call_live` refusal while an outcome is owed (§9.1) | **default, pending Manas** |
| IQ34 | **The inbound webhooks' nonce guard fails closed.** When Redis cannot answer, the answer and hangup URLs answer 503, so Plivo answers the caller from the Application's fallback answer URL (and retries the hangup); no captured callback can be replayed through a Redis outage (§3.4). Outbound Plivo callbacks keep PL9's fail-open. QA-F11 | — | **default, pending Manas** |
| IQ35 | **Calls nobody took, and the sweeps.** A call the startup sweep finds unfinished is `failed` (we dropped it; a bridged one `answered`, its outcome owed), still followed up. A hangup that lands before the answer has written the row is remembered 30 s (`INBOUND_EARLY_HANGUP_MS`) under the credential and tenant that verified it, and the answer ends the call at once as the caller who left (an open route's caller `abandoned_in_queue`, a closed or busy reason kept), answering `<Hangup/>`: no stream, no slot held. The periodic stale sweep (every 5 min) ends only calls whose `COALESCE(bridged_at, arrived_at)` is older than the longest parked budget plus the longest call plus 10 min, so it never ends a live call (§3.4, §10). QA-F9, QA-F4, SWE-m1, SWE-N1 | IQ18 (startup sweep) | **default, pending Manas** |
| IQ36 | **The kill switch.** `agency_inbound_enabled` off for the call's account: the carrier speaks the closed message (no queue, stream or voicemail, whatever the route's actions; it wins over `max_concurrent`), with a `closed` Missed row (`closed_cause = 'switched_off'`). The Missed list, its voicemails, Mark handled, Reopen, reassign, the badge's count and the summary stay available, and the console keeps Missed calls under a notice that callers hear the closed message. A flag read that fails counts as on (§8.1, §8.4). SWE-F4, SWE-F5, PM-N2 | IQ12 | **default, pending Manas** |
| IQ37 | **A credential disable keeps every number link, route attachment and the Application.** While disabled its calls are answered with the closed message and a Missed row; enabling it again restores answering with nothing to rebuild. Revoke stays refused while inbound is enabled (§8.2, §10) | **Replaces** the disable cascade (audit `cause = 'credential_disabled'` on older rows) | **default, pending Manas** |
| IQ38 | **`MEDIA_WORKERS=0` is a deployment invariant while any credential has inbound enabled**, and loud: the `agency_inbound_connect_available` gauge, a boot warning, the super-admin credential section's copy, the route editor's `connect_unavailable` warning and the paging alert `agy-inbound-connect-unavailable` (`operations.md`, "Deployment invariants" 8). The fallback answer URL (`PLIVO_INBOUND_FALLBACK_ANSWER_URL`) is hosted off the deployment and speaks the standard busy words in `en-GB`; a production boot with inbound enabled and none set warns (§3.2, §3.3). PM-M5, PM-M3 | IQ13 | **default, pending Manas** |
| IQ39 | **Monitoring.** The Application health check runs every 15 minutes (and at boot) over the credentials with inbound enabled, counted in `agency_inbound_applications{health}`; the `inbound` alert group (nine `agy-inbound-*` rules, thresholds sized for a pilot) and the dashboard's *Inbound calls* row; runbooks and thresholds in `operations.md`, "Alert runbooks" (§8.1). PM-M4 | — | **default, pending Manas** |
| IQ40 | **Usage.** Inbound calls with an account are counted apart from the dials (windowed on `arrived_at`): calls, "Picked up by the service" (our answer took the call), "Put through to an agent", carrier time (the carrier's `Duration` from its hangup callback, `carrier_seconds`, 0029, when known, else answer to end), hold time (queued to bridged, else to the end), talk time. The dial columns are unchanged (§9). SWE-F8 | — | **default, pending Manas** |
| IQ41 | **Missed rows.** A row closes as `reached` on an outbound call's person-reached outcome (a human connect or conversation written up with any code but `voicemail`, `no_disposition` and `redialed`) or on a later inbound call's bridge, the number matched by its digits, in the transaction that records it (0028, 0030). **Reopen** takes Mark handled's rules, is audited (`agency_inbound_call.reopened`), and a reopen racing a reached close leaves the row open. Supervisors reassign open rows to an active member who can work the account's rows (or the account), audited `agency_inbound_call.reassigned`; a departed assignee reads "Former member". The nav entry carries the open count as a badge (§7, §8.4) | 0028's close at the bridge | **default, pending Manas** |
| IQ42 | **Why a call was answered closed, and the summary.** `closed_cause` (`hours`, `switched_off`, `no_route`, `credential_inactive`; 0030), stamped at arrival. The inbound summary's metric definitions are inbound.md §11 (open-hours calls the denominator of every rate; time to answer from arrival, the greeting included). "Nobody free to answer" is the `overflow` outcome everywhere (the maximum wait, a full queue, or nobody in the pool could take calls); "All lines busy" (`overflow_no_capacity`) is its own line (§11). SWE-N7 | — | **default, pending Manas** |
| IQ43 | **Station frames and the bridge seam.** The console-only frames (`AGENCY_STATION_CONSOLE_ONLY_EVENTS`: `inbound_call`, `customer_waiting`) are dropped for a mobile station in one place (**amended by IQ46:** renamed `AGENCY_STATION_INBOUND_EVENTS`, dropped for a station that cannot take inbound calls), `StationRegistry.send`, whoever asks. The bridge seam gains `isCallEnding`, so a refused bind is told apart from a call ending (IQ30; `docs/seams.md` §3.1) | IQ20 | **default, pending Manas**; amended by IQ46 |
| IQ44 | **The route editor.** A new route opens Monday to Friday 09:00–17:30 in the browser's zone (the account has none), editable, the zone named beside the hours; advisory warnings (never refusals) for no opening hours, a recording with the notice switched off and a deployment that cannot connect agents; "Wait for their agent" says what `strict` does (IQ6) (§8.4) | — | **default, pending Manas** |
| IQ45 | **Before a route exists.** The super-admin Link confirmation says callers hear the closed message until an account route answers the number; the numbers list badges a linked number no route answers ("Inbound · no route"); a number's Inbound panel counts its open Missed rows that reached no account (count and latest), since no console screen lists them (§8.1, §8.3). PM-M7 | — | **default, pending Manas** |

Inbound calls on the mobile app, 2026-11-10 (P3's mobile app frames: IQ46 the server side, IQ47
the app side; [`inbound.md`](inbound.md) §4.3, §5.1, §5.2, §5.4, §6 "Mobile stations";
[`mobile.md`](mobile.md) "Inbound calls", "Missed calls"):

| ID | Decision | Amends | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| IQ46 | **The inbound capability on the station socket.** The station upgrade takes `caps=` (contracts `AGENCY_STATION_CAPABILITIES`, today `inbound`): comma-separated, a repeated parameter the union, an unknown value ignored, never refused (a 1008 is terminal for the app). A console station can always take inbound calls; a `client=mobile` station only with `caps=inbound`, so every older app build stays out of the pool and is never sent the frames. The capability belongs to the live socket (`StationRegistry`'s `canTakeInbound`, set at every attach), not the session row; it replaces every mobile exclusion (eligibility's `no_inbound_capability`, the release hook, the in-flight clash's hand-over, the could-take count and the queue tile, the send filter, now `AGENCY_STATION_INBOUND_EVENTS`). A station that cannot take inbound calls reattaching to a session holding one: an unbound offer cancelled as a lost station's (back at the head of the queue) and the agent returned to the pool; a bound call never re-adopted onto it, ended by the 8 s deferred hangup `answered` with its outcome owed (IQ17), the agent owing it in `wrapup`. A phone silent on a live inbound call is dropped as on a campaign call (M1). The device-away break queues behind an inbound reservation, call and wrap-up as behind a campaign call, and applies before the release hook can take a waiting caller | IQ6, IQ20, IQ29, IQ43 | **Decided** (Manas, 2026-11-10) |
| IQ47 | **The mobile app's inbound defaults.** The app connects with `caps=inbound`. An offer is shown on the station with a chime once per offer (call id and `offered_at`, Me → Sounds); the outcome pad shows once the call ends (the mobile campaign convention; the console allows it during the call); iOS reports the call to CallKit as an outgoing call, as campaign calls are. Telemetry: `agency_inbound_call_offered`, `agency_inbound_call_connected`, `agency_inbound_call_ended`, `agency_inbound_outcome_saved`, `agency_inbound_outcome_refused` (ids, codes and flags only; the console has no inbound events). Missed calls: a third segment of My calls, the agent's own rows only, the badge "100+" past the cap, the number shown (the row carries no caller name), no date filter, no voicemail playback ("Listen on the web console"); Call back fills the soft phone's pad and never dials ([`mobile.md`](mobile.md) "Inbound calls", "Missed calls") **Amended by IQ48, IQ53, IQ54, IQ57** (Missed calls on duty, the caller's name, the badge). | IQ20 | **Decided** (Manas, 2026-11-10) |

Inbound pilot rulings, 2026-11-11 (from the persona review of the mobile app's inbound calls,
[`status.md`](status.md) "Inbound calls on the mobile app: persona review 2026-11-11"; Manas took
the review's recommendations except where a row says otherwise):

| ID | Decision | Amends | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| IQ48 | **Missed calls on duty.** The station's waiting and wrap-up screens say "N customers to call back" (the agent's own open Missed rows) with a one-tap way to the list, and the Missed list opens while the agent is on duty on a campaign (My calls' other segments stay off duty only). Call back from it switches the campaign to the soft phone (SP7) in one step, with the number on the pad; it still never dials | IQ47 | **Decided** (Manas, 2026-11-11) |
| IQ49 | **Callers nobody owns.** An answered inbound call is a sticky signal: the agent who took the caller's latest answered inbound call inside the lookback is their agent next time, as for an outbound call. The account's unassigned Missed rows (an unmatched or withheld caller nobody answered) are shown to agents as a "Team" view any agent who sees the account can open; an agent claims a row, which assigns it to them (only while it is unassigned) and moves it to their own list | IQ20; `inbound.md` §4.1, §7 | **Decided** (Manas, 2026-11-11) |
| IQ50 | **A phone that leaves the app while available.** The agent is returned to the pool automatically when they come back within 30 s of the `device_away` pause (a longer absence still needs the tap). A `device_away` pause less than 30 s old counts as "could take" a caller, as a station in its rejoin grace does, so callers queue for them instead of overflowing at once. On return the station says how many customers rang while they were away (their own Missed rows since the pause), with the way to the list | IQ29 | **Decided** (Manas, 2026-11-11) |
| IQ51 | **A cellular call while available pauses the agent** for the length of the call, and returns them when it ends (only a pause the call itself caused). A cellular call during a station call is unchanged (the audio interruption) | — | **Decided** (Manas, 2026-11-11) |
| IQ52 | **The inbound wrap-up window is the route's** (Manas: start at 30 and let the route editor change it): each inbound route has a wrap-up window, 30 s by default, set in the route editor; every inbound call on that route takes it, on a console or a phone, with auto-return. The 30-minute lapse stays the backstop | **Replaces IQ32's window** (the matched campaign's `wrapup_seconds`, 120 s unmatched) | **Decided** (Manas, 2026-11-11) |
| IQ53 | **Voicemail playback in the app** is the first follow-up after the pilot; until then the row says "Listen on the web console" | IQ47 | **Decided** (Manas, 2026-11-11) |
| IQ54 | **The caller's name on a Missed row:** the matched contact's name (`match_name`, null when the caller was not matched or withheld their number) is on the wire row, and the app and console show it above the number | IQ47 | **Decided** (Manas, 2026-11-11) |
| IQ55 | **Calling back a DNC caller from Missed:** a missed call from a number on Do Not Call is not consent; Call back keeps the soft phone's DNC note and override (SP1), and the row says so | — | **Decided** (Manas, 2026-11-11) |
| IQ56 | **Older app builds:** the supervisor floor marks an agent whose phone station did not declare `caps=inbound`: "App can't take inbound calls — update the app" (on an account with inbound calls on). When the pilot starts, `MOBILE_MIN_SUPPORTED_VERSION` is raised to the first release that declares it, so every older build blocks on "Update required" (the only message an installed older build can be sent) | IQ46 | **Decided** (Manas, 2026-11-11) |
| IQ57 | **A supervisor's Missed badge** counts what their list shows: the count route takes the list's `assignee` (`me`, and IQ49's unassigned view), and the app asks for its own rows | IQ47 | **Decided** (Manas, 2026-11-11) |

Findings fixed with no decision of their own: tests pinned or made deterministic (QA-F1's hangup
cross-check, QA-F2's racy frame reads, QA-F13's synchronous hold stop, SWE-F5's kill switch on a
voicemail route); module splits with no behaviour change (SWE-m6: `inbound/start.ts`,
`inbound-handover.ts`; SWE-m7; SWE-m8's per-call completion promise for Hang up; SWE-n1, n3, n5);
the voicemail fetched only from its own relative route and never cached (SWE-m2, QA-F12); and the
pre-existing flakes QA-F6–F8.

One station per agent, 2026-11-11 (Manas: "an agent should be able to stay online in only one
station, either on one desktop browser or in mobile", and the rule spans tenants). `0035_one_station_per_agent.sql`,
contracts `agency/station-device.ts`; [`mobile.md`](mobile.md) "One station per agent",
[`architecture.md`](architecture.md) "One station per agent":

| ID | Decision | Amends | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| OS1 | **One live session per agent across every tenant.** `uq_agency_agent_live_agent (agent_user_id) WHERE left_at IS NULL` is the join upsert's arbiter; its `DO UPDATE` also requires the same tenant, so a session in another tenant is never rehydrated or touched by the upsert. A campaign join or soft-phone open while the agent is live in another tenant answers 409 `session_in_other_tenant` naming that tenant only (`tenant_id`, `tenant_name`, best effort); nothing of its campaigns or accounts crosses (but see OS7). 0035 ends any duplicates when it runs: per agent, every live session but the newest heartbeat is left, its transition logged when it was not already offline | the per-tenant rule | **Decided** (Manas, 2026-11-11) |
| OS2 | **Clients on `session_in_other_tenant`:** the console's station page is `StationFatal` with the server's sentence (falls back to "You are live in another organisation…"), the soft-phone page shows the sentence; the app refuses the join with it. No cross-tenant leave or switch is offered: the agent leaves it there, or OS7 ends it when it is idle. **Amended 2026-11-20 (agent station redesign, AS15):** neither console page shows the server's sentence any more. `StationFatal` says `joinRefusedLine` (`utils/agency/stationCopy.ts`) with the refusal's `tenant_name` ("You are working in <name>. Finish work there first, then come back here.", "another account" without a name), and `/dialer/phone` says the same line in the station's frame (`openRefusedLine`; the soft-phone client keeps `tenant_name` on that refusal). The rule (no cross-tenant leave or switch) is unchanged | OS1 | **Decided** (Manas, 2026-11-11) |
| OS3 | **The device that holds the station.** A device is one console page load or one app install; it sends a `device_id` (8–64 of `[A-Za-z0-9_-]`, a 400 otherwise) on the join, the soft-phone open and the station-token mint, and the session keeps the holder (`station_device_id`, 0035). From another device without `take_over`, each is refused 409 `station_on_other_device` (`session_id`, the `client_kind` that last attached) and changes nothing; with `take_over` the device holds it, and its socket supersedes the old one (4409) on attach. One atomic statement claims, writing only on a change (`claimStationDevice`); the join and the soft-phone open also pre-check read-only, so a join refused by the pre-check writes nothing (one refused by the claim, a race, has already rehydrated the row's mirror). A claim and the mint after it are two steps: a reconnect that claimed just before another device's take-over can mint after it, and the take-over's first upgrade then fails 4401, re-mints as holder and supersedes it (self-healing, one flap) | — | **Decided** (Manas, 2026-11-11) |
| OS4 | **Only the agent's own actions take over.** The console's join and soft-phone open take over when the agent opens the page (a reload is a new device id), but not on a page the browser reloaded by itself (`document.wasDiscarded`): that join asks, and a refusal shows "Your station is open on another device or window" with "Use this window instead" (the soft-phone page: its Retry). The Switch remedy's look-up of the other campaign's session never takes over (refused: "open on another device. Leave it there, then join here."). Every socket reconnect mints without; a refused mint stops as a 4409 does (no retry, "Your station moved to another device", "Use this window instead" mints with `take_over`). A back/forward-cache restore never takes over. The app's "Go on duty", "Use this phone instead", "Use it on this phone" and an open it is asked for take over; its own resumes (the soft-phone session it hosted, a campaign station on the soft phone with an unsettled call) and its reconnect ladder never do, and a refused resume shows "open on another device" with "Use it on this phone". A take-over is spent by the one mint it arms, whatever that mint answers, on both clients | OS3 | **Decided** (Manas, 2026-11-11) |
| OS5 | **Device ids are opaque and client-declared**, never an identity or a security input: the session is still the authenticated agent's own (Q8), and the holder only decides which of the agent's own devices reconnects by itself. The console keeps its id in memory per page load (not `sessionStorage`, which a duplicated tab copies); the app keeps its id in the preferences store, so a restart is the same device | OS3 | **Decided** (Manas, 2026-11-11) |
| OS6 | **Older clients.** A console or app build that sends no `device_id` can still join or open the soft phone (the agent's own action), which takes the station and leaves no holder; its reconnects (token mints) are refused `station_on_other_device` while a newer device holds the station, so a stale console tab or an older app never takes it back on its own (it shows a reconnect failure, and a reload or rejoin is its way back). Raising `MOBILE_MIN_SUPPORTED_VERSION` to the first app release that sends `device_id` removes the older-phone case | OS3 | **Decided** (Manas, 2026-11-11) |
| OS7 | **An idle session in another tenant does not lock the agent out.** When a join or soft-phone open is refused by the agent's session in another tenant, and that session has no station attached in any process (`stationPresence` is `absent`; `unknown` keeps it), no lease beyond `offline` / `break`, and no live attempt, redial being set up, soft-phone call or inbound call, it is ended exactly as the agent's own Leave ends it (lease cleared, row left, station detached) and the join is tried once more (`endIdleSessionElsewhere`). A session forgotten in a tenant (a laptop closed without Leave, a membership since revoked, the dialler since switched off there) would otherwise block every other tenant with nothing able to end it. A working session is never ended from another tenant. As built for Mode B (default, pending Manas with IQ67), a session on `away_ring` (no socket by design) still counts as idle (the agent is at a station here now, and keeping the absence would lock them out for up to 4 h); its parked phone is sent `ring_off` (`taken_over`), and a ring in flight holds the session | OS1 | **Decided** (Manas, 2026-11-11) |
| OS8 | **A rollback stays possible.** 0035 keeps `uq_agency_agent_live_tenant` beside the new index, redundant: the server image before it names that index as its `ON CONFLICT` arbiter, and Postgres refuses an arbiter no index matches, so dropping it would turn an image-only rollback into a 500 on every join. A later migration drops it once no image that names it can be deployed. A same-tenant duplicate reports either index's name, and two joins of one agent racing in one tenant can trip it before the arbiter: the upsert treats that 23505 as its empty answer (read the blocker, retry or refuse), never a 500 (`load/session-join-races`) | OS1 | **Decided** (Manas, 2026-11-11) |
| OS9 | **A session forgotten offline is ended.** Before OS9 a session stayed open until the agent pressed Leave (or OS7 ended it from another tenant): an agent who closed the laptop, killed the tab or lost the network was written `offline` and never left, so Live floor showed a red "Connection lost" card whose clock counted up for days. The idle-session sweep (`agency/idle-session-sweep.ts`, every 5 min in the primary, `bootstrap/agency.ts`) ends a session that has been `offline` for 2 h (`IDLE_SESSION_END_AFTER_MS`) and whose row nothing has written for as long (`updated_at`), never one on Mode B's away-ring (`away_ring_since`: its reconcile ends that absence and tells the phone), and only with nothing in it, by OS7's rule: no station attached in any process (`unknown` keeps it), a lease `offline` or gone (never `ring`), no live attempt, redial being set up, soft-phone call or inbound call. With media workers the sweep runs in the primary, whose dialer and soft phone hold nothing, so the last four answer "none" there; a working session is protected by the station's close never writing `offline` while a call is live, by the ownership key and by the two hours. The row is left first, under its own guard (the same conditions, under the row lock: `leaveIdle`), so an agent who rejoined since the read keeps the session; then, as Leave, the lease cleared, Need help ended, the station detached and its detach time forgotten (`afterSessionLeft`, shared with `leaveSession`), each step on its own. When it went offline is the later of `state_since` and its last `→ offline` event (the startup reaper's `markAllOffline` writes the event and leaves `state_since`; `updated_at` covers that event failing to be written), and `left_at` is that moment, not the sweep's: the floor then reads "Not signed in today" for yesterday's forgotten session, never "Left at" the sweep's minute, and no transition is logged (`offline` → `offline`). A station that attaches in the instant between the sweep's presence read and the row's end can lose the session, the race Leave and OS7 already have; the client then says the session ended. The console stops on a re-mint answered `session_ended` or 404 as on a 4404 ("Your work was closed"), where it used to retry for ever; the app already did. The agent's next Take calls opens a new session. **Not covered:** a session left in a working state with no station (a join whose station never attached, a failed `offline` mirror write) is not `offline`, so the sweep does not end it; it shows "Connection lost" until the next restart's `markAllOffline` | OS1, OS7 | **Decided** (Manas, 2026-11-21: sweep and end it); the 2 h cutoff and leaving the working-state rows alone **default, pending Manas** |

Mode B: ring an away agent's phone for inbound calls, 2026-11-12 (opt-in; the build plan's design
decisions D1–D8 as IQ58–IQ65, the build's rulings as IQ66–IQ74, and the plan review's binding
amendments as N1–N5). Migration `0036_inbound_ring.sql`, contracts `agency/push-devices.ts` and the
ring parts of `agency/inbound.ts`, flag `agency_inbound_ring_enabled`, seam `seams/push-sender.ts`
([`seams.md`](seams.md) §3.6). How it works as built: [`inbound.md`](inbound.md) §6.1 "Ringing an
away phone (Mode B)" (the server) and [`mobile.md`](mobile.md) "Ringing while away (Mode B)" and
"The ring channel (Mode B)" (the app). Each row says what was built where it differs from the plan.
Server and Android are built; iOS is a design only (IQ64); nothing has run on a phone, FCM or APNs.
All are defaults awaiting Manas.

Mode B's code comments cite only the IDs of this file (IQ58–IQ83, N1–N5, OS1–OS8): the plan
review's own item names, which clashed with build decisions B2/B3 and the mobile app's M5, were
replaced in the review fixes (the station close's one-script decision and the return to `away_ring`
are IQ67, the take-over ending `away_ring` is OS3).

| ID | Decision | Amends | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| IQ58 | **(D1) Who can be rung.** A phone rings only while its agent is on duty in a campaign session, on the reserved break `away_ring` ("Away · rings for inbound", contracts `AWAY_RING_BREAK_REASON`, in `RESERVED_BREAK_REASONS` and `APP_PAUSE_BREAK_REASONS`). The app asks for it on leaving the app, with `POST /sessions/:id/break {reason: 'away_ring'}`, which runs the device-away rules (applied by CAS from `available`, queued behind a call or a wrap-up); `device_away ↔ away_ring` is a break-to-break switch, never from any other break. A supervisor's force-available on an agent on `away_ring` is refused `409 agent_away_ring` ("They're away from the app; their phone rings for inbound calls."); force-break and sign-off work as before | — | **default, pending Manas** (2026-11-12) |
| IQ59 | **(D2) The windows.** A ring lasts 20 s (`AGENCY_INBOUND_RING_MS`); from the tap on Answer to audio the app has a 15 s grace (`AGENCY_INBOUND_ACCEPT_GRACE_MS`), covering a cold start on mobile data. Contract constants only, never env keys (Android mirrors them by hand in `RingPush.kt`'s `RingWire`) | — | **Decided** (Manas, 2026-11-13) |
| IQ60 | **(D3) One ring at a time.** One ring per agent at a time, to the one device that owns the session (`station_device_id`, OS3), checked at ring time; each caller rings one phone at a time | — | **default, pending Manas** (2026-11-12) |
| IQ61 | **(D4) What a push carries.** `ring`: only `type`, `call_id`, `offer_token`, `ring_expires_at` and `session_id`, never a name or a number; `ring_cancel` the call, the session and a `reason`; `ring_off` the session and a `reason`; `test_ring` nothing else. The app fetches `GET /inbound/calls/:id` once it has accepted | — | **default, pending Manas** (2026-11-12) |
| IQ62 | **(D5) Misses.** Two misses in a row (`AGENCY_INBOUND_RING_MAX_MISSES`) move the agent `away_ring → device_away` (reason `ring_missed`), and the app says why. A miss is a ring that expired or one answered but not bound within the grace (`accept_lapsed`): the agent's own. A Decline or `busy_on_phone` is not a miss, and, as built after review, neither is a failed push (`push_failed`, the carrier's trouble): an unregistered token revokes the device and ends the absence (`revoked`); any other failure keeps the agent ringable for that caller, runs no new search and pauses all ringing for `INBOUND_PUSH_RETRY_MS` (10 s), so a push outage neither walks a caller through the pool nor costs anyone a miss. The count (`agency_agent_sessions.ring_misses`) resets when a ring binds and whenever the agent goes `available`. An agent at the maximum is never rung (the ring rule, IQ78), and any later ring's end that returns them to `away_ring` sends the owed `ring_missed` end (a ring another caller's search started before it landed) | — | **Decided** (Manas, 2026-11-13) |
| IQ63 | **(D6) The order per caller.** (1) the sticky agent on screen and available; (2) the sticky agent ringable, rung for one window; (3) team members on screen, longest idle first; (4) team members ringable, one phone at a time, longest idle first (the break's `since`, rewritten on every return to `away_ring`), only when nobody is on screen to try. Under `strict`, a sticky agent who is away and ringable is rung, then the team takes over (IQ6 already hands an away agent's caller to the team). A caller is never rung again by an agent who declined or missed them | IQ6 | **Decided** (Manas, 2026-11-13) |
| IQ64 | **(D7) Android first.** iOS stays in the contract only (`platform: 'ios'`, an APNs VoIP sender behind the seam) and a design section ([`mobile.md`](mobile.md) "iOS: the design (Mode B)"); no Swift until iOS compiles. As built on Android, the lock-screen ring is its own activity (`RingActivity`) that never starts Flutter, because opening the app attaches the station and an attach during a ring is an answer (N5); the notification's Answer and the lock-screen Answer start `MainActivity` as an activity start, never from a broadcast or a service | — | **default, pending Manas** (2026-11-12) |
| IQ65 | **(D8) The flag and the opt-in.** `agency_inbound_ring_enabled` (scopes global, tenant, account; default off; not client-exposed; also needs `agency_inbound_enabled`) gates it. It is toggled on the generic flag screens, then each agent opts in per device (`agency_push_devices.ring_enabled`, off by default).
