As built, `away_ring` is refused 409 in this order: `ring_not_allowed` (not a campaign session, a flag off, or the absence already past its maximum age), `no_push_device` (no row for the station's device, revoked, or no token for its platform), `ring_disabled` (not opted in), `push_unavailable` (no credentials for the platform); the app then pauses `device_away` | — | **Decided** (Manas, 2026-11-13) |
| IQ66 | **A ring is an offer.** `away_ring` is a reserved break; a ring is the existing `reserved` state with an inbound offer on channel `push`. The spine `state` and the connector's phase both stay `offered`, so every `offered` branch keeps working; only the push differences branch (no 1.5 s bind timer, a ring timer and an accept timer, the release target). On the wire: `offer_channel` (`station` / `push`), `ring_expires_at`, `accepted_at`; `connects_at` is null on every push offer. As built, Redis marks it twice on the agent hash: `ring = 1` while on `away_ring` (only with `state = break`; every other state write clears it), and `push = 1` beside `inbound` while the held call is a push offer, written by the ring's reservation in the same script, so the station close sees a push offer whose CAS landed after its own synchronous check. Every unanswered end returns the agent to `away_ring` (`releaseInboundToAwayRing`, only while that call holds the session), never Mode A's `releaseAgent`, unless an OPEN station is attached here | — | **default, pending Manas** (2026-11-12) |
| IQ67 | **The socket and the session while away.** Once `away_ring` lands the app parks its socket and stops its microphone service. Its lease (`state = break, ring = 1`) is the exception to "leases are liveness detectors": TTL'd to the time left to the maximum age (`AGENCY_AWAY_RING_MAX_MS`, 4 h, measured from `away_ring_since`) and never renewed by a heartbeat; a reconcile at boot and every 60 s re-seeds or ends it from the database, and a socket close keeps it. The server ends it `away_ring → device_away` (then `offline` with no socket here) on two misses (`ring_missed`), the device revoked or its token reported unregistered (`revoked`), opt-in off (the Me card's toggle, `opted_out`; the away notification's "Stop ringing" is not an opt-out, IQ79), the flag off (`flag_off`), the maximum age (`max_age`), or another device taking the station over (OS3, `taken_over`), each audited `agency_session.away_ring_ended` and each sending Android a `ring_off` push. As built, two ends send no `ring_off` and are not audited: the agent coming back to the app (an attach or a join over `break, ring = 1` ends it as `device_away`; the app drops its own notification) and the app asking `device_away` while on `away_ring` (the switch). `away_ring_since` is stamped once per absence and kept across the rings in it (a ring's `reserved` keeps it); a ring that binds clears it with the misses (`ringAnswered`: the agent is in the app, on the call), and every other state (`available`, `offline`, any other break) and leaving clear it. So an `away_ring` queued during an answered call's wrap-up and applied at its release starts a new absence, and the 4 h counts from there. A queued `away_ring` applies from `offline` too (the app parked its socket during the wrap-up), and over no key (that lease lapsed). Leaving the session from another device (`/leave`) and OS7's end of an idle session elsewhere send the parked phone `ring_off` (`taken_over`), not audited. **As built (third review round, 2026-11-13):** those two send `ring_off` `left` instead (a new reason in `AGENCY_RING_OFF_REASONS`, counted `left` in `agency_away_ring_ended_total`): neither is a take-over, and `taken_over` stays for OS3's. A reason-less `offline` (the station close or a release whose Redis write could not answer, a dead media worker's slot reap) keeps `away_ring_since`, as the startup reaper does, and the reconcile decides; the slot reap leaves a session on `away_ring` alone. The `device_away → away_ring` switch is decided from Redis's record of the break (`reason` on the agent hash), never the session row's, and the entry script takes `break` only with `reason = device_away` | — | **Decided** (Manas, 2026-11-13) |
| IQ68 | **A ringable agent could take a caller** for IQ29's at-once overflow (`couldTakeLater`), as does one ringing now for another caller, except agents this caller has already declined or missed | IQ29 | **default, pending Manas** (2026-11-12) |
| IQ69 | **A ring starts only with a window of wait left:** the caller must have at least one ring window before the maximum wait (checked at the search and again at the ring). A ring in flight runs to its end, so the worst overrun is the 15 s grace | — | **default, pending Manas** (2026-11-12) |
| IQ70 | **Never ring an agent on a cellular call** (IQ51): Android declines at once with `busy_on_phone` when Telecom does not permit an incoming call or the audio mode is a telephony one (ringtone, in call, call screening, call redirect), and when a ring arrives while the phone is answering an earlier ring or on its call; a test ring on such a phone is dropped. As built after review, while the app's own ring is ringing, Telecom's refusal and the ringtone mode are its own, so only a cellular call's audio mode counts as busy (`PhoneSignals.busy`); a replacing ring Telecom refuses rings by its notification alone, and `agent_audio`'s `OwnRing.live` keeps the app's ring from reading as a cellular call (the `phone_call` pause, interruptions). The server never rings a `phone_call` break (only `break` with `ring = 1` can be reserved for a ring) | — | **default, pending Manas** (2026-11-12) |
| IQ71 | **The ring routes.** `POST /proxy/agency/inbound/calls/:id/ring/answering` (stops the ring timer, stamps `accepted_at`, starts the grace) and `…/ring/decline {reason: declined \| busy_on_phone}` need only the offer token, so native code acts at the tap without Flutter: 128 random bits, bound to (call, session), compared in constant time, never logged, audited or persisted; an unknown, spent or expired token, a malformed id or a call this process does not hold is a uniform `404 ring_not_offered`, and both share a per-IP limit of 30 a minute. `answering` is idempotent while the grace runs. The token-only decline stays valid until `accept` (counted `declined`, not a miss); after `accept` it is refused `already_accepted` and the station's hang-up is the way out (N4). `…/ring/accept {offer_token, device_id}` needs the agent's Firebase Bearer and `agency.station.connect`; the ring must be the Bearer's agent's, and `device_id` must hold the session's station (the station claim, never a take-over); it answers the call and a station bootstrap (`station_ws_url`, `station_token_expires_at`). Accept is idempotent for the same agent and device until the call binds (a lost answer retried); `already_accepted` for another device or once bound; `ring_expired` for this call's last ring that ended unanswered. Audited: `agency_inbound_call.ring_accepted` (the signed-in agent) and `agency_inbound_call.ring_declined`, whose actor is the agent the ring was for (the token was theirs), ids only | — | **default, pending Manas** (2026-11-12) |
| IQ72 | **After the outcome** the server releases to `available` as today; the app asks for `away_ring` again while it is hidden, and a pause asked for during wrap-up queues `away_ring`, applied by the release with or without a socket (re-checked first; `device_away` when it can no longer apply). A release that leaves a campaign outcome owed (`releaseOwingOutcome`) applies a queued `away_ring` as `device_away`: the phone is never rung for a session that owes an outcome | — | **default, pending Manas** (2026-11-12) |
| IQ73 | **On screen first, even mid-ring.** A station agent who becomes available after a caller's phone ring began, while that ring has not reached `answering`, takes the caller: the ring is cancelled (`answered_elsewhere`, no miss) and the station agent offered the caller (N3). As built after review, the ring's start is read once per caller in a search and whether it may still be taken over is asked again per candidate; a ring that ended mid-search leaves the caller to be offered as any queued caller | — | **Decided** (Manas, 2026-11-13) |
| IQ74 | **Reports:** `away_ring` is break time (not staffed); a rung but unanswered caller who overflows lands in Missed, as today | — | **Decided for now** (Manas, 2026-11-13: to revisit later) |
| IQ75 | **A push failure is the device's or the service's** (`pushFailureScope`, `seams/push-sender.ts`). The device's, for good: a token the carrier refused (`unregistered`: FCM `UNREGISTERED`, APNs 410, `BadDeviceToken`, `Unregistered`, `ExpiredToken`; `invalid`: FCM `SENDER_ID_MISMATCH` or `INVALID_ARGUMENT` on `message.token`, APNs `DeviceTokenNotForTopic`) revokes the device and ends its absence `revoked`. The device's, for now: a `rate_limited` the sender scoped to one device (its own Retry-After hold, an APNs 429, which is per token). Either way that caller never rings that phone again and the search goes on to the next phone at once. The service's: `unavailable` (5xx, 401/403, a bare FCM 404, a timeout, a network error, a message refused as malformed), `unconfigured` and an FCM 429 (`QUOTA_EXCEEDED` names no scope, so it is taken as the project's): the agent stays ringable for that caller and all ringing pauses `INBOUND_PUSH_RETRY_MS`. A bare FCM 404 no longer revokes. A failed push is still never a miss. As built, the sender's own Retry-After hold is as wide as the scope of the 429 that set it, and a held send answers that scope (2026-11-13, amended): an APNs 429 holds that device alone, an FCM 429 every Android send (the service's; no request is made for any phone while it lasts). So during a project-wide `QUOTA_EXCEEDED` whose `Retry-After` outlives the 10 s pause, each phone tried after a pause pauses ringing again rather than being marked one that caller never rings again (before, each pause walked the caller through every held phone and a team all on Mode B overflowed early); the cost is that an FCM 429 that was in truth one device's quota holds every Android phone back, and re-pauses ringing each time one is tried, for as long as its `Retry-After` lasts | IQ62 | **default, pending Manas** (2026-11-13) |
| IQ76 | **One absence, one `since`.** `away_ring → device_away` is a break-to-break switch inside one absence, so every end keeps the absence's start (`away_ring_since`) as `since`: in Redis, in the station's `agent_state` frame (before `ready` on a return), in the device-away route's answer and as the mirror's `state_since` (the floor's "since" and IQ50's recent-pause read see one pause; time per state and per reason is read from the transition log, which records the switch at its own instant, so reports are unchanged). Only the `offline` arm (no socket) starts at now. The enter stamps one server instant on Redis, the mirror and the frame. Every `ring_off` carries `away_since` (that start) and is held 10 minutes at the carrier, not an hour; the app drops one older than the absence it shows. **As built (third review round, 2026-11-13):** the return keeps it only when its end happened; when it does not (Redis not answering, a ring first), the rehydrate answers Redis's state and `since` as they stand and does not tell the row the agent is back | IQ67, IQ61 | **default, pending Manas** (2026-11-13) |
| IQ77 | **The token-only ring routes limit per IP and ringing call**, 30 a minute each; every id this process is not ringing shares the IP's one bucket, which stays the flood cap. Phones behind one carrier NAT share an IP, and a decline refused 429 lapses into an expiry, a miss that was not the agent's | IQ71 | **default, pending Manas** (2026-11-13) |
| IQ78 | **One ring rule.** Who may be rung is decided in one place (`AwayRingService`: the flags, `ringAllowed`, and the session's and device's side, `ringBlocks`): a campaign session; its station's push device registered, not revoked, with a token, opted in, its platform configured; the absence within its maximum age; fewer than `AGENCY_INBOUND_RING_MAX_MISSES` misses. The `away_ring` entry, the search, the ring as it starts and the reconcile all apply it, so an agent admitted to `away_ring` is one the search rings, and one it no longer rings is ended. As built: an agent at the maximum misses is refused `away_ring` with the existing `ring_not_allowed` (no new wire code: the app falls back to `device_away` and shows nothing, having already said why the phone stopped) until a ring binds or they go `available`; the reconcile ends an absence at the maximum misses `ring_missed` (an owed end that was lost); the search no longer counts a session past its maximum age as ringable. The reconcile still ends nobody for a sender with no credentials for the platform (no `ring_off` reason says it; the absence runs to its maximum age) | IQ62, IQ65 | **default, pending Manas** (2026-11-13) |
| IQ79 | **"Stop ringing" is per absence.** The away notification's "Stop ringing" stops this phone ringing for this absence only, as the ask's "switches to `device_away`": it is not an opt-out (`opted_out` comes only from the Me card's toggle, which stays the permanent switch), and the server has no route of its own for it. Native (`MainActivity`, before Flutter hears of the launch) declines a ring ringing at the tap, and any ring arriving in the next ring window (`RingWire.STOP_RINGING_HOLD_MS`, 20 s), as `declined`, never a miss (a test ring is just dismissed), and keeps the decline window across a process start. Native keeps the tap for Dart (`peekStopRinging`), which clears it (`clearStopRinging`) once `device_away` applied; native drops a tap older than one ring window plus the accept grace (35 s) unread. The returning station keeps its socket parked and asks `POST /sessions/:id/break {reason: 'device_away'}` before it attaches, since an attach with `caps=inbound` during a ring is an answer (N5). The server declines a ring not yet answered on that ask (IQ83: `declined`, never a miss, the absence ended as `device_away` keeping its start, answered applied); a ring already answered keeps the pause queued behind the call, applied at its release; a pause still queued when a ring ends unbound is settled by that end, and the switch `away_ring → device_away` takes any left, so none is applied after the agent's next call. While the answer is `reserved` (an answered ring: the pause queued behind it) or still `break/away_ring`, or the request failed in a way worth retrying, the app asks again each second, up to 10 s from the tap being read (`stopRingingBound`); then the station attaches without `caps=inbound`. An attach without it during a ring, answered or not, ends the ring `cancelled` instead of answering it, never a miss, and is the agent's return: the absence ends as `device_away` keeping its start (IQ76), the `agent_state` frame before `ready`, both naming `device_away` (N5, as amended by IQ83). The app declares inbound again by attaching afresh once a frame says the agent is on a break other than `away_ring`, or available. Leaving and coming back during the wait does not reopen the socket or park for `away_ring` again. The notice "Ringing stopped. You're paused." and `moveTaskToBack` follow only once the pause is confirmed (the HTTP answer, or that frame); no IQ50 return to calls. Otherwise, within another 10 s, or on a cold start that did not apply: "Couldn't stop ringing. Open your station to pause.", and the app stays in front. Hidden again in that absence, the app asks `device_away`, not `away_ring`, until the agent is back in the pool (`available` with the app in front), and the paused notification reads "Ringing stopped. You're paused. Open the app to take calls again." On a cold start the answer coordinator does the same for the absence's session (`stopRingingWithoutStation`) | IQ67 | **default, pending Manas** (2026-11-13) |
| IQ80 | **A late `ring_off` is dropped**, by native (`RingStateMachine.acceptsRingOff`) and again by Dart on return (`ringOffEndsAbsence`), when it names another session than the absence the phone shows; when its `away_since` starts more than 2 s (`RING_OFF_TOLERANCE_MS`) before that absence's start; or when it arrives once the agent is back (the away notification taken down). Without `away_since` (an older server) the push's FCM send time decides: sent more than 2 s before the shown absence began, dropped. A phone that knows of no absence takes it. So a `ring_off` held at the carrier never ends the next absence | IQ76 | **default, pending Manas** (2026-11-13) |
| IQ81 | **The return banner needs a concrete reason.** The server ends every return from `away_ring` as `device_away`, so "Your phone stopped ringing for inbound calls: …" shows only on one of (`ringOffReasonOnReturn`, first that holds): the `ring_off` native took for this absence (IQ80); two or more misses (`expired`, `accept_lapsed`) heard while away; the device status read on return (the flag off, the device revoked, missing or its token unregistered, opted out, or the server unable to push); or an absence of 4 h or more. No reason, no banner. The 4 h is the app's own constant (`awayRingMaxAge`, `ring_rules.dart`), the server's `AGENCY_AWAY_RING_MAX_MS` default: it must track that setting, since a deployment that changes it makes the app's inference wrong | IQ62, IQ67 | **default, pending Manas** (2026-11-13) |
| IQ82 | **A 429 on the token-only ring routes is retried once, natively** (`RingRequests.retryDelayMs`), after the server's `Retry-After` (delta-seconds; 1 s when there is none) when that wait fits, else not at all (a `Retry-After` that does not fit is respected, never cut short). For `…/ring/decline`, the whole send stays inside its 8 s budget (`DECLINE_BUDGET_MS`, the broadcast receiver's limit) with at least 1 s left for the retry; for `…/ring/answering` (no budget, the accept grace running), only a `Retry-After` of 5 s or less (`MAX_RETRY_AFTER_MS`). No answer at all or a server error gets the same one retry after 1 s. A decline still refused lapses into an expiry, a miss (IQ77) | IQ77 | **default, pending Manas** (2026-11-13) |
| IQ83 | **`device_away` during a ring not yet answered declines it.** The app's `device_away` (the away notification's "Stop ringing", IQ79) while a push offer holds the session `reserved` and has not reached `answering` is the agent saying no to that ring. It ends through the ring's one end, on the call's serial chain (`AwayRingService.declineRingForDeviceAway` → `RingOffers.declineForDeviceAway`), as `declined`, never a miss (IQ62): `ring_cancel` to the phone, the caller back at the head of the queue, searched for again and never rung on this phone again. The absence then ends as `device_away` keeping its start (IQ76; no `ring_off`: the app asked; counted `returned`), and the request is answered applied. A ring already answered or accepted is the agent taking the call: the pause queues behind it, as behind any call, and applies at its release. A pause queued behind a ring that ends unbound is settled by that end (`AwayRingService.releaseInbound`: a queued `device_away` ends the absence as `device_away`, a queued `away_ring` is what the agent is back on), and the switch `away_ring → device_away` takes any left: before, the queued `device_away` survived the agent's `/available`, rode on every `ready`, and paused them after their next call. As built, it amends N5 too: an attach without `caps=inbound` during a ring (answered or not) ends it `cancelled` and is the agent's return, the absence ended as `device_away` keeping its start, not Mode A's return to the pool | IQ79, IQ62, N4, N5 | **default, pending Manas** (2026-11-13) |
| N1 | **The reconcile's key.** The reconcile reads every live session with `away_ring_since IS NOT NULL`, never `break_reason` (a ring's `reserved` nulls it). It skips a session whose ring is in flight (the connector holds a push offer, or Redis an `inbound` field), re-seeds a missing key with a create-if-absent script (never an unconditional set), restores a mirror the startup reaper rewrote, and ends an absence past its age, its flags or its device. An end that must happen during a ring goes through the connector's cancel on the call's serial chain (`RingOffers.cancelForAwayRingEnd`), never a direct state write; an answered ring runs to its end and the next pass ends the absence. A flag read that fails ends nobody's ringing | IQ67 | **default, pending Manas** (2026-11-12) |
| N2 | **The heartbeat never renews `away_ring`.** `heartbeatLeaseMs` answers null while `ring = 1`, so a station ping in the moment before the app parks its socket cannot cut the 4 h TTL to the 45 s `break` lease; and the renew script (`RENEW_SCRIPT`) itself refuses a key with `ring = 1`, so a heartbeat that read `break` (`device_away`) just before a switch to `away_ring` cannot either. A named catalog break never gains `ring = 1` | IQ67 | **default, pending Manas** (2026-11-12) |
| N3 | **IQ73's order.** The freed station agent is first reserved for the caller under the account's lock (the search's or the release hook's CAS); then, in one step of the call's serial chain, the ring is cancelled with no new search and the station agent offered the caller, or, if `answering` landed first, the station agent is given back. The `answering` handler runs on the same chain and re-checks that the ring is still unanswered with its timer live | IQ73 | **default, pending Manas** (2026-11-12) |
| N4 | **Decline after Answer.** The token-only decline is valid until `accept`; after it the agent's hang-up on the station applies. Either way it is `declined`, never a miss | IQ71 | **default, pending Manas** (2026-11-12) |
| N5 | **The app's station attaching mid-ring.** An attach during an accepted ring leaves the state `reserved` (no `ring` cleared, no reason rewritten, no `ring_off`) and binds once `ready` and the `inbound_call` frame are sent. An attach with `caps=inbound` during a ring not yet answered (the agent opened the app by its icon) is an implicit answer and accept: it binds at once and the phone is sent `ring_cancel`. An attach by a build without `caps=inbound` ends the ring `cancelled` and is the agent's return: the absence ends as `device_away` keeping its start, never Mode A's return to the pool (IQ83). An attach or a join over `break, ring = 1` (no ring) is the agent's return: `away_ring` ends as `device_away`. As built on the app, while a ring's accept is in flight a return to the app neither reopens the parked station socket nor lets it reconnect on a mint of its own, so the station attaches only with the accept's URL; `already_accepted` for a call the station already holds (or receives within 5 s) is the app's own attach having answered first, and the call goes on | IQ66, IQ67 | **default, pending Manas** (2026-11-12) |

## 5. Still open

| # | Question | Context | Owner |
|---|---|---|---|
| Q3 | Contract choices made during the build | (a) the session settings map is keyed by `account_id` across every account the caller's memberships reach; (b) there is no separate per-account dialer-analysis toggle: analysis is governed by `analyze_calls` alone; (c) usage counts carry seconds, windowed on `dialed_at`; (d) super-admin shapes for change role, revoke and add-to-account exist; (e) `tenant.update` / `account.update` are added only if a console page needs them (`account.create` was added on 2026-11-05 for the Accounts page, AA1) | Manas (ratify) |
| — | `supervisor_hold` | Declared in the wrap-up hold union, never produced by the server. Keep, remove or build a producer | Manas |
| — | Shutdown grace | The completion-notice drain is 30 s and Docker's default stop grace is 10 s. Implemented, pending ratification: `stop_grace_period: 45s` in `docker/docker-compose.prod.yml`; and `stop()` now awaits in-flight pacing ticks and owner sweeps, bounded at 5 s (`TICK_DRAIN_TIMEOUT_MS`), with every dial site re-checking before the carrier (Rulings of 2026-10-16/17, "Pacing shutdown"). 5 s plus the 30 s drain fits inside 45 s | Manas (ratify) |
| — | Rate floors | Campaign rates are withheld under 25 dials (`AGENCY_CAMPAIGN_MIN_RATE_DENOMINATOR`, applied by the console), agent rates under 20 (`AGENCY_ROSTER_MIN_RATE_DENOMINATOR`, the server's `rates_reportable`). Both are in the contracts, side by side. Should they be one number? | Manas |
| — | Display locale and rounding | Built, pending ratification: `en-IN` for dates and numbers on the analytics surfaces; live timers round down, aggregate durations round (Rulings of 2026-10-16/17). Other pages still use the reader's locale (`status.md` follow-ups) | Manas (ratify) |
| 1, 3–6 | Launch defaults | Built, pending ratification (§2) | Manas |
| — | Tidy-up defaults | Built, pending ratification: the **default, pending Manas** rows of the 2026-10-16/17 rulings (§4) | Manas (ratify) |
| — | Scaling defaults | Built, pending ratification: the **default, pending Manas** rows of the 2026-11-02 scaling rulings (§4) | Manas (ratify) |
| IQ58–IQ83, N1–N5 | Mode B: ringing an away agent's phone | Built on the server and Android (§4, the Mode B table). Decided by Manas on 2026-11-13: IQ59, IQ62, IQ63, IQ65, IQ67, IQ73, and IQ74 for now (to revisit). Still pending ratification: IQ58, IQ60, IQ61, IQ64, IQ66, IQ68–IQ72, the second review round's IQ75 (push failures by whose trouble they are; amends IQ62), IQ76 (one absence, one `since`; amends IQ67) and IQ77 (the ring routes' limit per call; amends IQ71), the refactor's IQ78 (one ring rule: refused and ended at the maximum misses; amends IQ62, IQ65), the app's IQ79 ("Stop ringing" per absence, through `device_away`, never an opt-out; amends IQ67), IQ80 (a late `ring_off` dropped; amends IQ76), IQ81 (the return banner only on a concrete reason, its 4 h tracking `AGENCY_AWAY_RING_MAX_MS`) IQ82 (a 429 on the token-only ring routes retried once; amends IQ77) and IQ83 (`device_away` during a ring not yet answered declines it, and an attach without `caps=inbound` during a ring ends the absence as `device_away`; amends IQ79, N5), N1–N5, and OS7's treatment of an `away_ring` session as idle. Before a pilot: a field test on Android phones (the Telecom answer from a headset or a car, Android 14/15, battery killers), the Play Console full-screen-intent declaration, FCM credentials; iOS waits on its first compile, an Apple account and an APNs key | Manas |
| — | Host size for 500 calls | 500 calls needs more than 2 vCPUs whatever the process layout ([`scaling.md`](scaling.md) §8.1); the infrastructure change was left out of the scaling work by decision | Manas |
| 2, 7, 8 | Launch open items | Existing numbers, launch style, domain / freeze / rollback window | Manas, at launch |
| 9 | Metering | Out of v1; design not started | Manas |
| B15 | Roster supersede | Three schema changes plus a replace lock, and clearing the owners of the contacts it retires (CA11); `AGENCY_ROSTER_REPLACE_ENABLED` stays off until then | Manas |
| — | Soft phone vs one live call per number across the account | Should a tagged soft-phone call to a number whose contact is `connected` (bridged, or in wrap-up), on the tag or on any campaign of the account, be refused, as `in_flight` is? And are the remaining races worth a per-number lock: a manual claim against a pacing claim on two rows of one number (the pre-dial's account-wide check is a read, not a lock), and the agent's own dial (`my-contact-call.ts`, `claimForAgentDial`), which runs outside the pacing engine's per-account claim lock, against a pacing claim on another campaign of the account? Each is a window of milliseconds (§4, "Roster upload, choosing the duplicate column"; MC4) | Manas |
| WR1, WR3, WR4, WR7–WR13 | Wrap-up redial defaults | Built, pending ratification (§4, "Wrap-up redial"). WR3 is the ask's open question: do redials use up the contact's `max_attempts`? Built as no | Manas (ratify) |
| CA2–CA10, CA13–CA15 | Contact assignment defaults | Built, pending ratification (§4, "Contact assignment"). CA5's strict-mode consequence: a callback that falls back waits for a supervisor | Manas (ratify) |
| DN1–DN10 | Dedicated caller ID per agent | Built, pending ratification (§4, "Dedicated caller ID per agent"): the owner, not the dialling agent, decides; the rotation excludes dedicated numbers unless all are; unstaff releases the number whatever `on_unstaff`; the PATCH refuses to drop a dedicated number | Manas (ratify) |
| — | Per-number concurrency under dedicated caller IDs | `phone_numbers.max_concurrent_calls` is not enforced on any dial path (the account's limits are, per provider where set), and a dedication concentrates traffic on one number: in `pool` mode several agents can dial one owner's contacts from that owner's number at once, a supervisor's soft-phone calls to the owner's contacts use it too, and the same number can be dedicated on several running campaigns. Enforce a per-number limit at dial time (and refuse or fall back to the rotation when it is reached), or accept the carrier's own limit? | Manas |
| — | Dedicated caller ID in the mobile app | The mobile soft phone sends the campaign's `caller_ids[0]` by default; since DN4's rule the server keeps a requested number only while it is in the rotation (or the contact's owner's), so this is harmless, but the app should omit `caller_id` by default and let the server pick. The app does not show the agent their dedicated number (the console's My contacts does, from the summary's `caller_id`) | follow-up |
| OD2–OD8 | Automatic outcomes for unanswered calls | Built, pending ratification (§4, "Automatic outcomes for unanswered calls"): the five mappable outcomes (switched off arrives as Busy), no suppress or terminal entry, stored on the attempt beside the write-up and never on the contact, stamped at settle and never rewritten, catalog edits that break a mapping refused, campaign dials only (not the soft phone or redials), inherited by retry campaigns. OD1 (label only) is decided | Manas (ratify) |
| — | Automatic outcomes: where else they show | Built as a label on the attempt only (OD4). Open: (a) should My contacts, the roster, the attempt and customer-history filters, retry selectors and Insights show or filter by it; (b) should the mobile app show it (agents call back from there too); (c) is a label like "Retry later" right on the attempt that exhausts the contact, or on an `invalid` number the retry engine always suppresses (a non-terminal "Wrong number" entry can be mapped there today; a terminal one is refused) | Manas |
| MC1–MC7 | Several running campaigns per account | Built, pending ratification (§4, "Several running campaigns per account"): fewest-live-calls-first fair share decided in the pacing round; one open split per account, run after a 1 s wait and closed at a 5 s deadline, late reporters taking the leftover while it is open; a pending-dial ledger across rounds with a 60 s bound on a stuck hold; a claim-shortfall cap; the number busy check widened to the account (pacing, the agent's own dial, and the soft phone's dials, `in_flight` only). MC3's two-process overlap is the next row | Manas (ratify) |
| MC3 | Several campaigns of one account under two leading processes | While two processes lead campaigns of one account (a leader failover, or a deploy overlap; an old leader learns it lost a lease only at its next renewal), each splits the account's database headroom among its own campaigns (the open split, the claim lock and the pending-dial ledger are per process), so live attempt rows can exceed `max_concurrent_calls`; the account concurrency guard refuses the excess calls at admission, the same exposure one campaign has in split-brain. Close it with an account-level lease or a Redis reservation of the account's slots, or accept it? | Manas |
| AC1–AC3 | Abandonment ceiling defaults | Built, pending ratification (§4, "Abandonment ceiling in the console"): the console range 0.5–10%, a warning but no role gate above 3% | Manas (ratify) |
| AG2–AG5 | Abandonment grace per campaign | Built, pending ratification (§4, "Abandonment grace"): whole seconds 1–5 beside the ceiling, default 3; each call judged by the grace stamped on its attempt when it was placed, so a change applies to new calls only; inherited by retries and held by a template's safety section; the dashboard draws the default and says so | Manas (ratify) |
| AA1 (tenant-wide seat), AA2, AA3, AA5, AA6 | Account creation defaults | Built, pending ratification (§4, "Account creation"): a tenant admin needs a tenant-wide seat; names unique in the tenant ignoring case; creates in one tenant serialised; accounts listed oldest first. AA4 (zero call capacity until a super admin allocates) is decided | Manas (ratify) |
| CH6–CH11, CH12 b–d, CH13, CH14 | Customer history defaults | Built, pending ratification (§4, "Customer history"): the 30-minute search id, the retry-chain grouping, what each role is shown, the audit coverage, 30 searches a minute per user, the inferred upload file, export in the console only, the history bounded to a number's newest 2,000 roster rows | Manas (ratify) |
| CH12 a, CH12 e | Customer history: what the schema cannot show | (a) Must DNC removals appear in a number's history? They would need the number kept on removal, today deliberately not recorded. (e) Should the roster ingest drop a bracketed trunk `(0)` as the soft phone and the search do? It changes what existing rosters dedupe on | Manas |
| PL1–PL16 | Plivo on tenants' own accounts | Built, pending ratification (§4, "Plivo on tenants' own accounts"), and unproven on a real Plivo account until step 5: the 403 reading (PL1), the two automatic pauses, the caller-ID and sync rules, the recording host, the calls-per-second default | Manas (ratify) |
| CR1–CR15 | Carrier account modes; VoiceLink on tenants' own accounts | Built, pending ratification (`carriers.md` §3; CR2, CR4, CR9, CR11, CR13 and CR15 decided 2026-11-25, answers in §6), and unproven on a real tenant VoiceLink account: the login check's refusal statuses (CR3), the shared token (CR4), numbers by hand (CR6), one VoiceLink allocation per account (CR13); whether every tenant's VoiceLink account is on our API host (CR2) | Manas (ratify) |
| — | Inbound: live Plivo checks (P0) | Application create and number link, answer/hangup parameters and timeout (the 4 s answer budget), the fallback answer URL, the Record API and its callback, moving a live streamed call to new XML, DTMF over the stream ([`inbound.md`](inbound.md) §12). P1 and P2 are built against the mock carrier; nothing Plivo-facing is verified. Needs a Plivo account, as PL step 5 does | Manas (account) |
| IQ25–IQ45 | Inbound fix-pass defaults | Built, pending ratification (§4, "Inbound fix pass"): the 15 s rejoin grace, the 120 s unmatched wrap-up, the 5 MB / 120 s upload limits, the busy words for withheld and DNC callers, the kill switch's closed message, the alert thresholds, the default route hours | Manas (ratify) |
| IQ25 | Inbound standard recordings: listening and licence | Nobody has listened to the seven recordings in a call, and they come from a "low" Piper voice. They are CC BY-SA 4.0 derivatives (OpenSLR 83): attribution is in `apps/server/assets/inbound/README.md` and ShareAlike applies to the files. Sign off both, or replace them with recordings of the same names | Manas |
| — | Inbound retention for a UK pilot | Voicemails and the inbound spine age out on `AGENCY_RETENTION_DAYS`, unset by default (rows kept). Decide the window under UK GDPR, and how one voicemail is deleted on request | Manas |
| LF1–LF39 | Live floor | LF2 (the currency), LF3 (customer names on the cards) and LF8 (Join call, whisper) open; LF4–LF7 decided 2026-11-19 and built (Phase 3); LF39 decided 2026-11-24 and built; LF1, LF9, LF10 and Phase 3's defaults LF11–LF38 built, pending ratification (§"Live floor (LF)") | Manas |
| AD1–AD2 | A call summary delayed by the AI service | Built, pending ratification (§"Summary delayed by the AI service (AD)"): every queued summary reads delayed while the AI vendor's newest answer is a refusal (the newest refusal of a queued job is newer than the newest completion), whichever tenant's | Manas (ratify) |
| TR1 | Truncated recordings: how often the same audio is transcribed | Built, pending ratification (§"Truncated recordings (TR)"): identical bytes are transcribed at most twice across attempts that each end `AUDIO_TOO_SHORT`, then taken as truly truncated (any other ending in between starts the count afresh: at worst 2 per attempt) | Manas (ratify) |
| AR1–AR7 | Archived campaigns | Built, pending ratification (§"Archived campaigns (AR)"): archiving is a flag beside the status, not a status; draft and stopped only (not completed); archived campaigns are left out of every campaign list, picker and search, a picker keeping one it already holds; `agency.campaigns.write`; only Start is refused on an archived campaign | Manas (ratify) |
| B17 | Internal header names | `x-mgkvc-*` names are on the in-process wire only; renaming them is a follow-up | lead |
| — | Gated items | Vendor setup; Playwright happy path (real Firebase sign-in); parity check against production data; dark pilot; a real VoiceLink call and a real recording analysed; launch | Manas |

## Rulings of 2026-11-15: navigation group labels and items (NV)

Reshma (ops lead): in the Settings hub the group headings (You, This account · <name>, People and
access) and in the campaign bar Monitor, Data and Set up sat too close in look to the clickable
entries beside them. Console navigation styling only: no label, id, route, gate or analytics
event changed.

| # | Question | Ruling | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| NV1 | How does a navigation group label differ from the items under it? | One look, one component: `components/common/NavSectionLabel` (and its `.module.css`) renders the Settings hub's group headings, the campaign bar's group labels and the search's result-group labels. It is 11px, bold, uppercase, tracked, in `--text-muted`, with a hairline from the words to the end of its group; no hover, focus or active state, `cursor: default`, not selectable; a heading (Settings), a span (campaign bar) or a presentation div (the search's listbox), never a link, button or tab stop. Its group names itself by it: the Settings lists and the campaign bar's `role="group"`s with `aria-labelledby` (the search's groups already did). Items never take the label's colour: tabs (the shared `common/TabBar.module.css`, which the campaign bar now composes, so section tabs change with it) moved from `--text-muted` to `--text-secondary`; Settings entries stay `--text-secondary`, gain a trailing chevron and an inset focus ring. Hosts set only spacing, so the label's text lines up with its items' text (`__tests__/components/navSectionLabel.test.ts` reads the CSS; `SettingsHub`, `CampaignTabs` and `GlobalSearch` tests the semantics) | default, pending Manas |
| NV2 | Where does the campaign bar's group label sit? | Above its tabs, not in the row beside them (in the row it read as one more tab), with its hairline spanning the group; the vertical divider between groups went, the hairlines and a gap carry the grouping. The labels now stay on a phone, riding over their groups as the bar scrolls sideways, since above the tabs they cost no width. Amends IN14's "a divider between them" and "on a phone ... the labels hide" | default, pending Manas |

## Rulings of 2026-11-15: recording honours "recording off" (RC)

From an ops report: one tenant's calls were recorded with recording off, another's recordings
often would not play ("We could not reach the service that stores this recording"). The
mechanism and the procedures are in [`operations.md`](operations.md) "Recordings a call did not
ask for" and "Recordings that will not play".

| # | Question | Ruling | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| RC1 | What happens to a recording a call did not ask for? | `agency_calls.recording_requested` (decided at the dial or the arrival: the campaign's, the soft phone's or the inbound route's `record_calls`, under the account's `allow_recording`) is the one source of truth. A carrier's recording URL for a call that did not ask is discarded at every bridge door (live callback, terminal event, ending confirmation, late post-teardown data, which reads the row's flag), logged at warn once per call (call id, provider, never the URL) and counted (`recording_discarded_unrequested_total{provider}`). `WebRtcCallRepository.update` stores a non-null `recording_url` only on a row that asked. Every read goes through one predicate (`packages/db/src/utils/recording-request.ts`: `honourRecordingRequest` for the repository's rows, `recordingUrlSql` / `hasRecordingSql` for SQL readers): call detail and playback (campaign, soft phone, signed URL), customer history's `recording`, Insights' recorded count, the agent workbook's Recorded column, the analysis queue and runner. A row from before RC1 holding such a link therefore reads "not recorded" until the retention purge clears the link (RC5) | Fix |
| RC2 | Tell VoiceLink not to record? | No, and it does not matter: whatever VoiceLink does with its recording, our platform never honours a recording callback for a call that did not ask for one, on any carrier. `add_lead` (`voicelink.types.ts`, `voicelink-carrier.fixture.json`) has no recording field and none is invented; VoiceLink records on its account default and RC1 discards what it sends at every door (live, terminal, late), counted in `recording_discarded_unrequested_total{provider}`. Nothing is asked of VoiceLink | **Decided** (Manas, 2026-11-15: discard regardless of the carrier) |
| RC3 | Why does a recording "not play"? | Every proxy failure the console reads as "could not reach" is logged with a bounded `reason` (`host_not_allowed`, `redirect_refused`, `too_many_redirects`, `upstream_unauthorized`, `upstream_not_found`, `upstream_status`, `timeout`, `network`, `access_error`; `access_refused` for the 404) and counted in `recording_fetch_failures_total{provider, surface, reason}` (`surface` playback or analysis). A redirect refusal logs the target host and the host that sent it, never a URL. The playback proxy now gives up after 20 s without response headers (504 `timeout`; it used to hang until the browser gave up); the body still streams untimed. The 20 s starts at the first request to the carrier: resolving the access before it is our database and keyring, so a stall there is not reported as the carrier's `timeout` (a failure there is `access_error`, on both surfaces) | **default, pending Manas** (the 20 s) |
| RC4 | Follow a Plivo recording's redirect? | Off by default: `PLIVO_RECORDING_REDIRECT_HOSTS` (exact hostnames, lowercased on load; one with a scheme, port, path, wildcard or IP address is refused at boot) lists hosts a redirect hop may go to, from `media.plivo.com` or from an earlier listed hop (at most 3 hops), never the first URL, and a hop never carries the credential. Empty follows none, as before. To be set only to a host a `redirect_refused` log named; whether Plivo redirects at all is unknown until Plivo step 5. **Amended 2026-11-18 (Manas, after staging refused `aps1.media.plivo.com`):** a hop from or to any Plivo media host (`media.plivo.com` or a subdomain of it, PL6 as amended) needs no listing: those hosts are on Plivo's own list, which every hop is checked against. The listed hosts stay exact and redirect-only; config still cannot add a first-URL host | **default, pending Manas** |
| RC5 | Rows from before RC1 that hold a link the call did not ask for | Got rid of. Every retention run, whatever its windows, clears `recording_url` and `recording_duration_seconds` on each `agency_calls` row whose `recording_requested` is false, in keyset batches picked `FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED` off the partial index `idx_agency_calls_recording_unrequested` (0046), so a run with nothing to clear reads nothing (`maintenance/retention-purge.ts`, `unrequested_recordings_cleared` on the report and in the Slack summary); the first run after this release clears the old links and later runs find none. Its failure is the step's own (`unrequested_recordings_error`, logged, a warning in Slack): the transcript and voicemail steps still run. Each cleared campaign call fires 0012's Insights trigger once (one dirty mark; the recompute counts as before), on that first run only. Until it runs, every read already treats such a link as not recorded (RC1), and `0043_recording_honours_request.sql` had Insights recount their buckets. Our link only: any audio a carrier kept is the carrier's (RC2) | **Decided** (Manas, 2026-11-15) |
| RC6 | Other ways "recording off" could still record | None found that RC1 does not close. A tagged soft-phone call takes the campaign's `record_calls` (SP rulings, `softphone.md` §4.1; by design). A retry campaign inherits its parent's `record_calls` unless overridden, under the same account gate. An inbound call records only when its route's `record_calls` and the account allow it and an agent is bound; a refused admission (`no_capacity`) takes the flag back on the spine, the leg row and the live session, so a URL the carrier still sends is discarded at the bridge's door and counted. A soft-phone call's own `recording_requested` takes the account's `allow_recording` at the dial, as its media leg does (the "Recording" badge and customer history read it). A campaign dial reads `record_calls` from the campaign at each pacing tick and each agent dial, not the session; the session's `record_calls` from the join only drives the agent's "Recording" badge, which can be stale after a mid-session toggle (follow-up, not changed) | No change |

## Rulings of 2026-11-15: Do Not Call scope (DS)

Reshma (ops lead): marking a contact DNC applied across the whole tenant; she asked for a
one-campaign option beside a separate every-campaign one. Where she marked it was never
established: that open question was closed with the defaults (`status.md`, "Manas's rulings on
the feedback branch"). Finding: the station (web and mobile), the soft phone and inbound already wrote one campaign
(or the call's account) unless someone chose wider; the **Do Not Call page's Add numbers** was
the one path that always wrote an every-campaign entry, with no choice offered (and for an
account admin it was refused outright). That is the likely source.

| ID | Question | Ruling | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| DS1 | How the Do Not Call page chooses a scope | Every add states one: **Every campaign**, **this account** (account scope is enforced at dial time, `pre-dial-gates.ts` passes the campaign's account) or **one campaign** (a picker of the current account's campaigns, any status). Nothing is preselected; Add waits for a choice. `POST /dnc` takes an optional `scope` (`tenant`, `account`, `campaign`) that must agree with `account_id` / `campaign_id` or the request is refused `400 invalid_dnc_scope`; absent, the ids decide as before (neither ⇒ every campaign) for an older caller. Bulk add is the same request (up to 1,000 numbers), so it gets the same choice | **default, pending Manas** (no preselection) |
| DS2 | One campaign: which, and how stored | One of the tenant's campaigns in an account the caller manages (any account for a member of the whole organisation); a campaign in another account or tenant, or no such campaign, is the same `404 campaign_not_found`, so the answer never says one exists. Completed and stopped campaigns are accepted (a block there holds if it runs again). Stored as the station's mark is, `account_id NULL, campaign_id <id>`, so the page and the station write one row per scope and a re-add from either is "already listed"; an `account_id` sent beside it must be the campaign's own and is not stored. The audit row is on the campaign's account, scope `campaign`, ids only | **default, pending Manas** |
| DS3 | Who may block a number in every campaign | `agency.dnc.manage` on a membership of the **whole organisation** (no account), on the page and, now, on the station's "Never call again (any campaign, forever)": an admin of one account could escalate from the station although the page refused them the same row. The station still offers them the campaign mark. The console hides the option when the membership the server will resolve is an account's (`mayManageEveryCampaignDnc`) | **default, pending Manas** (behaviour change for account-scoped admins on the station) |
| DS4 | A narrower add where a wider entry already blocks the number | Not written: the number is reported `already_covered` with the covering entry's scope ("4 already blocked in every campaign", "1 already blocked in every campaign of the North account"), so the list does not show a redundant row. A wider add over narrower entries is written beside them, and they stay (they carry who and why); removing the wider entry leaves them blocking. The station's mark does not check coverage (it writes its campaign row as before) | **default, pending Manas** |
| DS5 | What a removal says | The confirmation is titled with who may dial again ("Let every campaign…", "Let Q4 Renewals dial this number again?"), names the entry's scope, and sorts the other entries for the number the caller can see (read when the dialog opens; Remove waits for that answer) by where they reach (`blockOverlap`, `pages/agency/dnc/dncPageCopy.ts`): an entry that reaches everywhere the removed one did (every campaign covers all; an account entry its own account's entry and its campaigns'; a campaign entry only the same campaign) keeps it blocked ("…but Q4 Renewals still won't dial it. It stays blocked by another entry: North account"); one inside its reach blocks it only there ("It stays blocked only where another entry applies: Campaign: Q4 Renewals"); any other is named as blocking it **elsewhere** ("Another entry blocks it elsewhere: Campaign: Summer"), never as keeping it blocked. An entry whose account cannot be read (a campaign that no longer exists) never counts as covering. The toast follows: "another entry still blocks it for Q4 Renewals", "…can dial it again, except where another entry still blocks it", or plain "…can dial it again" | **default, pending Manas** |
| DS6 | What an account admin lists and removes | The entries that belong to their account: its account entries and its campaigns' entries (a campaign entry is stored without an account, so the old `account_id = <own>` filter hid every station mark on their own campaigns and made them unremovable). Every-campaign entries stay unlisted and unremovable for them (`scope=tenant` and `account_id=tenant` are `403`). Reviewer's point, unchanged (DS4 and DS6 behave as built): an account admin's add returns `already_covered` with the scope of a tenant-wide entry they cannot list, so the add is an existence oracle for those entries; and if an admin of the whole organisation later removes that tenant-wide entry, the account's request is gone with it (nothing was written for the account). Customer history and the soft phone already show tenant-wide entries to account users | Ownership: **default, pending Manas**. **Open, for Manas:** whether an account admin should SEE every-campaign entries (read only), and whether an account's add over a tenant-wide entry should be written anyway, so it outlives that entry's removal |
| DS7 | The words for scope | Everywhere an entry is shown: **Every campaign**, **North account** (or **This account** where the name is not known; customer history, which spans every account the caller can see, says **An account**, since the entry may not be the one in view), **Campaign: Q4 Renewals**; a scope a newer server sends is named as the widest, never by its code. The Do Not Call page, customer history (was "Every account" / "Account: North"; a campaign entry keeps its account in brackets), the soft phone's DNC refusal and call detail, console and mobile alike. The page's list entries carry `scope`, `scope_account_id`, `account_name`, `campaign_name` (read by primary key after the list). Wire codes unchanged | **default, pending Manas** |
| DS8 | A `suppress` outcome on the station | Found during DS, fixed the same day: on the web station, the mobile app and a supervisor filing for an agent, an outcome that suppresses (`do_not_call`) moved that one contact to `suppressed` / `dnc` and wrote **no** `dnc_entries` row, so another roster row with the number in the same campaign stayed dialable, a re-upload brought it back, and the mobile wrap-up's confirmation (`StationCopy.suppressConfirmBody`) promised an entry that was not written. The disposition route (`api/routes/agency/attempts.ts`) now treats it as the soft phone and inbound treat the same outcome: it writes the **campaign** entry (`source` agent, `reason` `disposition:<code>`, `added_by` whoever filed it, the supervisor when filed on an agent's behalf, whether or not the contact still belonged to the attempt), the public layer writes its `dnc_entry.created` audit row (once, on a new row; never the number), and the campaign's other roster rows on the number are suppressed after the release (step 5a; outside the transaction, so a failed sweep costs only the early sweep: the entry blocks them at dial time). Only with the outcome's **first** write, decided on the attempt row locked in the transaction, and in one transaction with it (as the DNC route's optional disposition): a failed entry rolls the outcome back, so the agent's retry is a first write again and writes both, and a same-code replay (a queued retry, a supervisor re-filing from history) writes no entry, so it never puts back a number an admin has since removed. A number that is not usable E.164 writes nothing, as `markDnc` always has, and the disposition answer says so: `dnc_recorded` (optional; present only when an entry was attempted, `true` listed, `false` not) is mapped by the public layer from the internal answer's `dnc_entry` (which it strips), and both station clients add the `dnc_recorded: false` copy after saving ("This contact won't be dialled again. It couldn't go on this campaign's Do Not Call list…"). Never tenant-wide. Insights' `dnc_marked` and the agent report's count agent-source entries, so they now include station suppress outcomes, not only Mark DNC presses. Tests: `test/integration/api/station-suppress-dnc.test.ts` (mutation-checked). Also corrected: the station's copy for `dnc_recorded: false` (console and app) said "We're still adding it"; it now says the number could not go on the list and to ask an admin to check it; campaign settings' summary of a suppress outcome said "nothing dials them again" and now names this campaign's list | **default, pending Manas** |

## Rulings of 2026-11-15: soft-phone calls in campaign results (SR)

Reshma (ops lead) asked for soft-phone calls in the campaign results and the exported CSV, and in
the connect rate and analytics, so a campaign's calls can be tracked from one place. Soft-phone
calls tagged to a campaign were already on Insights (`calls.softphone_calls`, a placed count), the
Call log's Soft-phone calls view and the agent report. These rulings add them everywhere a campaign
reader looks, beside the dialler's figures. Detail: [`softphone.md`](softphone.md) §7 "Campaign
results", [`insights.md`](insights.md), [`reports.md`](reports.md).

| ID | Ruling | Status |
|---|---|---|
| SR1 | **Beside the dialler's, never blended.** Agents choose whom to ring on the soft phone, so a soft-phone call folded into the dialler's connect rate would inflate a figure that measures the list and the pacing. The dialler's connect rate is unchanged (human connects ÷ placed first dials, IN10) and is labelled "Dialler connect rate" wherever soft-phone figures show beside it, the campaign report's PDF and workbook included (SR8); the soft phone's placed, connected and connect rate, and an **All calls** total, sit next to it. No rate is served on the wire (counts only, the Insights rule) **Note 2026-11-22 (CL7, CL29):** the total beside them is the **Calls by type** table's All calls column, which has counts and no rate; the dialler and the soft phone together are **Outbound**, and their rate the Outbound connect rate | default, pending Manas |
| SR2 | **One definition of a soft-phone call on a campaign** (`softphone-campaign-sql.ts`), shared by Insights, `/stats`, the campaign report and the agent report's count: tagged to the campaign and in its tenant; **placed** = `dialed_at` set (a row whose dial failed before it was sent is not a call; a DNC or calling-hours refusal writes no row; an override that went ahead is a call); **connected** = outcome `connected` (the dialler's Connected figure's rule, and the Soft-phone calls list's own outcome filter; set when the call ends, so a call still in conversation is placed, not yet connected); **reached a person** = bridged and coded neither `voicemail` nor `no_disposition` (the dialler's `HUMAN_CONNECT_ATTEMPT_SQL` on a soft-phone call). Its connect rate is reached ÷ placed: a soft-phone call has no wrap-up redials, so every placed call is a first dial. A range is half-open `[from, to)` on `created_at` (the tag's index; a call is placed seconds after it is created), as Insights' count already was | default, pending Manas |
| SR3 | **All calls** = the dialler's and the soft phone's counts added: placed = dials (wrap-up redials included) + soft-phone placed; connected = Connected + soft-phone connected; connect rate = (human connects + soft-phone reached) ÷ (placed first dials + soft-phone placed). The page's one floor (25 in the denominator, IN5) applies to each rate on its own denominator **Amended 2026-11-22 (CL7, CL29, call log P4):** the dialler and the soft phone together are now called **Outbound**: placed and connected as above ("Outbound calls placed", "Outbound calls connected" on the report's Summary), and their rate the **Outbound connect rate** (same formula, same floor); **All calls** is the three call types added (dialler, soft phone, inbound) and has counts only, never a rate | default, pending Manas |
| SR4 | **Untagged soft-phone calls belong to no campaign**: they are in no campaign figure, export or report (the copy says so on the export's preamble, the report's definition and the console cards). The tag is fixed at the dial and only a running or paused campaign can be tagged (`SOFTPHONE_TAGGABLE_CAMPAIGN_STATUSES`), so a call cannot be tagged to a campaign after it completed; a call placed while it was paused stays on it, counted when it was created. A row in another tenant carrying the campaign's id (the tag has no foreign key) is never counted. An agent who later moves account keeps their calls on the campaign they were logged to (the campaign, not the agent's account, scopes them) | decided by the ask |
| SR5 | **When soft-phone figures show.** The server always serves the counts; the console shows them while `agency_softphone_enabled` is on for the account, **or** whenever the counts hold any call (a soft phone switched off later does not hide calls already logged to the campaign, and All calls stays the sum of what happened). With the flag off and no calls, the console shows exactly what it did before (the dialler's rate labelled just "Connect rate"). The Call log's export button and tip name the file's `soft_phone` rows by the same rule (the workspace's `/stats` count; the file carries them whatever the flag says). The soft phone's and All calls' rates below the floor say "Not enough calls", counting calls placed. This changes Insights' existing Soft-phone calls figure, which used to show whatever the flag said **Note 2026-11-22 (CL7, CL29):** "All calls' rates" here is the Outbound connect rate since P4 of the call log; the same floor and words apply to it. Inbound's column of Calls by type shows by this rule on its own flag, and also while the campaign has missed calls still to follow up | default, pending Manas |
| SR6 | **The campaign call export** (`GET /proxy/agency/campaigns/:id/attempts.csv`) carries the campaign's tagged soft-phone calls as rows beside its attempts, newest first on one keyset (`listCallLogForCampaign`, internal `GET /agency-campaigns/:id/call-log`). A new column after `auto_disposition_code`, `source` (`dialler` or `soft_phone`, wire-style), says which (PT12's `promise_date` and `promise_amount` follow it); no existing column moves, is renamed or changes meaning for a dialler row. A soft-phone row fills the attempt columns from the call's own: `attempt_id` = the soft-phone call's id, `contact_id` = the roster contact its number matched (empty for none), `phone` = the number dialled, `agent_user_id`/`agent_name` = who placed it, `recording_call_id` = its media leg; `attempt_number`, `wrapup_seconds` and `auto_disposition_code` are empty. The attempt list's filters mean the same on a soft-phone call (state, outcome, code, agent, contact, phone, caller ID, `from`/`to`, `placed`, `dialed_from`/`dialed_to`); the four attempt-only ones (`redial`, `agent_manual`, `agent_reopened`, `override_applied`) select no soft-phone call at `=1` and all of them at `=0`. New filter `source=dialler|soft_phone`; `source=dialler` is the file as it was apart from the `source` and promise columns. The 50,000-row ceiling, the time budget, the filename and the audit row are unchanged (the audit row's filters name `source` when sent). The Call log's JSON views stay one source each. *(Amended 2026-11-22 by CL11: the Call log's JSON view is one combined list, `GET /proxy/agency/campaigns/:id/call-log`, over the same read; the export, its columns and `source` are unchanged. [`call-log.md`](call-log.md))* *(Amended 2026-11-22 by CL13–CL16, P2: the read has a third source, the inbound calls filed under the campaign. `source` gains `inbound` (and `to_follow_up`, the open Missed rows), and three columns are appended after the promise columns: `wait_seconds`, `missed_reason`, `callback_of`; every earlier column keeps its place. [`call-log.md`](call-log.md) §4)* | default, pending Manas |
| SR7 | **Surfaces left as the dialler's** (no soft-phone figures): the Insights Agents tab's per-agent rows and leaderboard, Trends (series and best hours), Health (caller IDs, abandonment, carrier failures, recording), the Insights CSV buttons (outcomes, dispositions and the other tables, which are tables of dials), Home's board, and the agent record (roster, scorecard, the Analytics page's Agents tab). Each measures the dialler (pacing, the list, the caller IDs) or an agent's dialler work; the agent report already counts the agent's soft-phone calls apart. Per-agent soft-phone columns on the Agents tab are the likeliest next ask | default, pending Manas |
| SR8 | **Reports.** The campaign report's dataset carries `calls.softphone` (it embeds Insights' activity). When the window holds a placed soft-phone call (the report has no flag to read, so SR5's "any call" half alone; the PDF and the workbook share one gate, the renderer's `campaignSoftphoneFigures`), its workbook's Summary appends seven rows after every existing one (Soft-phone calls placed, Soft-phone calls connected, People reached on the soft phone, Soft-phone connect rate, All calls placed, All calls connected, All calls connect rate: SR3's sums, the floor of 25 on each rate's own denominator), its PDF's Calls section adds a Soft-phone calls line and an All calls line, and the dialler's rate is labelled "Dialler connect rate" on the PDF's KPI and the Summary row (SR1); otherwise neither shows and the rate stays "Connect rate". The definitions gain `softphone_calls` (which also explains All calls), and Connect rate's says it is the dialler's. On Insights and in the report the soft-phone counts are read live while the dialler's come from the rollup (its `as_of`, normally seconds behind), so All calls can add counts taken moments apart. The account report is unchanged (its definitions leave `softphone_calls` out; a follow-up) **Amended 2026-11-22 (CL7, CL29, call log P4):** the gate is the renderer's `campaignCallsByType` (a soft-phone call or an inbound call in the window). The seven Summary rows are now the soft phone's five (the four above and Soft-phone talk time) and the three **Outbound** rows, which are the old three under new names with the same numbers (Outbound calls placed = All calls placed, Outbound calls connected = All calls connected, Outbound connect rate = All calls connect rate); then the inbound rows, and the call types added as **All call types: calls / connected / people reached / talk time**. No "All calls …" row name is reused: a lookup on an old name finds nothing rather than a number that now includes inbound calls (review, 2026-11-22); the PDF's two lines gave way to the **Calls by type** table. `softphone_calls` no longer explains All calls: `outbound_connect_rate`, `inbound_calls` and `calls_by_type` do | default, pending Manas |
| SR9 | **No new index.** Measured on 2M soft-phone calls (100k on one campaign over 90 days) and 1M attempts: `/stats`' lifetime count 47 ms cold (a bitmap range of `idx_agency_manual_calls_campaign`), Insights' 7-day count 10 ms, the call log's first page 23 ms and a page 45 days back 3.5 ms (each branch a range of its own index; the soft-phone keyset's row comparison is applied as `created_at <=` on the index). No migration was added | decided |
| SR10 | **Who sees them.** The floors are unchanged: Insights and the export are `agency.supervise` (as the Soft-phone calls list is); `/stats` stays `agency.campaigns.read` and now carries three aggregate soft-phone counts (no number, agent or call). Agents never reach these surfaces; the mobile app reads none of them | decided |

## Rulings of 2026-11-15: contact import check and re-import (IM)

Reshma's asks: check a CSV and review the result before anything is written, then import the
checked file; correct and re-import a file without duplicating anyone, with imported, skipped and
rejected rows each shown and downloadable. Built in `agency-csv-ingest.ts` (the screen),
`agency-ingest-screen.ts`, `agency-ingest.service.ts`, `roster-presence.repository.ts`, the ingest
routes, migration `0042_ingest_check_and_skipped.sql`, and the console's `useRosterIngest` and
`IngestSummary` (both the builder's "Who to call" step and a campaign's Add contacts page).

| # | Topic | Ruling | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| IM1 | The check | The check is the existing `dry_run` job, run by the SAME CSV pass and the SAME per-batch screen as the import (no second validator). It writes no contact and no chunk marker. It names the campaign whenever one exists (the Add contacts page always; the builder once its draft exists), so it reports what the import will do against that campaign's roster and Do Not Call entries: rows in the file, ready to import, already in this campaign, rejected with a reason per row, duplicates within the file, Do Not Call rows by scope. Both read the roster as it stood when their job began, so a check and its import differ only when the campaign or the list changed in between | **default, pending Manas** |
| IM2 | Importing a checked file | **Import N contacts** is `POST /ingest/jobs/:id/import` on the finished check: the server re-reads the SAME stored upload with the check's own column mapping (copied from the check's row; the client cannot change it), as a new job with its own result, which the console compares with the check and explains when they differ, naming each count that moved. One import holds a check at a time: `uq_agency_ingest_jobs_check_job`, over pending, running and completed imports; a second click or a second tab answers 200 with that import, and a check's poll names it (`import_job_id`), which the console follows instead of offering Import again. **An import that failed or was cancelled lets its check go**: while the check is valid, Import starts a new import of it (the console offers **Import again**), the rows that did land being skipped (IM3); the failed import keeps its `check_job_id`. An import holding the check in an account the caller cannot see is not named: 409 `check_imported_elsewhere`. On a schema without 0042 a check's import is refused 409 `check_import_unavailable` (a 5xx body is masked); its poll still answers. A check is importable for 24 h after it finished (`AGENCY_INGEST_CHECK_VALID_HOURS`, then 410 `check_expired`: check again; the review says until when); an unfinished, failed or cancelled check, or an import's id, is refused with a code; a check made for one campaign cannot be imported into another. The builder may check before its draft exists and import into the draft. Storage: a missing upload fails the job `upload_missing` ("Upload it again"), a missing download answers 410 `export_expired`. No lifecycle rule was added for `agency-ingest/` objects (uploads and exports are kept, as today): how long to keep customer files is a retention decision | **default, pending Manas** (the 24 h window, and the retention of uploads) |
| IM3 | Already in the campaign: skipped, not rejected | A row whose duplicate key a contact of the campaign already carries is **skipped**: counted in `skipped` (`accepted + skipped + rejected = rows_read`), listed in a skipped-rows download, never sent, and nothing about the existing contact changes (B15 stands: no replace, no update). The key is the operator's: the number (by its digits) for the phone dedupe; the key columns (matched by header trimmed and case-insensitive, values trimmed and case-insensitive) for a column key; and for a row with no key (no duplicate check, or a blank key part) an exact copy (the insert's own `row_fingerprint`). Only contacts written before the job began count, so an import never finds its own rows. Any state counts (an exhausted or suppressed contact is still in the campaign; calling it again is a retry campaign). "Already in the campaign" is decided first, for every row carrying the key (a repeat in the file and a Do Not Call number included), and claims no key. **Behaviour change:** with the default phone dedupe, a top-up row whose number is already in the campaign used to land as a second contact when any other column differed; it is now skipped. "Don't remove duplicates" keeps landing such rows (only exact copies are skipped). With "Don't remove duplicates", two exact copies within ONE file are both counted ready (the check) and imported (the import's `accepted`), and the roster's `row_fingerprint` then refuses the second on arrival: the summary's refusal line says "already in this campaign, or an exact copy of an earlier row in the file". Counting them in the pass would hold a fingerprint per row of the file | **default, pending Manas** |
| IM4 | A Do Not Call row claims no key | **Supersedes "DNC row claims its key"** (§4). The screen asks the Do Not Call list per batch BEFORE any row of the batch claims its key, so a row on a Do Not Call number holds no key and a later row with the same loan on a callable number is imported. Why: under the old rule, fixing and re-importing could never get that loan called (the callable row was "a duplicate" of a row that was itself never imported, on every re-import). Calling the loan's other number is not a Do Not Call breach: the list is per number, and every dial is still checked. Fail-closed is unchanged: a list that cannot be read fails the job (`dnc_unavailable`) before anything of the batch is sent | **default, pending Manas** |
| IM5 | Which Do Not Call list | Each Do Not Call rejection names the widest entry that suppressed it: "for every campaign in your organisation", "for every campaign in this account", "for this campaign" (`dnc_by_scope` on the job; the reason in the download; the review's line in the same words). The scope is read after the gate and only labels: if that read fails the rows are still rejected, labelled `unknown`. The review says plainly that correcting the file does not import a Do Not Call row | **default, pending Manas** |
| IM6 | Reasons in plain words | Every per-row reason says how to fix the row (wire codes unchanged), e.g. "The 'Mobile' column is empty. Add the customer's phone number, or delete the row."; a duplicate names the row it repeats in words ("The same phone number is already on row 12 of this file"), never a key value; no reason starts with a character a spreadsheet reads as a formula. The downloads carry the phone cell as the file had it and are in file order, through the agency exports' CSV writer (`agency-csv.ts`): a cell starting `=`, `+`, `-`, `@`, tab or CR gains a leading apostrophe, as in every other export, a `+91…` number included; the import reads exactly that apostrophe back off (`unguardFormulaSigil`, also in the column analysis), so a downloaded row fixed and uploaded again imports as the value it was. A NUL (which Postgres cannot store) past the encoding probe's first 512 bytes is dropped from a data cell; a header carrying one refuses the file `unsupported_encoding` before anything is sent. The review shows the first 20 rejected rows (`rejected_sample`: line, column, the phone cell, code, reason; no other column) | **default, pending Manas** |
| IM7 | One import per campaign at a time | An import (not a check) is refused 409 `import_in_progress`, naming the running file, while another import into the campaign is live (`pending`/`running` with a heartbeat under 10 min: a job orphaned by a restart does not block). Decided under a transaction-scoped advisory lock on the campaign. Why: two imports reading the roster before either writes would both land a contact the other had under the operator's key; the fingerprint only catches exact copies. **Behaviour change:** two supervisors (or a double submit) starting imports into one campaign at once used to both run; the second is now told to wait. Checks are not limited: any number run at once, each reading the roster (a column key holds every key of the campaign's roster in memory for the job) | **default, pending Manas** |
| IM8 | An Excel customer list (2026-11-24, ClickUp "Campaign customer upload: accept Excel (.xlsx) as well as CSV"; console side CS7) | **Converted once, at upload.** `POST /ingest/upload` reads the workbook's sheet and stores it as CSV (`text/csv`, the key's name ending `.csv`: `customers.xlsx.csv`); the analysis, check, import, re-import and both downloads read that CSV unchanged. The 201 says `format: 'xlsx'` and `sheet: { name, other_sheet_names, other_sheet_count }` (`format: 'csv'` and no `sheet` for a CSV); `file_name` stays the supervisor's (`customers.xlsx`), `file_size_bytes` is the workbook's. The downloads are named without `.csv`, `.xlsx` or `.xlsm` (`customers-rejected-rows.csv`). Built in `agency-xlsx.ts` (the converter), `agency-xlsx-zip.ts`, `agency-xlsx-runner.ts` and `agency-xlsx-worker.ts` (where it runs). **Which sheet:** the first VISIBLE worksheet in tab order (choosing another sheet is not in the first cut, Manas, 2026-11-25: the supervisor saves the sheet they want first, or as CSV) (the workbook's `<sheets>` order, not the order or names of its parts); `other_sheet_names` is the other visible worksheets in tab order, at most 255, each name cut to 31 characters (Excel's longest), and `other_sheet_count` how many there are; hidden and very hidden sheets, chart sheets (and dialog and macro sheets) are never read nor named; none visible: refused. **Cells:** text as written; rich text runs joined (phonetic runs left out; only `<t>` text counts, so a pretty-printed `<is>` adds no whitespace); a hyperlink is its display text; a formula its cached result, empty without one; a boolean `TRUE`/`FALSE`; an error its text (`#N/A`), as Excel's Save As CSV; Excel's `_xHHHH_` escapes decoded; U+0000 dropped. A cell holds at most 65,536 characters (past Excel's 32,767 and Google Sheets' 50,000); a longer one refuses the workbook, as its text arrives (the reader never holds more than 4 × 65,536 characters of one text). **Numbers, as the sheet shows them** (Manas, 2026-11-25): `1,25,000.00` under `#,##0.00`, `15%` under `0%`, `₹1,250.50` under `[$₹-4009]#,##0.00` (`agency-xlsx-numfmt.ts`, each format compiled once per `numFmtId`, in one linear pass). Kept as before: General (and a scientific format) writes a whole number in full digits, never an exponent (`919876543210`; past 2^53 its 15 significant digits then zeros, as Excel holds it: `98765432109876500000`), and any other number at 15 significant digits (`0.1+0.2` is `0.3`; General's own display depends on the column's width); a format of 2 to 32 zeros and nothing else (one section, no literal) pads a whole number (`09876543210`; any other number under it, and every other zeros format such as `00000;(00000)`, is rendered: `012346`, `(00005)`); dates, times and elapsed times as below. **Phone columns are read by value** (Manas, 2026-11-25): in a column whose header looks like a phone column's, by the rule the column analysis uses for its phone-column suggestion (`looksLikePhoneHeader`, `phone-header.ts`, one function both use: `phone`, `mobile`, `cell`, `contact`, `msisdn`, `number`, `tel`; headers only, from the row the ingest takes as its header: the first row holding a value, and in a one-column sheet not one of only spaces or tabs, which the ingest skips; the conversion runs before mapping), a whole number is written in full digits when its format shows the number as plain digits (General, scientific, padding, or a format with only placeholders, grouping, decimals and spacing: `#,##0`, `0.00`, `#,##0_);(#,##0)`), so `9876543210` under `#,##0` imports; a format with `%`, a scaling comma, a currency or locale symbol or words of its own is rendered as shown even there (a `Contact Rate` column's 1 under `0%` is `100%`, a `Cancellation Charges` column's 1250 is `₹1,250.00`), and a date or time format still gives a date or time (a `Last Contact Date` column's dates stay dates), a non-whole number there, and every other column (`EMI`, `Loan No`), is written as shown. The rule is loose on purpose (a `Loan Number` column's whole numbers are written in full too).
