A format renders: up to four sections (positive; negative; zero; text); a last section holding an unquoted `@` is the text section wherever it stands, and the numeric sections before it are read as Excel and LibreOffice read them (`#,##0.00;@` gives -1250.5 as `-1,250.50`, `0.00;-0.00;@` gives 0 as `0.00`); one numeric section gives a negative number a leading `-` (before any currency symbol: `-₹1,250.50`); the negative section shows the number as it writes it (`(1,250.00)`, `5 Dr` under `0" Cr";0" Dr"`, `owes 5`); **the minus is added only when the negative section looks like the positive one** (picked by sign, the same literal text in the same places, only digits and colour differing: Excel's red-negative presets `0.00;[Red]0.00`, `"₹"#,##0.00;[Red]"₹"#,##0.00`, and `0;General`), the colour having carried the sign (Manas, 2026-11-25: `-1250.00`, `-₹5.00`); a section with words of its own is written as shown; **zero is zero** (a deliberate departure from Excel, which writes `-0`): a value whose shown digits are all zero is written as zero, in the zero section if there is one, else the positive section, never `-0`, `-₹0` or `(0)` (a section that shows no digits is chosen by sign alone: `"Cr";"Dr"` gives -0.3 as `Dr`); conditions (`[>=100000]`) choosing the section (a negative number under a condition gets its `-` unless the condition is `<` or `<=` zero or less, LibreOffice's rule: `[<0]"owes "0` writes `owes 5`), as in Excel's Indian lakh format `[>=10000000]##\,##\,##\,##0;[>=100000]##\,##\,##0;##,##0` (`1,50,000`, `12,34,56,789`; past 99 crore the first place takes the extra digits, `123,45,67,890`, as Excel shows it); colour tags dropped; a currency or locale tag as its symbol (`[$₹-4009]` is `₹`, `[$-409]` nothing); quoted text and `\` escapes as written, `_x` one space, `*x` nothing (each taking a whole character, an emoji included); `0`, `#`, `?` (a space) placeholders; `%` (×100 each); a trailing comma (÷1,000 each); `General` inside a section. Rounding is Excel's: half away from zero on the 15-digit decimal value (1.005 under `0.00` is `1.01`). **Grouping is Indian** (3, then 2s: `12,50,000`) whatever the commas' positions (confirmed, Manas, 2026-11-25): how Excel shows `#,##0` on an Indian system, and what `#,##,##0` asks for in Google Sheets and LibreOffice (a workbook made on a western system shows `1,250,000` where this writes `12,50,000`: the same number to every reader). Built-in ids: 1–4, 9–10, 37–40 as ECMA-376 lists them; 5–8 and 41–44, which depend on the workbook's locale, in their en-IN form with `₹` (when styles.xml spells the code out, as Excel does for 41–44, that code wins); 49 (`@`) is General, and so is any format with no numeric section (`@` spelled out, `"Ref "@`): a number under it is written as General. A format with no integer placeholder puts the integer before its own point (`"Rs.".00` gives 12.5 as `Rs.12.50`). Not rendered, the General rule instead: fractions (`# ?/?`), scientific formats, a thousands comma with literals among the digits, more than 32 forced integer digits, 30 decimals, 64 placeholders, 10 `%` signs or 10 scaling commas in a section, an unclosed bracket or quote, a bracket that looks like a condition but does not parse (`[>=1E5]`), a number the format's scaling takes past what a double holds (`0%` of 1e307), more than one `General` in a section, a code longer than 255 characters (Excel's own limit). Formats are read from `<numFmts>` only (a conditional format's `<dxfs>` are not cell formats). A cell costs at most a section's 255 literal characters, 64 placeholders and one General. Each code is compiled once per workbook, in one linear pass, its slots precomputed. A text cell is its text whatever the format. **What reads these numbers:** the agent station's facts (`parseContextFact`, AS19): money reads `₹1,25,000.00`, `₹ 1,25,000.00` and `1,25,000.00` as 125000.00; number reads `1,25,000.00` and the lakh format's `12,34,56,789`; `(1,250.00)` and `15%` are read as neither, so the card shows them as written (the rule "when in doubt, null"; a CSV saved from Excel already carried this text). Nothing else reads a contact's numbers: promised amounts are the agent's own, Insights and reports read none. **Dates:** a date format gives `YYYY-MM-DD`, then ` HH:mm` (`:ss` when the seconds are not zero) when the time is not midnight; a time-only format `HH:mm[:ss]`; an elapsed format (`[h]:mm`, `[mm]:ss`, `[ss]`) the duration in hours, `HH:mm[:ss]` (90 minutes under `[mm]:ss` is `01:30`, not Excel's `90:00`: the same duration in one form). Year first because the agent station's date facts read it as a calendar day (`parseDateFact`, AS19, with a time of day and no zone), where `25/11/2026` would need the day-first rule and a serial (`46351`) is refused. Both date systems (1900 with its 29 Feb 1900; 1904 from `workbookPr`). Not a date Excel can name, written as the number: a negative serial, one from 1 Jan 10000 on once rounded to the second, in either system (`2958466`; `2957004` in the 1904 system), and day 0 of the 1900 system under a format with no time (a date-and-time format shows its time). A `t="d"` cell (an ISO date, from strict writers) by the same rule through its cell's style (a time style gives the time of day); a zone, if written, is left out. **Merged cells:** the top-left value only, the rest empty; at most 10,000 merges; two over the same cell refused. **Shape:** columns A to the last column holding a value (formatted empty cells, and values a merge hides, do not widen it), every row padded to it; one line per sheet row, a blank row an empty line, trailing blank rows dropped. **Row numbers (also a CSV change):** the ingest numbers a row as a spreadsheet shows it: records and blank lines before it. Before, it counted the file's lines, so a blank line was not counted (rows after it one too high) and a quoted cell with line breaks counted one row per line (rows after it too low, though a spreadsheet opens that record as one row). Now row N of the review, the stored `csv_line_number` and both downloads is row N of the sheet, and of a CSV as Excel opens it; numbers are provenance only (`source_row_number` is not written). **Text is written raw** through RFC 4180 quoting with no formula guard, so a cell imports as a CSV holding that text does (an apostrophe our downloads add is read back off, IM6). **What the file is:** by its bytes, then its name. A compound file (an `.xls`, and also what an `.xlsx` with a password is) is refused under any name; a zip is read as a workbook under any name (a workbook renamed `.csv` is read as the workbook; no zip is a CSV); text named `.xlsx`/`.xlsm` is refused; any other text is a CSV as before (`.xls`-named text included). **Where it runs:** never in the server's process. A child process (`dist/xlsx-worker.js` in the image, its own esbuild entry) with `--max-old-space-size=1024`, the workbook on stdin, the CSV back on stdout (a pipe the child waits on, so the CSV is never whole in memory), and no environment but `PATH`. A child process and not a worker thread: a worker's `resourceLimits` do not hold in this server, because the image starts the primary with `--max-old-space-size=4096` and V8 applies that process-wide flag to every isolate (measured: a worker capped at 32 MB grew past 300 MB under it). 1 GiB: the primary has 4 GB and each media worker 768 MB; one conversion adds at most 1 GiB of heap plus the workbook's bytes (at most 512 MiB), enough for a realistic million-row list's shared strings. A deadline of 5 minutes, both passes and the S3 upload included (a million rows of ten columns convert in under a minute; the largest CSV allowed in about four); it reaches the S3 upload too. One conversion at a time, server-wide; the slot is freed when the child exits, whatever the CSV's reader does (a failure ends the CSV and the child's stdout at once, so an S3 upload that stalled or never started cannot hold it). The client's going away kills it, and so does the server closing: the upload route's `preClose` hook stops every conversion still live (a child still reading, and one whose CSV is still being stored in S3: a conversion is live until the route is done with it, not only until its child exits), and a conversion asked for after it (a body still arriving at SIGTERM) is refused at once, so `app.close()` (and the shutdown order behind it) does not wait out the deadline. The child's `oom_score_adj` is 1000, so if the host runs out of memory the kernel kills it before the server; it exits when the server's IPC channel closes. How the child ended is decided once it has exited, its stdio has closed and its IPC channel has disconnected (Node may deliver the child's last message after its `exit`; a channel still open 2 s after the exit is decided anyway), so a good workbook is never taken for a crash; the slot is freed at the exit. A child that dies is this upload's answer, never the server's, and the S3 multipart upload reading its CSV is aborted; a fork that cannot start (too many open files) is an error for the upload, not a crash. A bundle without `dist/xlsx-worker.js` says so at boot and on each Excel upload (a 500), never falling back to tsx. **Refusals**, 400 `{ error, code, message }` (`AgencyUploadRefusalCode`; in order `workbook_unsupported`, `not_a_workbook`, `workbook_unreadable`, `cell_too_long`, `workbook_no_sheet`; for any file also `no_file`, `upload_malformed` and `upload_stopped`, the client having gone): "This is an older Excel file (.xls) or a workbook with a password, which cannot be read. Open it in Excel and save it as an Excel Workbook (.xlsx) without a password, or as CSV, then upload it again."; "This file is named like an Excel workbook but is not one. Open it in Excel and save it as an Excel Workbook (.xlsx) or as CSV, then upload it again."; "This Excel file could not be read. Open it in Excel and save it again as an Excel Workbook (.xlsx) or as CSV, then upload it again." (not a workbook, damaged, encrypted parts, rows out of order, references that are not cell references, XML nested deeper than 64, more than 10,000 parts, more than 10,000 merges or overlapping ones, more than 65,536 cell styles or 4,096 number formats; and any failure the reader did not expect, never a 500); "A cell in this workbook holds more than 65,536 characters, more than a spreadsheet cell can. Shorten it, save the workbook and upload it again."; "This workbook has no sheet to read: every sheet is hidden or is a chart. Unhide the sheet with your customers, save the workbook and upload it again." **Limits** (approved, Manas, 2026-11-25), as the CSV path refuses them: the workbook is under the multipart limit; a first pass counts rows and width and stops at the first row past the header plus `AGENCY_MAX_ROWS`, or at the first value past column 100 (as its text arrives, before it is held), refused 422 `{ error, code: 'too_many_rows' \| 'too_many_columns', message }` in the ingest's words, before anything is stored; the CSV stops past `CSV_MAX_BYTES`, 413 `{ error, code: 'file_too_large', message }` "This workbook is too large to read: its sheet comes to more than 512 MB as CSV, and a file can be up to 512 MB. Split the customers into smaller files and upload them again." (a file over the multipart limit, CSV or Excel, is 413 `file_too_large` "This file is too large. A file can be up to 512 MB."). The reader's own caps on its work are refused 422 `workbook_too_complex` (below), never as a size: a part that expands past the smaller of its cap (the sheet 4 × `CSV_MAX_BYTES`, the shared strings 4 × their character budget, the others 16 MiB) and 100 times its compressed size plus 16 MiB; shared strings past 256 Mi characters, each string charged 16 more, or more strings than (rows + 1) × columns; more `<c>` elements in one pass, empty ones included, than 2 × (rows + 1) × columns. The conversion's own limits, 422 `{ error, code, message }`: `workbook_too_slow` "This workbook took too long to read. Save the sheet with your customers as a CSV file, or as a workbook with only that sheet, and upload it again." (the deadline while the workbook is still being read); `workbook_too_complex` "This workbook is too large or complex to read. Save the sheet with your customers as a CSV file, or as a workbook with only that sheet, and upload it again." (the heap cap, the kernel's OOM killer, or one of the reader's caps on its work above). A second Excel upload while one converts: 429 `{ error, code: 'workbook_busy', message, retry_after_seconds: 60 }` with `Retry-After: 60`, "Another Excel file is being read just now. Try again in a minute." (a CSV is never held up; a file named `.xlsx`/`.xlsm` is refused before it is converted or stored: the rest of its body is read and dropped first, within the multipart limit, because a browser still sending a large body often reports an early answer as a network error; past the limit the answer goes with `Connection: close`). At most 10 conversions started by one user in 15 minutes (`agency-xlsx-start-cap.ts`, in memory; a `workbook_busy` does not count): past it, 429 `{ error, code: 'upload_rate_limited', message, retry_after_seconds }` with `Retry-After`, "You've uploaded a lot of Excel files in a short time. Try again in N minutes, or upload the list as a CSV file.", before anything is converted, the rest of the body dropped as for `workbook_busy` (after detection for a workbook under another name). The server's side, 503 `{ error: 'Service Unavailable', code, message, retry_after_seconds: 60 }` with `Retry-After: 60`, through the error mask's reviewed opt-out: `server_restarting` "The server is restarting. Upload the file again in a minute." (the server closing mid-conversion or mid-upload; the connection is closed with it); `upload_storage_slow` "The file was read, but it could not be saved in time. Upload it again in a few minutes." (the deadline after the workbook was read, with only the S3 upload left). **Library:** not exceljs's streaming `WorkbookReader`: it writes every sheet to a temporary file when the shared strings come later in the zip (they do, in what Excel writes), decodes each chunk of XML on its own (25 of 20,000 Hindi names came out with U+FFFD in a probe), reads only `xl/worksheets/sheetN.xml`, keeps the last run of an inline rich string, reads a formula's boolean or error result as a number and caps nothing. The reader is the zip's central directory and `node:zlib`, with exceljs's own XML parser (`saxes` 5.0.1, a server dependency); exceljs 4.4.0 is a devDependency that writes test workbooks | **Decided** (Manas, 2026-11-25): the rules, the row numbers' change for CSV files, numbers as the sheet shows them with phone columns by value and red negatives keeping their minus, Indian grouping, the limits (1 GiB, 5 minutes, one at a time) and the 429 and 503s; another sheet not in the first cut |

Open for Manas: the three behaviour changes above (IM3's top-up, IM4, IM7) (IM8 is decided); how long uploads and
the rejected and skipped downloads are kept (IM2); whether "already in the campaign" should
ever update a contact's other columns (it does not: B15).

## Rulings of 2026-11-15: promise to pay (PT)

Asked by Reshma (ops lead; collections teams): when an agent marks a contact as having promised to
pay, record the promised payment date (and optionally the amount), show it, and let supervisors
follow promises up. Built across the server, migration `0041_promise_to_pay.sql`, the contracts,
the console and the agent app. Wire: `packages/contracts/src/agency/promise.ts` and the fields it
names; shared rules `packages/domain/src/promise-to-pay.ts` (ported by hand to
`apps/mobile/lib/core/station/promise_rules.dart`); server `apps/server/src/agency/promise-to-pay.ts`.

| # | Topic | Default used | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| PT1 | What a promise is | A catalogue entry opts in with `requires_promise`: the agent must enter `promise_date` (`YYYY-MM-DD`, the customer's calendar day) and may enter `promise_amount`. Stored on the call it was recorded on (`agency_call_attempts`, `agency_manual_calls`, `agency_inbound_calls`: every promise kept in history) and copied to the contact as its latest (PT6). A same-code replay corrects it (last-write-wins, as notes and `callback_at`); a supervisor may file it on behalf like any outcome. A replay that sends the date already stored on that call is not refused for that day having passed (a retry queued across midnight, an amount corrected after the day): only the past-date check is waived, only for that exact date and code; a changed date is checked in full and the 30-day bound always holds (all three paths, `validatePromiseFields`' `recordedDate`). A corrected date moves a booked follow-up on every path (the station's `releaseFromDisposition`, the soft phone's `rescheduleFromManualReplay`, inbound's `rescheduleFromInboundReplay`, which also moves a corrected `callback_at`) | **default, pending Manas** |
| PT2 | The follow-up | Off by default. `promise_follow_up_days` (1–14, only on a `requires_promise` entry) books a call that many days after the promised date: the start of that day in the customer's zone, handed to the callback arm of the disposition decision and deferred into the calling window (a day with no window moves to the next calling day). It is a callback in every other respect: the entry's code joins `callbackCodes`, so CA4 ownership (on the station only: the agent who took the promise owns the follow-up on `strict` / `preferred`; a follow-up booked from a soft-phone or inbound call assigns no owner, as their ordinary callbacks do not), CA5's grace and fallback, the callback bucket and rebalancing treat it as one; DNC is checked at dial time. `callback_at` is NOT written on the attempt (it records what an agent asked the customer for), so Insights' "callbacks booked" does not count follow-ups. A booked follow-up stays booked if the entry is later edited or removed (as a callback does); without its code in the catalogue it reads as an ordinary due contact. The reverse holds too, as for `requires_datetime`: adding `promise_follow_up_days` to an entry already in use puts its code in `callbackCodes` at once, so an open contact whose last outcome is that code now reads as a callback (the callback bucket, `waiting=callback`, CA4 / CA5 on its owner) though no follow-up was booked for it; its `next_attempt_at` is unchanged. **The split this leaves** (found in review): everything that reads `callbackCodes` counts follow-ups (`waiting=callback`, rebalancing, the allocation counters' open and overdue callbacks), while Insights' "callbacks booked" and IN4's on-time / late / missed callback timing read the attempt's `callback_at` and do not; so a supervisor can see follow-ups as open or overdue callbacks with no booked callback behind them, and none is ever classed on time or missed. Reconciling is a choice: record the follow-up's instant where the rollup reads it (every callback figure then counts it), or keep follow-ups out of `callbackCodes` (none does) | **Decided** (Manas, 2026-11-15: the split stands as built); 1–14 days and the Insights counting **default, pending Manas** |
| PT3 | A promise alone never dials | Without a follow-up the contact goes exactly where the entry's flags and the retry policy send it, as for any outcome (a plain label: `completed`; a `retry`: the retry's delay). Pinned by `promise-to-pay-routes.test.ts` "PT3" and mutation-checked | **Decided** (ask) |
| PT4 | Which dates | A real `YYYY-MM-DD` date, not before the customer's today and at most 30 days after it (Manas, 2026-11-15; was 180). "Today" is in the contact's zone when usable, else the campaign's, else UTC (the calling-hours choice). The console and the app check a day either side of the agent's own today (they are not given the customer's zone) and let the server decide the edge. Today itself is accepted ("I'll pay this evening") | **Decided** (Manas, 2026-11-15: 30 days) |
| PT5 | The amount | Optional; ≥ 0, at most two decimal places and twelve digits before them; stored `NUMERIC(14,2)`, a decimal string on the wire, never a float. No currency is configured anywhere, so it is printed as typed, grouped in the console's locale (`en-IN`: `1,25,000`) with no symbol, in the console and the app | **default, pending Manas** (a currency setting) |
| PT6 | The contact's latest promise | Denormalised on `agency_contacts` (date, amount, the promised day's start and end instants in the customer's zone, the recording call's start, a follow-up mark) with the partial index `(campaign_id, promise_date) WHERE promise_date IS NOT NULL`: reading "the latest promise across three call tables" per row of a million-contact list, and filtering on it, could not use an index. Ordered by the recording call's start: a later call's promise replaces it, the same call's replay corrects it, an older call's replay never overwrites a newer one. A retry campaign's contacts do not inherit it (their history shows it) | **default, pending Manas** |
| PT7 | Where a promise stands | `upcoming` (before the day), `due_today`, then `missed` or `followed_up`. **Followed up**: an outcome was recorded on the contact by a call that started after the promise's call and on or after the promised day (a reminder call before the day does not count). **Missed** means the day ended without that, never "unpaid": there is no payment signal, and the copy says so. Judged on stored instants, so the list's SQL and the clients need no zone. A replay of the promise's own call that keeps its date keeps the mark (the later call still followed it up); a moved date, or a new promise from another call, starts unmarked (`recordContactPromise`). `followed_up` is a fourth state beyond the ask's three, so a passed promise someone dealt with is not called missed | **default, pending Manas** |
| PT8 | Refusals and older apps | Four action codes (`promise_required`, `invalid_promise_date`, `invalid_promise_amount`, `promise_not_expected`), the same strings as the soft phone's and inbound's `reason`. A promise sent with another outcome is refused, not dropped. No capability signal. An app that predates the field shows the server's `message` for the station's unknown code, which names the fix ("update it or pick another outcome"); on the soft phone and inbound the code (`disposition_invalid`, `inbound_disposition_invalid`) is one it knows, so it shows its own generic outcome line ("Check the outcome, its note and its callback time"), not the server's. The agent can pick another outcome or let the wrap-up lapse as with any unsaved outcome. An app that knows promises shows each reason in the console's words on all three surfaces (`promiseRefusalCopy`). A capability (`caps=`) would only hide an outcome the agent needs; updating the app is the remedy | **default, pending Manas** |
| PT9 | What the agent sees | The station's `reserved` panel carries the contact's latest `promise` (date, amount, state) and each prior attempt its own; the console shows it above the contact's fields and in the history, the app under the contact's name (ringing, on call, Details). The soft phone and inbound panels show it in the prior attempts | **default, pending Manas** |
| PT10 | Catalogue combinations | Refused at the catalogue write with the field named: a promise beside `requires_datetime` (two schedules for one answer: use the follow-up) or `suppress`; a follow-up beside `retry` or `terminal`, or without a promise; any promise in the soft phone's or an inbound route's own catalogue (no campaign contact to keep it on). Allowed: a promise on a `terminal` entry (record it and close the contact), beside a `retry` (the retry decides), `override`, `requires_note`, `is_success`. Both validators (public and internal) share one rule and its words; the console mirrors them | **default, pending Manas** |
| PT11 | Automatic outcomes | A promise entry cannot be an unanswered call's automatic outcome (OD3's rule, `autoDispositionRefusal`): nobody spoke to the customer, so there is no promise | **default, pending Manas** |
| PT12 | Exports | Appended at the END, so a consumer reading by position keeps its columns: the attempts CSV `promise_date`, `promise_amount`; the contacts CSV `promise_date`, `promise_amount`, `promise_state`. The customer-history CSV puts it in "Details", as the automatic outcome is | **default, pending Manas** |
| PT13 | Where it can be filed | Every outcome path that settles a campaign contact: the station (paced dials, the agent's own call, wrap-up redials), a tagged soft-phone call and an inbound call matched to a contact. An untagged soft-phone call and an unmatched inbound call cannot (their catalogues refuse it, PT10). A tagged soft-phone call that claimed no roster contact (its number is not on the list, or the contact is closed and the call only linked it) cannot either: there is no contact it settles to keep the promise or book its follow-up on, so it is refused `disposition_invalid` / `promise_needs_contact` before the fields are checked, and the console's and the app's pads do not offer promise entries on such a call (`contact.claimed`). A Mark DNC that carries an outcome marks an earlier promise followed up; one that carries a promise outcome is refused `promise_required` before anything is written (the route takes no date). The station's contact promise write is its own statement after the outcome's (as its contact release is; a failure fails the save and the agent's retry writes it); the soft phone and inbound write it in the outcome's transaction | **default, pending Manas** |
| PT14 | Reports, Insights and analytics | Reports and Insights enumerate outcome codes as before: a promise entry is one more code (its promise is not reported there yet). No analytics event is renamed: the console adds `requires_promise` and `has_promise` to `agency_disposition_submitted` and `has_promise` to `agency_softphone_outcome_saved` (booleans, never the date or amount); the app sends neither event | **default, pending Manas** |

## Rulings of 2026-11-16: live call listening (LS)

Supervisors see the calls in progress in their account and listen in silently from the console:
listen only, forked from our own bridge in the process that owns the call, never touching the
agent's or the customer's audio. Planned from the code, reviewed and locked by Manas on
2026-11-16; LS14, LS15 and LS16 are the build's own calls in phases 1, 2 and 3, LS17 the voice review's; LS18–LS20 are the product owner's decisions on the persona review, LS21 is Manas's decision on its database outage (decision 4), LS22 its server calls and the final review's. Manas ratified the build's calls (LS14–LS17, those in LS19 and LS20, LS22 (a)–(u)) on 2026-11-17, so every LS ruling is decided but LS22 (v) and (w), the build's fixes on the PR review. The full table, the architecture, the wire
format, the lifecycle and the phase status are in [`listening.md`](listening.md). Permission
`agency.calls.listen` (floor `account_admin`); flags `agency_live_listen_enabled` and
`agency_live_listen_unrecorded`; migrations `0047_listen_sessions.sql`,
`0048_listen_sessions_unverified.sql` and `0049_listen_history_index.sql`; contracts
`packages/contracts/src/agency/listen.ts` and `api/agency/live-calls.ts`.

| ID | Decision | Status |
|---|---|---|
| LS1 | Consent: `agency_live_listen_enabled` (global, tenant, account; default off; client-exposed) is the gate; the inbound notice becomes "This call may be recorded or monitored for quality and training purposes." (its WAV regenerated); outbound, the agent's script | **Decided** (Manas, 2026-11-16); the consent and notice wording's legal check (LS1, LS2, LS18) done (Manas, 2026-11-17) |
| LS2 | The agent: no live indicator; while the flag is on, a standing line "Calls may be listened to by a supervisor for quality and training." on the console station (info popover, join screen) and the app's station info sheet (copy only). The "Supervisor listening" pill is not built. The one-time notice that also shows the line is a build call, LS22 (u) | **Decided** (Manas, 2026-11-16) |
| LS3 | Who: `agency.calls.listen` at `account_admin` (tenant admins and owners by the hierarchy); the list needs `agency.supervise`; agents, operators, viewers and super-admins cannot listen; your own call is refused `listen_own_call`; mobile supervisors out of scope | **Decided** (Manas, 2026-11-16) |
| LS4 | Unrecorded calls: `agency_live_listen_unrecorded` (default on). Off for the call's tenant or account: "Recording off", Listen disabled ("Listening is turned off for unrecorded calls"), the grant `409 listen_recording_off`. On: listenable, the row still says "Recording off" | **Decided** (Manas, 2026-11-16) |
| LS5 | Caps 2 per call, 1 per supervisor (a second ends the first `superseded`), 20 per process; peak 100 calls, 5–6 supervisors; the tap runs in the process that owns the call, no listen worker, no audio over IPC | **Decided** (Manas, 2026-11-16) |
| LS6 | The supervisor's notice ("This session is logged. Do not record or share what you hear.", always shown) and a first-use confirmation per browser ("Listening is logged"); each listen a session row and the audit pair `agency_call.listen_started` / `_ended` on the campaign's History | **Decided** (Manas, 2026-11-16) |
| LS7 | Fail closed on the way in: a failed grant, token or row insert refuses the socket with a reason the bar can show (no row, no audio); a failed audit write is logged and counted, the row is the record | **Decided** (Manas, 2026-11-16) |
| LS8 | The tap never touches the call: fed after the relay write, its own codecs, every entry point guarded, a 50-frame fault budget (`tap_error`), one bad listener stays one, back-pressure on the listener's socket only (48 KiB drop, 256 KiB close `send_backlog`) | **Decided** (Manas, 2026-11-16) |
| LS9 | Numbers masked in the live list (`****1234`); Show/Hide fetches the full number on demand (`agency.supervise`), never polled, re-masked on leaving; not audited | **Decided** (Manas, 2026-11-16) |
| LS10 | Revocation immediate on a role change or removal (`revokeUser`, IPC `listen.revoke`), otherwise within 30 s by a re-check that reads the database, not the membership cache; both end `permission_revoked` | **Decided** (Manas, 2026-11-16) |
| LS11 | The wire: one tag byte (`0x01` customer, `0x02` agent) and 160 bytes of A-law per binary message; text frames control only; the browser mixes, the server does not | **Decided** (Manas, 2026-11-16) |
| LS12 | `agency_listen_sessions` is kept for the platform audit log's horizon, deleted by the same daily maintenance at the same cutoff | **Decided** (Manas, 2026-11-16) |
| LS13 | The call detail pages show "Listened to by" (who, start–end, duration) from `agency_listen_sessions`, to `agency.supervise` holders | **Decided** (Manas, 2026-11-16) |
| LS14 | Phase 1's own calls: the refusal statuses (`listen_call_not_live` a uniform 404); the socket's close codes and each end reason's code; a CHECK that a listener is never the call's agent; no foreign keys on the session table and a Down that refuses while it has rows; retention by a row's start; `inbound_route_name` on the live row; `AGENCY_LISTEN_MAX_PER_USER` as a setting; the relay dwell sampled one frame in 50 | **Decided** (Manas, 2026-11-17) |
| LS15 | Phase 2's own calls: the fault budget counts each leg's failed frames in a row apart; a sink whose `send` throws or answers `closed` is ended `listener_disconnected` (`tap_error` is the budget only); a sink's `frames_dropped` counts its soft-limit drops and the frames the tap failed while it was attached; a call is listenable once the bridge was announced (`bridged`) and through the agent's re-attach window, a parked inbound caller `not_bridged`; a listener ends `call_ended` as the call starts to settle (VoiceLink's `ending`, the top of the terminal teardown), so nothing said after the hangup reaches it (revised by the voice review); the hard cap is reached only by bytes other than media (LS17 ends a listener after 10 s of drops) | **Decided** (Manas, 2026-11-17) |
| LS16 | Phase 3's own calls: the grant's internal handler is `POST /listen-grants` (owner `body_session`), since every `/sessions/:id` route is the session's own agent's (Q8); the upgrade reserves its slot under the caps before it writes the row, and a call gone once the row is written closes it `call_ended` with no audit pair; `revokeUser` re-checks the supervisor against the database (a promotion ends nothing) and fails closed (what it cannot check ends), while the periodic pass fails open until a session has gone 4 × `AGENCY_LISTEN_RECHECK_MS` unverified (coordinator decision, server review; 2 × and `access_unverified` since LS21); the flags are read through `strictSnapshot`, so a flag-store outage is "cannot check" (the grant 503 `listen_unavailable`), never a default; the upgrade asks the database too and carries a per-supervisor revocation generation from the grant's token through its reservation, so a revocation between grant and admission refuses it (4403); a failed row close is retried on the re-check tick and a live process sweeps its own rows it no longer holds (`server_restarted`); a cross-worker supersede ends only older rows; a stopping process refuses `listen_unavailable`; retention on the audit partitions' month boundary; `maxPerUser` counts per tenant; the super-admin's role change and removal revoke too; one listen per supervisor across media workers (`listen.revoke` `superseded`, relayed by the primary); the list's `listenable` from the open rows, the grant and the upgrade from the owning process, a supervisor's own listen never counted against them; in one process HTTP closing ends the listens `server_shutdown` after the WebSocket plugin has closed the sockets (no `ended` frame), a media worker before (1001); the listener reads at most 256 bytes a message; the live row's `aht_seconds` is the campaign stats' (cached a minute) and its `correlation_id` the spine row; a withheld number masks as `****`; `listen_sessions` on the campaign attempt and soft-phone call reads | **Decided** (Manas, 2026-11-17) |
| LS17 | The voice review's calls: a μ-law customer (Plivo) reaches listeners through a 256-entry μ-law → A-law table (the G.711 per-sample conversion; both 8 kHz, so no resampling, no frame of lag, at least as faithful as the codec path), not the tap's own codec pair as the locked design said; a transcoded leg's first output led by silence to a whole frame; a listener with every frame dropped at the soft limit for 10 s ended `send_backlog`; a failed message counted in 20 ms frames in `frames_dropped` and the fault budget; the alert on the tap's cost is `agy-listen-loop-delay` (event-loop delay p99 above 40 ms for 10 min while listens are open), replacing the relay-dwell alert | **Decided** (Manas, 2026-11-17) |
| LS18 | The inbound consent gap is accepted: the notice plays only on a recorded call whose route has its notice on, while unrecorded calls are listenable by default (LS4). Tenants own their route settings; the product neither changes nor blocks them. The enablement checklist (`operations.md` "Enabling live call listening for a tenant", item 2) makes it explicit: before the flag goes on, every inbound route that should be monitorable plays a notice that mentions monitoring, or `agency_live_listen_unrecorded` is off for that tenant or account | **Decided** (Manas, 2026-11-16) |
| LS19 | The account's listening history: who listened to which call, for every call type (`GET /proxy/agency/listen-history`, `.csv`; `agency.supervise` alone, as amended: the flag is not read, so turning listening off does not hide past listens), filters for supervisor, agent and date range; a supervisor's sessions on one call merged on the server into one entry while each starts at most 30 s after the earlier ones ended (or while one is open); the CSV masked and audited `agency_listen_sessions.exported`; a campaign's listen History rows name the agent (`detail.agent_name`, at the read). The build's calls (merge rule, limits, audit shape) are listed in `listening.md` | **Decided** (Manas, 2026-11-16); amended 2026-11-17: the history is gated by `agency.supervise` alone, so turning listening off does not hide past listens; the build's calls **Decided** (Manas, 2026-11-17) |
| LS20 | Guard rails on `agency_live_listen_enabled`: a handling policy in the super-admin flag routes (a reason to turn it on; never in a bulk rollout; no global On, the global Off kept as a kill switch), the enablement checklist in `operations.md`, and a read-only Settings line fed by making `agency_live_listen_unrecorded` client-exposed (the console reads both from the client flag map) | **Decided** (Manas, 2026-11-16); the global-On refusal and the client exposure are the build's calls, **Decided** (Manas, 2026-11-17) |
| LS21 | When the access check cannot be made (persona review, decision 4): the periodic re-check ends a session not verified for 2 × `AGENCY_LISTEN_RECHECK_MS` (60 s at the defaults; `STALE_AFTER_RECHECKS` 4 → 2) with a distinct end reason `access_unverified` (close 4503 `ACCESS_UNVERIFIED`), not `permission_revoked`; the console says listening stopped because access could not be checked and offers Try again. A contract addition (end reasons, close code and its mapping, the metric's label; not mirrored in Dart) and `0048_listen_sessions_unverified.sql` extending the end reason CHECK (0047 unchanged). Amends LS16 (d) | **Decided** (Manas, 2026-11-16) |
| LS22 | The persona review's server calls ([`listening.md`](listening.md) "LS22 in full"): (a) the immediate path's unreadable checks end `access_unverified` too; (b) its close code 4503, an older console reconnecting on it; (c) `live_at` (0048), stamped as a session goes live, "Listened to by" live rows only, older rows backfilled; (d) protocol pings every 10 s, a pong counted as liveness; (e) 20 client messages at once, 5 a second, then 1008 `listen_message_rate`; (f) a refusal sends no `ended` (a tap ended under the attach 4404, opening it throwing 1011 with the row `tap_error`, stopping meanwhile 1011); (g) a token store outage at the upgrade 1011, a token given twice 4401; (h) every close then terminated after 5 s; (i) `customer_phone_withheld` on the live row and `404 live_call_number_withheld` (a union of its own) on the reveal; (j) the fault budget through `openListenTap`'s options; (k) the upkeep's held ids read at its sweep, a tick in flight not stacked; (l) `POST /users/invite` and the invite's claim re-check listens; (m) the load check's timing bounds with 4+ CPUs or when asked; and the final review's: (n) `Cache-Control: no-store` on the live-call routes and the history's page; (o) the upgrade's access check and row insert each bounded to 10 s (1011 `listen_unavailable`); (p) the env default `FF_AGENCY_LIVE_LISTEN` dev and staging only, warned at boot; (q) a tap that throws as it opens counted `agency_listen_tap_errors_total{stage="open"}`; (r) a failed close of a dead process's rows retried on the primary's re-check tick, `server_restarted` counted for live rows only; (s) a cross-process supersede reaching a listen still in its upgrade; (t) the history reading one page's visits on `idx_agency_listen_sessions_account_started` (`0049_listen_history_index.sql`); (u) the agent's one-time notice (console `StationListenFirstNotice`, app `ListenFirstNoticeCard`); and the PR review's: (v) one supersede rule (`supersededBy`) for any `AGENCY_LISTEN_MAX_PER_USER`: a new listen ends the supervisor's own on its call (never two on one call, and the per-call cap never counts them) and, of the rest, those past the cap, ranked across every worker's rows (`supersedeTargets`), local and remote alike; (w) the member routes call the membership-change subscribers without waiting for them (the re-check off the admin's request path), and on a seat added the re-check leaves a session it could not read to the periodic pass and its staleness cap instead of ending it at once (a read that says no still ends it; a role change or removal still fails closed) | **Decided** (Manas, 2026-11-17); (v) and (w) the build's calls (2026-11-17) |

## Live floor (LF)

The supervisor's landing page, Home (B28a), redesigned as Live floor: tiles, Needs you now beside
the active campaigns, and the agents. Phase 1 is console only, on Home's reads (the board, Today so
far, the missed-calls count). Phase 2 adds one account-wide floor read (an agent card grid,
"N of M assigned", promises and "Will pay", who paused a campaign); Phase 3 lets supervisor and
agent reach each other (Need help, a message to an agent, break limits, giving late callbacks to
free agents, one account-wide callbacks list); Phase 4 (Join call, whisper) reopens LS's "listen
only" and waits for a ruling. Controls for what is planned and not built are real buttons marked
`aria-disabled` and "Coming soon" (`components/common/ComingSoon.tsx`): focusable, so a keyboard
user can reach one and read its tooltip (what it will do), and pressing one does nothing; never
links that go nowhere.

| ID | Decision | Status |
|---|---|---|
| LF1 | Home is renamed Live floor in the sidebar, the breadcrumb, the page and its copy; the URL (`/agency/home`), the gate (`HOME_GATE`), the ids (`home`) and the badge rules stay, and search still finds it by "home" (`PAGE_KEYWORDS`). A Callbacks section (`/agency/callbacks`, gated as Live floor: `CALLBACKS_GATE`) follows Campaigns, with a badge in the danger tone counting every active campaign's overdue callbacks from the shared board (`useCallbacksBadgeCount`, no request of its own), a lower bound ("4+", "at least 4") past the board's cap or while a campaign's callbacks are not known, as the Live floor tile is; the phone's menu dot (B28a (6)) lights for either badge and says both; until the account-wide list exists the page lists each campaign's late callbacks, linking to its Callbacks tab. The campaign filter is client side over the board's campaigns and kept in `?campaign=`; with a campaign picked, Needs you now says how many rows it leaves out. Wrap-up reads "Choosing outcome" on Live floor only (`CHOOSING_OUTCOME_LABEL`) until the wording is decided for every screen. Customers reached's line divides conversations by every dial, so it says "of dials answered", never the connect rate (B28a), withheld under 25 dials. Today so far's talk time (B28a) is not on Live floor: its tiles are Agents working, Calls today, Customers reached, Successes (or Will pay) and Late callbacks; talk time stays on the campaign pages (the agents' performance table, Insights' handle time) | **Default, pending Manas** (built 2026-11-18, Phase 1) |
| LF2 | Will pay and the currency. Promise amounts carry no currency (PT5), so the tile and the campaign cards show the amount with no symbol, in en-IN grouping ("2.4 lakh promised today"). Proposed: an account currency setting, default INR, so the console can show "₹2.4 lakh". With no active campaign whose catalogue records promises (`promise_campaign_ids` empty) the tile is Successes | **Open, Manas** |
| LF3 | Customer names on the agent cards. The floor read (B28b) gives every `agency.supervise` holder the name of the customer on each agent's call, without the listening consent flag (LS1): it is not listening. Never the number | **Open, Manas** |
| LF4 | Need help (Phase 3; the station's control is still Coming soon until the console and app changes land). Decided (Manas, 2026-11-19), as proposed: every supervisor in the account is alerted; the request clears on a response, on cancel or after 15 minutes; a chime on by default, mutable per browser. **Built on the server (2026-11-19):** the agent raises it on their own live session with `POST /proxy/agency/sessions/:id/help` and cancels it with `DELETE` (`agency.station.connect`; anyone else's session is the agent routes' uniform 404), on a call or in wrap-up (LF13); it is the session row's `help_raised_at`, `help_call_id`, `help_answered_at`, `help_answered_by` (`0052_floor_assist.sql`). Every `agency.supervise` holder in the account is alerted **through the floor read alone**: `GET /proxy/agency/floor`'s `AgencyFloorAgent.help` (`{ raised_at, call_id }`, null once answered, cancelled, expired or ended, and on every row while the flag is off, LF11), which the console polls every 5 s; there is no supervisor push channel (a Need help can take up to 5 s to appear). It clears exactly once, on the first of: a supervisor's listen on the agent's call going live or their message reaching the agent's station (LF14), the agent's cancel, `AGENCY_HELP_EXPIRY_MS` (15 minutes) after the raise (LF12), or the session ending; the station hears `help_answered` and `help_cleared` (LF15); each end is one audit row (LF18); expiries are alerted (`agy-help-unanswered`, LF19). The chime and the card are the console's | **Decided, Manas 2026-11-19** (server built 2026-11-19) |
| LF5 | Talk to agent (Phase 3; the console's controls are still Coming soon until its change lands). Decided (Manas, 2026-11-19), as proposed: text messages over the station socket (`supervisor_message`), not voice; kept 90 days for audit; Send reminder is the same route with prefilled words. **Built on the server (2026-11-19):** `POST /proxy/agency/sessions/:id/messages` (`agency.supervise`, then the account scope, then the flag), body `{ text }`, trimmed, 1 to 500 characters (`400 message_invalid`), answers `201 { id, session_id, sent_at, delivered: true }` once the frame `supervisor_message { id, text, from_name, sent_at }` was written to the agent's live station (console or app); with none that can receive it, `409 agent_not_live` (LF16, LF17). Kept in `agency_supervisor_messages` (tenant, account, session, agent, sender, text, `sent_at`, `delivered`), deleted after 90 days by the retention purge on every run (`AGENCY_SUPERVISOR_MESSAGE_RETENTION_DAYS`); one audit row per message (LF18). A message that reaches an agent whose Need help is open answers it (`how: 'message'`) | **Decided, Manas 2026-11-19** (server built 2026-11-19) |
| LF6 | Break limits (Phase 3). Decided (Manas, 2026-11-19), as proposed: an optional `limit_minutes` per break reason; defaults Tea 15, Lunch 30, others none; without one the floor's 30-minute rule (`long_break`) applies; reserved reasons never get one. **Built on the server 2026-11-19** (LF20): `AgencyBreakReason.limit_minutes`, refused at the campaign-config write out of 1–240 whole minutes or on a reserved reason; the built-ins' Tea 15 and Lunch 30; the floor read's `break_limit_minutes`; the bootstrap's `break_reasons` carry each limit for the station's countdown. The console's `long_break` (`utils/agencyAgentFloor.ts`) and the editor in campaign Settings › Agents follow | **Decided, Manas 2026-11-19** (server built) |
| LF7 | Give late callbacks to free agents (Phase 3: a Coming soon control on the late-callbacks row until the console follows). Decided (Manas, 2026-11-19), as proposed: on `strict` and `preferred`, assign the overdue callbacks whose owner is away to agents live on the campaign, fewest due first (CA5's fallback, now); not offered on `pool`. **Built on the server 2026-11-19** (LF21): the operation `late_callbacks_to_live_agents` on `POST /proxy/agency/campaigns/:id/assignments` (preview, then commit with its token), `409 late_callbacks_pool_mode` on `pool`, audited `agency_contacts.assigned`; the account-wide callbacks list beside it (LF22) | **Decided, Manas 2026-11-19** (server built) |
| LF8 | Join call and whisper (Phase 4, not built: Coming soon controls on the cards). Reopens live listening's "listen only" (`listening.md`): the supervisor's microphone mixed into the bridge (both legs to join, the agent's leg to whisper), a new permission (`agency.calls.join`), a consent flag as LS1, recording and the agent's notice. To decide after Phase 2 is in use | **Open, Manas** |
| LF9 | Needs you now's order (`HOME_RANKS`, `homeRank` in `utils/agency/home.ts`): Need help (Phase 3), then what stopped a campaign dialling (a stall in the danger tone: automatic pauses, the DNC check, caller IDs, carrier capacity), a working agent whose screen has been disconnected for more than 20 seconds (agent station BE-11, added 2026-11-20: `disconnectedItems`, the alerts read's own rule, so Live floor, where the shell's banner is hidden, lists what the banner lists; danger tone, no "Got it" since it clears itself when the screen is back, never on the sidebar badge), break overruns (Phase 2, LF10), late callbacks, the other dangers, the warnings, then information; campaign order within a rank. The campaigns still on the default calling hours are one information row ("3 campaigns still use the default calling hours", Review lists each to `…/settings#calling-hours`, `groupCallingHours`). Its "Got it" is held per campaign (amended 2026-11-19, PR review): it puts aside each campaign the row covers under that campaign's own row id (`calling-hours-default:<id>`, the ids Home's per-campaign rows always had), the row shows only the campaigns not put aside and is itself put aside when all are, and a campaign newly on the default hours brings it back, for that campaign. A campaign filter changes which campaigns the row covers, never what was put aside (`attentionFor` splits the rows before it groups them). It used to be held under one grouped id against the group's count, which the filter changed: put aside with one campaign picked, it came back as soon as the filter was cleared. Late callbacks whose owner is away carry a disabled "Give to free agents" marked Coming soon (Phase 3's action) beside the link to the campaign's Callbacks tab. "Got it", the badge's count, the stale rules and the never-an-all-clear rule are B28a's, unchanged | **Default, pending Manas** (built 2026-11-18, Phase 1) |
| LF10 | Phase 2 on the console (B28b's reads). Breaks over the floor's one limit (30 minutes, the campaign floor's `long_break`; never the soft phone) are Needs you now rows ranked `break-overrun`, "Priya M on Tea for 42 min", with a coming-soon Send reminder; "Got it" holds per break (its start is in the row's kind) until it runs another 15 minutes. They are **not** on the sidebar badge, which reads the shared board alone on every page; the floor is read on Live floor alone (5 s), so a badge counting them would change its number with the page. The page's own Needs you now count includes them. Will pay's amount has **no currency** (none is stored, PT5, pending LF2): the exact sum (BigInt hundredths) said in lakh and crore, "2.4 lakh promised today", or the plain amount under a lakh; "No amounts recorded" when no promise has one. With a campaign picked, the cards are that campaign's sessions (an absent agent or a soft-phone session names none) and Agents working is its rows on shift of its staffed agents. **Review fixes (2026-11-18):** a row with a call in progress is On a call whatever its session's state (a soft-phone session stays `break`/`soft_phone` through its manual calls); a soft-phone session with no call is "On soft phone", a chip of its own shown only while anyone is on it, never a break; Make available is offered on a card only when stuck (past the wrap-up window and its grace, or an inbound outcome owed off wrap-up), never on an ordinary inbound wrap-up; a card counts "N conversations today" (dialler conversations, its tooltip says so; the drawer opened from it says "Conversations today"); break rows count on against the page's clock (30 s at first; from the cleanup refactor of 2026-11-19 the floor's one 5 s risk clock, which also ranks the cards, so a card's long break and its row cross the limit together, and a risk shows at most 5 s late) and are marked not refreshed with the floor; late callbacks past the board's first 25 active campaigns read "4+" on the tile and the badge ("at least 4 callbacks overdue") and "At least …" on the Callbacks page; a card in wrap-up says "After the call" (or "After an inbound call"), not the last customer's name: the floor read carries a call only while it is bridged. **Second review (2026-11-18):** the Make-available drawer reads its agent from every floor and closes once the session is gone or no longer offers it; the cards' clocks, the risks and the break rows are measured on the server's clock (the floor read's offset, as the Live calls list's time on call); a break row's "Got it" holds for 15 minutes from the Got it (its count is the break's seconds); with the floor unread (loading, failed, refused or stale) Needs you now never gives an all-clear but says the breaks were not checked; "N more things on other campaigns" leaves out rows put aside; Retry reads the board, the floor and the promises again, and the Agents panel has its own; Today so far and the promises read again at local midnight; the Callbacks page says no callback is late only when every active campaign was read and known, in its own error and refusal words | **Default, pending Manas** (built 2026-11-18, Phase 2) |
| LF11 | Floor assist's flag, `agency_floor_assist_enabled` (`packages/contracts/src/flags.ts`, the server registry): **off by default**; a super admin turns it on per tenant or per account with an override row (`FF_AGENCY_FLOOR_ASSIST` sets the global default, dev and staging). It gates Need help's raise and cancel and supervisor messages (`403 floor_assist_disabled`, checked after the permission and the account scope) and the floor read's `help` (null on every row while off, or while the flag cannot be read: never the floor a 503; the floor reads it with the listening flags from one snapshot of the account's flags per poll). It does not gate break limits, giving late callbacks to free agents or the callbacks list (their gates are permissions). Client-exposed (the console and the app show their controls from it). An ordinary flag with **no handling policy**: neither surface hears or records a call, so a bulk rollout and a global On are allowed. A flag read that throws fails the request closed (a 500), as the dialer flag does | **Default, pending Manas** (built 2026-11-19) |
| LF12 | Need help's expiry is read, not waited for: a request is open only while raised, not answered and raised less than `AGENCY_HELP_EXPIRY_MS` (15 minutes, a contracts constant) ago **on the database's clock** (`now()`), and every read applies it (the floor, the station's `ready.help`, the raise, cancel and answer). The station's `ready.help` is read with the repository's own rule (`openForSession`), never from the attach's row on the server's clock: read after the new socket is registered and beside the rehydrate, so a request cleared before that read is not shown; one cleared between that read and `ready` has its `help_cleared` delivered to the new socket first, and the console and the app ignore a `ready.help` whose `raised_at` they have already seen cleared, so it is not put back on screen (the server keeps no memory of it); a failed read omits the field. A sweep in the primary every 15 s (`HELP_SWEEP_INTERVAL_MS`, and once at boot) clears the expired ones, which is what sends the agent's station `help_cleared { expired }` and writes the audit row, at most one interval late; a raise, cancel, answer or session end that finds its session's request past the expiry expires it in the same statement instead (and says so, audited as `expired`), so an expired request is never overwritten or answered unaudited. Every transition is one statement on the session row (`WITH prior AS MATERIALIZED (… FOR UPDATE)`, returning the request as it found it), so each request ends exactly once whoever races for it. The session ending (the agent's leave, or an idle session ended from another tenant's join, OS7) clears an open request (`session_ended`) before the station is detached; a session whose station dropped but did not leave keeps it until it expires | **Default, pending Manas** (built 2026-11-19) |
| LF13 | Who may raise Need help, and when. The session's own agent only (`agency.station.connect`, `requireOwnedSession`: a colleague's, another account's or another tenant's session, and a supervisor's raise for an agent, are the uniform 404). Only on a call or in wrap-up: the agent's lease is `on_call` or `wrapup` (the session row's state when Redis cannot answer), or the session holds a soft-phone call (live, or ended and owing its outcome) or a connected inbound call; otherwise `409 help_not_on_call`. Raising again while one is open changes nothing and answers the open one (no second audit row). `call_id` is the call the agent is bridged to at the raise (`agency_calls.id`, what Listen takes, on any of the three spines), null in wrap-up; a failed read of it raises without one. A cancel with nothing open answers `help: null` too. **Changed by the agent station's BE-9 (AS5, 2026-11-20):** a raise is accepted in any state of the live session (joined and not left: `offline` after joining, `available`, `reserved`, `on_call`, `wrapup`, `break`); the state is recorded with the request (`agency_agent_sessions.help_state`, 0059, cleared in every statement that clears `help_raised_at`) and served as `AgencySessionHelp.state_at_raise` (a soft-phone call counts as `on_call` while live and `wrapup` while it owes its outcome, a connected inbound call as `on_call`); `help_not_on_call` stays in the union for older servers and is no longer sent. With no call, a supervisor's message answers it (LF14) | **Default, pending Manas** (built 2026-11-19; BE-9 2026-11-20) |
| LF14 | What answers Need help: a supervisor's **listen going live** on that agent's call (the listener socket admitted and `ready` sent, not the grant: a grant whose socket never opens answers nothing), on any call of the session, and a supervisor's **message the agent's station took** (a message refused `agent_not_live` answers nothing). Phase 4's Join will be a third. An answered request is cleared from the floor at once (`help: null`): the floor shows no "answered" state; who answered is on the row (`help_answered_at`, `help_answered_by`) and the audit row. Raising again after an answer is a new request. An answer is stamped at the later of its own `now()` and the raise (`GREATEST(now(), help_raised_at)`): `now()` is the answering transaction's start, and an answer that waited on the row while the agent raised again over a request past the expiry answers the new request, never before it was raised (`ck_agency_agent_sessions_help`) | **Default, pending Manas** (built 2026-11-19) |
| LF15 | What the agent's station hears. `help_answered { raised_at, by_name, how }` then `help_cleared { raised_at, reason: 'answered' }` on an answer; `help_cleared { raised_at, reason }` alone on a cancel (`cancelled`), an expiry (`expired`) and the session ending (`session_ended`). Every frame carries the `raised_at` of the request it is about, so a client clears only the request it shows and a late frame never clears a newer one; `help_cleared` is the one frame that ends the request on screen, `help_answered` says who and how. `by_name` and a message's `from_name` are the user's display name, trimmed and cut to 120 code points as LF17 cuts the sender's (`senderNameForFrame`, a listen's answerer's name too), null when it cannot be read or is blank (the client says "Your supervisor"). The raise itself sends no frame (the HTTP answer says it). A reconnecting station is told an open request on `ready` (`ready.help`). A frame no station took is not resent: the row is the state | **Default, pending Manas** (built 2026-11-19) |
| LF16 | Which stations get the floor assist frames: a console always; a phone only when its station upgrade declares the capability `floor_assist`, so an app build that cannot show a message is never sent one, and a message to it is `409 agent_not_live` rather than a message nobody sees (as the inbound frames, IQ46). **Staged for the app:** the three frames are their own union (`AgencyStationFloorAssistFrame`, `packages/contracts/src/agency/floor-assist.ts`), not yet in `AgencyStationServerFrame`, and `floor_assist` is not yet in `AGENCY_STATION_CAPABILITIES`, because both are vocabularies the app's wire package must decode (`fixtures/agent/literals.json`). The app's Phase 3 change moves them in, with its Dart types, fixtures and literals, in one commit; until then no phone can declare it and every message to a phone is refused `agent_not_live`. **Done (2026-11-19, the app's Phase 3):** the frames are members of `AgencyStationServerFrame` (the dialer's and the console's), `floor_assist` is in `AGENCY_STATION_CAPABILITIES`, and the app declares it on every upgrade (LF34) | **Default, pending Manas** (built 2026-11-19) |
| LF17 | Messages. `agency.supervise` only (agents and operators get the RBAC 403; an agent cannot message their own station). The text's line breaks are made `\n` (`\r\n` and a lone `\r`), then it is trimmed and holds 1 to 500 characters counted as Unicode code points (`AGENCY_SUPERVISOR_MESSAGE_MAX_CHARS`; a Telugu or emoji message counts what it shows), plain text, never markup. A text holding a control character is **refused**, never stripped (`400 message_invalid`, at the public layer and the internal handler alike, `normaliseMessageText`): the C0 controls but tab and line feed (`U+0000`, which Postgres cannot store, among them), DEL, and a lone UTF-16 surrogate; refused rather than stripped so the agent reads exactly what the supervisor wrote. The sender's name is read on the server and cut for the frame to 120 code points with an ellipsis (`senderNameForFrame`), never a reason to refuse the message. A session outside the caller's account is the uniform 404; a session that has ended, or one with no station attached in the process that holds it, is `409 agent_not_live` and nothing is stored. A message is stored (`delivered` false) before its frame is sent and marked delivered once the socket took it; one the socket refused in between stays undelivered and the route answers `409 agent_not_live`. Once the socket took it the message has been delivered, so nothing after fails the send: the supervisor gets the `201`; the mark is tried twice and, failing both, logged and counted (`agency_supervisor_message_mark_failures_total`); the metric, the audit row and the Need help answer are each attempted whatever the others did (the mark and the answer run together, the answer naming the sender the message already carried). So a row reading `delivered` false is a message no station took, **or** (rarely, and counted) one a station took whose mark failed twice; the audit row's `delivered` is the socket's answer either way. The internal handler is `POST /supervisor-messages` (owner: the body's `session_id`), outside `/sessions/:id`, whose internal routes are the session's own agent's (Q8), as the listen grant is | **Default, pending Manas** (built 2026-11-19) |
| LF18 | The record. One audit row per Need help request at each end: `agency_session.help_raised` (the agent), then exactly one of `help_cancelled` (the agent), `help_answered` (the supervisor; `details.how`), `help_expired` or `help_session_ended` (the system actor), each with `raised_at` and `call_id`, filed under the session and its campaign (so they show on the campaign's History); `agency_session.message_sent` per stored message (the supervisor; the message id, the agent, whether a station took it and its length, **never the text**, which stays in `agency_supervisor_messages`). Messages are deleted 90 days after they were sent by the retention purge on every run, whatever `AGENCY_RETENTION_DAYS` says (a fixed policy, LF5); the audit rows follow `AUDIT_RETENTION_DAYS` (85 by default), so a message's audit row can go up to five days before the message | **Default, pending Manas** (built 2026-11-19) |
| LF19 | The alert. Need help requests left to expire are counted (`agency_help_requests_cleared_total{reason="expired"}`, beside `agency_help_requests_raised_total`, the other reasons, `agency_help_answer_seconds`, `agency_supervisor_messages_total{result}` and `agency_supervisor_message_mark_failures_total`, LF17), and `agy-help-unanswered` warns (Slack, never pages) on any expiry in 30 minutes: an agent left asking for 15 minutes with a customer on the line is worth a word to the floor's lead, not an outage. No dashboard panel yet; the runbook is `docs/operations.md`, "Alert runbooks" | **Default, pending Manas** (built 2026-11-19) |
| LF20 | Break limits as built (LF6). **The rule:** `limit_minutes` is a whole number of minutes, 1 to 240 (`isAgencyBreakLimitMinutes`, contracts `agency/breaks.ts`); out of range, a fraction, a string or `null` is refused at the campaign-config write, one `break_reasons` issue per entry naming its code (leave the key out for no limit), by the internal handler and the public layer alike (`validateBreakReasons`). A reserved reason (`RESERVED_BREAK_REASONS`) is refused from the catalog altogether, as before, so it can carry no limit: one refusal, not two. A stored limit the rule refuses (written by hand or before the rule) is dropped from its entry when the catalog is read (`resolveBreakReasons`), never the entry. **The defaults:** Tea is a new built-in code, `tea` ("Tea", 15), after Break, not a relabelled `break` (a session on `break` keeps what it chose); Lunch gets 30; the others none. The built-ins are served at read time, so every campaign that lists no reasons of its own (an empty column, every campaign until an operator edits its list) gets them at once, and a campaign's saved list is never rewritten, by a migration or by the read: it keeps the limits its operator gave it (none, until they add some). **Who reads it:** the floor row's `break_limit_minutes` (`breakLimitMinutes` over the session's campaign catalog; null when not on a break, for a reason with none, a reserved code or a code the catalog does not know); the station's bootstrap `break_reasons`, so the console station and the app find the limit of the break they are on by its code, as they find its label, and count down from `agent_state.since` (the anchor they count a break's time from already); a limit edited mid-shift reaches an agent at their next join. The long-break risk stays the console's (`floorRisk`): the reason's limit, else 30 minutes, never the soft phone | **Default, pending Manas** (built 2026-11-19, Phase 3 server) |
| LF21 | Give late callbacks to free agents as built (LF7). **Where:** an operation of the assignment routes, `{ kind: 'late_callbacks_to_live_agents', group_same_number? }`, previewed then committed with the preview's token like every operation (the console's "Give 4 late callbacks to free agents?" is the preview), behind the routes' own gate (`agency.supervise`, the campaign in the caller's account, the `agency_contact_assignment_enabled` flag, without which no campaign is strict or preferred), never the Live floor's assist flag. Its types sit beside `AgencyAssignmentOperation`, not in it (`AgencyLateCallbacksToLiveAgents`, `…Preview` with `live_agent_user_ids`, `…Result`, `…StaleRefusal`, `…Refusal`), so the roster's menus keyed by `AgencyAssignmentKind` keep the supervisor's seven. **What moves:** a `pending` callback (the owner's `callback` bucket, booked or inbound) due at or before now (CA5's selection with no grace) whose owner has no session present on the campaign (joined and not `offline`: CA5's and the progress read's `live`; not the board's 15-minute absence, so the preview's count can differ from the late-callbacks row's for an agent gone a few minutes). Never a callback in the pool (one the CA5 sweep already handed there stays there), never a present owner's (on a break counts), never one on a call. **To whom:** the agents with a present session that is **not on a break** (the session's state is not `break`: lunch, tea, `device_away`, Mode B's `away_ring` and every other reason; the button gives them to *free* agents; **Default, pending Manas**, 2026-11-19) AND an active staffing row on the campaign, dealt fewest callbacks due first: each receiver's load is their own callbacks due now on the campaign; the emptiest are raised level first and the remainder goes one each to the fewest at the start (ties by agent id), the oldest callbacks to the emptiest; one number's contacts go together. With nobody live and free (every live agent on a break included), the preview moves nothing and lists no one. An owner on a break is still present and keeps their callbacks (CA5's presence, unchanged): on a break, an agent neither gives nor receives. **Refused:** `409 late_callbacks_pool_mode` on a `pool` campaign, preview and commit. **Recorded:** one `agency_contacts.assigned` audit row (`kind`, batch, counts, `per_agent`) and a history row per contact with reason `reassign` and the supervisor as actor: the 0009 CHECK (a `VARCHAR(20)`) holds no other value and the console labels every reason, so a reason of its own is a later migration with its label. **Concurrency:** a second press, or two at once, moves nothing twice (the commit re-plans under row locks; the second finds nothing and is `409 preview_stale` with a preview moving 0, no audit row); the CA5 sweep and the hand-off never both move a callback (the commit locks its rows `FOR NO KEY UPDATE` and the sweep's pick passes locked rows over; a callback the sweep pooled first is outside the re-plan, so the commit is stale). When the two meet mid-way, the sweep pools the callbacks the commit has not locked yet and the commit is stale: neither moves the rest, which stay with their away owner until the next sweep pass (30 s) or another press; nothing moves twice and nothing is lost | **Default, pending Manas** (built 2026-11-19, Phase 3 server) |
