The page shows supervisor messages too (LF36's rule). The station's own soft phone and inbound views had it from AS5. **(b) A message alert in the app:** one short vibration, never a sound (a sound could reach the customer on a call), when a message first arrives: once per id, once per burst (each message within 2 s of the one before), only while Me → Vibration is on; that switch now also gates the call-arrival and connect buzzes it names (before LF39 it gated nothing), the buzz on a refused Save staying form feedback. The console is unchanged: the banner and the screen-reader announcement. **(c) Telemetry**, the same names and properties from both clients, never text, names or customer details: `agency_help_raised` (`surface`: `station`, `inbound` or `softphone`; `on_call`) on the first raise answer naming a `raised_at` not yet reported, never on a frame; the end events come from the frames, for a request in scope (on screen, a raise in flight, or one the agent pressed Cancel for; a raise whose reply failed is no longer in flight, and a frame counts for a raise in flight only if it names a request raised no earlier than 60 s before the press on the server-aligned clock, so an older request's expiry sent ahead of a new raise's reply counts for nothing); every frame is heard, those about a request off screen included, without changing the screen: `agency_help_answered` (`how`: `listen`, `message`, or `unknown` for a `help_cleared {answered}` with no `help_answered` seen; `wait_bucket`) on `help_answered`, a cancel that lost to an answer included; `agency_help_cancelled` (`wait_bucket`) only on `help_cleared {cancelled}`, the server's proof the cancel won (the cancel's reply is `help: null` either way); `agency_help_expired` on `help_cleared {expired}`; one end event per request, none for `session_ended`. Also `agency_help_refused` (`action`, `code`) per refusal, `agency_supervisor_message_shown` (`on_call`) once per id, and the console's `agency_supervisor_message_sent` (`kind`: `talk`, `reminder` or `coming_to_desk`) after a 201. `on_call` follows the screen: a campaign call once bridged or owing its outcome (the dialler sends no `agent_state` for it), an inbound call once connected or owing its outcome, the soft phone only while its call is connected or owes its outcome (never dialling, queued or ringing). `refused`'s `network` only when no response came back. `wait_bucket` is `agency_floor_intervention`'s buckets on the server-aligned clock | **Decided** (Manas, 2026-11-24; built) |
| LF40 | Live floor's two side-by-side panes, Needs you now and Campaigns, are one component (`FloorPane`): the heading, its count, note, chime and link stay put while the body scrolls inside a fixed height (`min(520px, 70vh)`, `.paneBody`), so a long list never pushes the Agents panel down the page. Rows are ranked most urgent first (LF9), so the top of the box is always the most urgent. While items are out of sight below, the bottom edge fades and "N more items below" ("N more campaigns below") says how many: an item counts until it is fully in view (`paneOverflow`). Pressing it brings the first of them to the top and focuses what it offers, or the box when it offers nothing, so focus is never dropped as the button goes; it glides unless the reader asked for less motion. On a phone-width page (the page's container 600 px or narrower) the cap is lifted: a box that scrolls inside a page that scrolls is harder to use there than a long page. Everything under Needs you now's heading scrolls together (the rows, "N more things on other campaigns", For your information and what was put aside). Not a bell in the top bar: these rows are what is wrong now and clear themselves, the page's work rather than a feed to read, and the sidebar badge already counts them. A top-bar alert for Need help on other pages is a separate question, not built | **Decided, Manas** (2026-11-19, built the same day) |
| LF41 | No `429 help_too_soon` (agent station BE-9's open question). Raise, cancel, raise is not throttled, because it is already bounded where it matters: a raise while one is open changes nothing (the same request, no new id, audit row or metric, LF13); a new request needs the agent's own Cancel first, and each raise and cancel is audited under the agent, so misuse is visible; supervisors hear it through the alerts read (AS16), polled every 5 s, and a console chimes at most once per poll however many new ids it sees, while a request raised and cancelled between two polls is never shown or chimed; each raise is one row update, under the per-user rate limit (`RATE_LIMIT_USER_MAX`, 600 a minute). A throttle would add an error code every app and console build before it shows as an unknown refusal (AS18). Revisit if the audit log shows an agent toggling it | **Default, pending Manas** (fix stream, 2026-11-20) |

## Summary delayed by the AI service (AD)

From 5 to 18 November 2026 the AI vendor refused every transcription on staging (credits used
up). A refused job is requeued without using an attempt, so it stays `queued` and the call
`pending`, and every such call said "We're listening to the call and writing the summary." for up
to 13 days. Decided 2026-11-19 while fixing that copy:

| ID | Decision | Status |
|---|---|---|
| AD1 | The signal. A call's summary is `analysis_delay: 'vendor_unavailable'` when its job is `queued` and the vendor's newest answer was a refusal: the newest `vendor_refused_at` of a queued job with `error_code = 'RATE_LIMITED'` is newer than the newest `completed_at` of any job (none yet reads as never); else null (`analysisDelaySql`, `packages/db`). Both columns are 0054's, each written by one statement: `requeueRateLimited` stamps the refusal (with the code, one constant `RATE_LIMITED_ERROR_CODE`), `completeWithAnalysis` the completion; completion and a manual retry clear the refusal, and a graceful requeue or stale recovery keeps it as old as it was, so a deploy does not make an old refusal new. Not `updated_at`: every write moves it, a heartbeat included. Each side is one probe of a partial index (0055), not a walk of the queue. Not only the call's own job: the vendor and its keys are the server's, and one breaker holds every claim while it refuses (D10), so a job not yet tried (one probe per pause, so they pile up while it lasts) waits for the same reason. Calls of every tenant read the same refusal. It clears at the first summary written after the last refusal, although refused jobs stay queued: they are requeued with the longest backoff and claimed last, so after an outage they drain for hours while summaries are written (a rule on queued `RATE_LIMITED` rows alone would read every pending call delayed through that drain). A later refusal turns it on again, so a rate-limited drain may flick between the two as answers alternate. It can stay on without an outage in narrow cases, since only `completeWithAnalysis` writes the answer side: if the work left after an outage all ends skipped or failed, none completes and the notice stays until the queued refused jobs are gone; with the analysis worker disabled it stays; and the retention purge (by `created_at`) can delete the job holding the newest completion, so an older completion, or none, is compared with the refusal. The breaker's own state is in the analysis worker's memory (a child process), so the routes read the database. Read for a `pending` call only: the attempt detail, the soft-phone detail's media leg, the soft-phone list rows | **Default, pending Manas** |
| AD2 | What reaches a reader. Only the category: never the vendor's words, its quota or billing detail (they stay in `error_message` and the logs, #96). The console says "Summary delayed: the AI service is not available right now. It will be written automatically when it is back." with a clock in place of the spinner, and "Delayed" in the soft-phone list; ordinary pending keeps its copy. Kept for a tenant without `agency.analytics`, like `analysis_status`. The mobile app does not read it | **Default, pending Manas** |

## Truncated recordings (TR)

A transcript covering under 80 % of the call's recorded duration is taken as a truncated fetch (a
carrier serving the file before it is finished) and re-fetched, in the attempt and on later
attempts (`docs/architecture.md`, the analysis runner). Each transcription is paid for, and most
re-fetches of an unfinished file return the very same bytes: before the fingerprint, an unchanged
truncated recording cost up to ~9 transcriptions per job. Fingerprinting each fetch (length and
SHA-256) and never transcribing bytes already found short cut that to 1, but "short" is read from
the transcript itself (the largest `end_seconds` the transcriber returned), and for Gemini those
timestamps are written by the model: a short transcript of a complete file can be the
transcriber's miss (an early stop, squeezed timestamps), which a second transcription of the same
bytes used to rescue. Decided 2026-11-19 on review of that change:

| ID | Decision | Status |
|---|---|---|
| TR1 | Identical audio gets one re-transcription. A fetch whose bytes match the audio last found short is transcribed again while those bytes have come back short only once; once they have come back short twice, in this attempt or the job's earlier ones, a fetch of them is not transcribed and counts as still truncated. Different audio is always transcribed and starts its own count. The fingerprint and the count ride in the `AUDIO_TOO_SHORT` requeue's `error_message` (`[recording sha256=… bytes=… covered=…s shortCount=N]`, a tag without the count read as 1), read only under that error code and only while the call's current duration still finds it short, so any other ending in between starts afresh: a retryable `TRANSCRIPTION_FAILED` (a vendor 5xx or timeout, through `requeueForRetry`), a re-fetch that throws, a rate-limited requeue or a manual retry. The trade-off: an unchanged truncated recording costs 2 transcriptions per unbroken run of attempts ending `AUDIO_TOO_SHORT`, so 2 per job when every attempt ends that way, and at worst 2 per attempt (up to 2 × `DIALER_ANALYSIS_MAX_ATTEMPTS`, more with manual retries) when other endings come between (was ~9 per job before the fingerprint, 1 with it), and a transcriber's miss on a complete file is rescued when the second transcription covers the call; a transcriber that misses twice on the same bytes is not tried a third time, and the job fails `AUDIO_TOO_SHORT` (a manual retry starts afresh) | **Default, pending Manas** |

## Agent station redesign (AS)

The rulings AS1–AS18 (one state per screen, AI last, end call kept protected, target, will pay,
call-later routing unchanged, phone display per campaign, break over-time alerts, device speech,
light by default, Indian English, supervisor alerts on every page, scope, old app builds) are in
[`agent-station.md`](agent-station.md) §1, with the build plan beside them. Decided by Manas
on 2026-11-20. AS19 (supervisors choose the card's facts, 2026-11-21) is there too, with its
review rulings R1–R13 and S1–S16 (§1, "AS19 review rulings"); a comment that cites `AS19 R<n>` or
`AS19 S<n>` resolves there, not to this file's S1 or to the build streams and slices named S1 to S4.
AS20 (More details: every other column of the customer's row under the card, `more_hidden` for
agents only, phone-looking values masked, 2026-11-24) is there too.

## Archived campaigns (AR)

A supervisor archives a draft or a stopped campaign to take it off the Campaigns page; it is listed
on its own Archived campaigns screen (`/agency/campaigns/archived`, linked under the Campaigns
list and found by search) and can be restored. Asked for by Manas on 2026-11-21 ("campaigns in
draft or stopped state should be archivable, and they should come up in a separate screen"); the
rest are defaults decided while building it:

| ID | Decision | Status |
|---|---|---|
| AR1 | Archiving is not a status. `agency_campaigns.archived_at` (0063) marks it, beside `archived_by_user_id` and `archived_by_name`; the campaign keeps `draft` or `stopped`, so nothing that reads `status` (reports, Insights, customer history, the pacing leader, the status badge) changes, and a restore puts it back as it was. The wire carries `archived_at` and `archived_by` (folded like `last_transition_by`) | **Default, pending Manas** |
| AR2 | Only a `draft` or `stopped` campaign is archived (`AGENCY_CAMPAIGN_ARCHIVABLE_STATUSES`); any other is refused 409 `campaign_not_archivable`, one already archived 409 `campaign_archived` (the codes: `AGENCY_CAMPAIGN_ARCHIVE_ERROR_CODES` in `packages/contracts/src/errors.ts`). Not `completed`: only what was asked for (adding it is one word in the list and the CHECK). An archived campaign moves nowhere: Start on an archived draft is refused 409 `campaign_archived` ("Restore it before starting it", also a lifecycle code), including when an archive lands between Start's read and its write; Pause, Resume and Stop get the ordinary `invalid_campaign_transition` (a draft or stopped campaign is a source of none of them); `transitionStatus` requires `archived_at IS NULL`, and the CHECK `ck_agency_campaign_archived_status` is the backstop. The archive's conditional UPDATE and Start cannot both win. The console does not send Start for a draft its preflight re-read finds archived | **Default, pending Manas** |
| AR3 | `GET /campaigns` leaves archived campaigns out unless `archived=true`, which lists only them, newest archived first (`total` narrowed the same way; any other value is a 400). So the Campaigns page, Analytics and ⌘K search's campaign names leave them out, and pickers offer none for a new choice. A picker that already holds one keeps it, marked "(archived)", so it stays visible and can be removed: an inbound route's team, a report subscription being edited, and the Do Not Call list's filter (whose entries still stop that campaign's dials). An archived campaign's own pages, reports and history are unchanged, reached from Archived campaigns, and their breadcrumb goes through it. With every campaign archived, the Campaigns page says so and links there in place of the getting-started guide. The analysis-profile guard still counts an archived draft as a dependent (a restore revives it) and names it "archived draft" | **Default, pending Manas** |
| AR4 | Restore (`POST …/unarchive`) clears all three columns; 409 `campaign_not_archived` on one that is not archived. Offered on the campaign's archived notice and on each Archived campaigns row, with no confirm (nothing is lost by it); Archive asks first and says that nothing is deleted | **Default, pending Manas** |
| AR5 | Who archived it is the session's user, resolved to a display name by the public route as the lifecycle controls are, and shown "Archived on 20 Nov 2026 by Ravi Kumar"; never a caller-supplied id, and an id that is not a UUID is stored as unattributed. Each archive and restore writes a platform audit row (`agency_campaign.archived`, `agency_campaign.restored`) on success only, shown on the campaign's History; the console sends the PostHog event `agency_campaign_archive_action`. "Restore" in the console and the audit is `unarchive` in the routes and the wire | **Default, pending Manas** |
| AR6 | Permission: `agency.campaigns.write` for both, the PATCH's (archiving tidies the account's list and dials or stops nothing, so not `agency.supervise`); the screen reads with `agency.campaigns.read`. Gated on the dialer flag like the PATCH | **Default, pending Manas** |
| AR7 | What an archived campaign may still do. The server refuses only Start (AR2): its settings can be edited, contacts uploaded, agents staffed and a retry campaign created from it (the retry is a new campaign on the Campaigns page). The console offers none of the new work on it (no Add contacts, Retry contacts or Start) until it is restored, but keeps its settings and New campaign (on an ended one). Agents staffed on an archived draft still see it in the app and console as not started; it cannot start until restored. Home's "has any campaign" counts archived ones (an account whose campaigns are all archived is not new) | **Default, pending Manas** |

## Compare agents (CP)

The rulings CP1–CP18 (three agents at most, will-pay as promises made on every call type, no agent
view yet, coaching now and appraisal later, logged-in time with breaks, no worker process to start
with, no list filter, one campaign per comparison, one definition per number, neutral metrics never
flagged, findings only past chance, no language model, analysis metrics with coverage, no free-form
formulas, exports through the report pipeline, no Inbound lens yet, outcome metrics chosen by the
supervisor as sets of outcome codes, direction from the supervisor) are in
[`compare-agents.md`](compare-agents.md) §1. CP1–CP7, CP17 and CP18 decided by Manas on
2026-11-21; CP8–CP16 stand as recommended.

## One call log per campaign (CL)

A call is a call: its type (Dialler, Soft phone, Inbound) is a column, its campaign is a column,
and every list of calls is the same combined read with different filters fixed. Manas approved
the proposal as a whole on 2026-11-22 ("go ahead and build this end-to-end"); the twelve open
decisions in it were accepted with it, not one by one, so each stands as a default until he rules
on it. CL13–CL16 are rulings made while building P2, CL17–CL24 while building P3, CL25–CL30 while building P4, CL31–CL38 while building P5, and CL39–CL42 while building P6, defaults on the same footing. The model, the surfaces, the phases (P1–P6) and what is built:
[`call-log.md`](call-log.md).

| ID | Decision | Status |
|---|---|---|
| CL1 | A soft-phone call placed from a Missed row's Call back keeps the type **Soft phone**, with a "Call back" marker linked to the missed inbound call. It is not relabelled (no "Soft-phone Outbound" type): a missed call and its call back are two calls, one of each type. **Built 2026-11-22 (P2):** the link is `agency_manual_calls.callback_of_inbound_call_id`; how it is kept is CL15 | default, pending Manas |
| CL2 | The setting **Every call must belong to a campaign** is per account, with a default campaign on each inbound route (the campaign an unknown caller is filed under) **Built 2026-11-22 (P3):** the setting is `agency_call_settings.require_campaign` (0071; where it lives and who may change it: CL17), and the route's default is `agency_inbound_routes.default_campaign_id`, the route editor's **Campaign for unknown callers** | default, pending Manas |
| CL3 | The setting is off by default. Switching it on is refused, in the console and by the server, until every active inbound route of the account has a default campaign **Built 2026-11-22 (P3):** `PUT /proxy/agency/call-settings` answers `422 default_campaign_required` naming the routes; a route is refused the same code on a create or a change that leaves it without one while the setting is on. Which routes count, and how the two checks are kept from passing each other: CL18 | default, pending Manas |
| CL4 | An unknown caller gets the route's default campaign when the call arrives, and the agent may change it at wrap-up. A caller matched to a roster contact keeps that contact's campaign; it cannot be changed **Built 2026-11-22 (P3):** the arrival files an unknown caller under the route's default (`campaign_source = 'route'`) whether or not the setting is on. Who may change it, when and to what: CL23. The outcomes such a call uses: CL20 | default, pending Manas |
| CL5 | Supervisors are not exempt from the setting: a call a supervisor places or saves needs a campaign too **Built 2026-11-22 (P3):** `SoftphoneService.placeCall` refuses `campaign_required` before it looks at who is dialling, and the inbound outcome refuses it on behalf as it does for the agent; both are tested with a supervisor | default, pending Manas |
| CL6 | A Do Not Call entry written from a call whose campaign is only where it was filed (an inbound call filed by the route, the agent or a supervisor; a tagged soft-phone call whose number matched no roster contact) is written for the whole account, not for the campaign, as an untagged call's is today **Built 2026-11-22 (P3):** an inbound call's mark was already scoped by its **match** (`matched_campaign_id`), never by where it is filed, so a call filed by the route, the agent or a supervisor writes the account's entry with no change to that rule; the soft phone's scope is now the campaign's only when the call matched a roster contact (`manualDncScope`), which also holds with the setting off. The audit rows and the answers say `account` | default, pending Manas |
| CL7 | "All calls connect rate" is renamed **Outbound connect rate** wherever it shows; the formula does not change. Inbound calls are never added into a rate with outbound calls **Built 2026-11-22 (P4):** the label is one constant, `AGENCY_OUTBOUND_CONNECT_RATE_LABEL`, on the console's Calls tab, the Overview's connect-rate tile ("outbound"), the Performance card, the report's PDF and its workbook Summary; no wire field or metric was renamed (there was none: the rate was never served). A campaign's Calls by type table says "not added" where an all-calls rate would be (CL29) | default, pending Manas |
| CL8 | A catch-all campaign ("General enquiries") is an ordinary campaign. To check when P3 is built: whether a campaign with no contacts completes itself; if it does, a campaign that is some route's default must not auto-complete, by the smallest safe rule, recorded here **Checked and built 2026-11-22 (P3):** it does. Start on a campaign with nothing to dial is refused (`campaign_roster_empty`, `campaign_roster_exhausted`), and the pacing leader completes a running campaign once nothing is outstanding (`PacingEngine.maybeFinalize`), so a catch-all campaign could never be running, and only a running or paused campaign can be chosen on the soft phone. The rule, the smallest that keeps it usable: **while a live inbound route files its unknown callers under a campaign, that campaign may be started with nothing to dial, and is not completed when its list runs out.** It stays running until a supervisor stops it (`stopping → stopped` is untouched). Nothing else about the campaign changes. A route check that fails completes nothing on that tick and is asked again on the next. **The consequence, when the route's default is a real dialling campaign:** it stays `running` after its last contact is dialled, until a supervisor stops it or the route names another campaign; the route editor says so ("The campaign stays running while it is chosen here, even when nobody is left to dial."). **It does not spin (review, 2026-11-22):** a drained campaign held open this way is quiescent, so pacing skips it between safety ticks instead of claiming, counting and asking the routes every round while an agent sits available. Contacts arriving or reopened (`contacts_changed`), any write to the campaign row, and a route taking or dropping it as its default (`campaign_changed`, published by the route's create, change and delete) wake it at the next round; a contact made pending by a path that publishes nothing is found at the safety tick. Its skipped rounds count as `no_contacts` | default, pending Manas |
| CL9 | The agent's My calls tabs (Campaign calls, Soft-phone calls, and Missed calls beside them) merge into one list with type chips and a To follow up chip, in the console and the app **Built 2026-11-22 (P5):** `GET /proxy/agency/my-calls`, the console's `/dialer/attempts` and the app's My calls tab; the chips are To follow up, All, Dialler, Soft phone, Inbound. The Missed calls page and the app's Missed calls list stay beside it (CL36, CL38) | default, pending Manas |
| CL10 | Old calls with no campaign are never backfilled with a guess. They show as "No campaign"; filing them by hand comes later **Built 2026-11-22 (P3):** switching the setting on writes nothing to any call; Settings › Calls counts the last 30 days' calls filed under none (`unfiled_calls`). The "No campaign" filter of All calls is P5 **Built 2026-11-22 (P5):** All calls' and My calls' `campaign_id=none`; the label is "No campaign", or "Before the rule" for a call older than the switch-on (CL35) | default, pending Manas |
| CL11 | The campaign Call log's JSON view is one combined source: dialler attempts and the soft-phone calls logged to the campaign on one keyset, with a `type` filter and per-type counts (`GET /proxy/agency/campaigns/:id/call-log`). Amends SR6's "the Call log's JSON views stay one source each" and IN14's two views | default, pending Manas |
| CL12 | An inbound route's own outcome catalog stays, for calls with no campaign. With the setting on every call has a campaign, so the route's catalog is unused **Built 2026-11-22 (P3):** `resolveInboundCatalog` takes the route's catalog only for a call filed under no campaign; the route editor says so under its outcomes once a campaign for unknown callers is chosen. What a filed call uses instead: CL20 | default, pending Manas |
| CL13 | **How a filter reads on each call type** (P2, built 2026-11-22). A filter of the Call log means on each type what that type's own column says, and a filter with no meaning on a type leaves that type out, rows and counts alike (the rule P1 set for the soft-phone status). On an inbound call: agent is who **answered** (a missed call has no agent, so an agent filter matches no missed call under Inbound or All; *amended 2026-11-22, review:* under To follow up the agent filter reads who the Missed row is **assigned to**, CL14); the outcome code is the outcome saved; `connected` is a call an agent answered and no other attempt outcome matches; phone is the caller's number, caller ID the number they rang; the dates are on `arrived_at`; every filter about a dial (placed, the dialled window, redial, the agent's own dial, reopened, override) and the soft-phone status leave inbound calls out. What happened gains three values only an inbound call matches, in the same `outcome` param: `missed` (any call with a Missed row), `voicemail`, `abandoned_in_queue`, worded "Abandoned (hung up while waiting)" apart from the dialler's "Abandoned (no agent free)". The table is in [`call-log.md`](call-log.md) §4 | default, pending Manas |
| CL14 | **To follow up** (P2, built 2026-11-22) is a view of the Call log, not a type: the campaign's **open** Missed rows, by the Missed list's own rule in one spelling (`inboundMissedOpenSql`). **The dates do not narrow it** (every other filter does): the Call log opens on today, and a caller owed a call back two days ago is still owed one. Its rows are inbound calls, so `counts.all` does not add them a second time. *(Amended 2026-11-22, review:)* under this view the agent filter matches the row's assignee (`follow_up_assignee_user_id`, the Missed list's own), for the rows and the count: nobody answered a missed call, and who still owes these is the question asked. While the chip is on the console switches the date fields off and says why. Hidden at 0; shown beside All even when no other type chip is. Mark handled and Call back on its rows are the Missed list's own actions and routes | default, pending Manas |
| CL15 | **A call back's link is a marker, never a rule** (P2, built 2026-11-22; how CL1's link is kept). `POST /softphone/calls` takes `callback_of_inbound_call_id`; the server keeps it only when it names a Missed row of the caller's account whose caller is the number being dialled (digits compared), and the client sends it only while the pad still holds the number Call back filled, once. A link that does not hold (another account's row, an answered call, a changed number, an unknown id, *and, amended 2026-11-22 after review, a string that is not an id at all: the edge no longer answers 400 for one*) is **dropped and the call is placed all the same**: no refusal code. "Called back" on the missed row is the latest soft-phone call **dialled** from its Call back, whatever campaign it was logged to. A call back does not close the Missed row; reaching the customer or Mark handled does, as before | default, pending Manas |
| CL16 | **An inbound row of a campaign's log** (P2, built 2026-11-22) is an **ended** inbound call **filed under** the campaign (`agency_inbound_calls.campaign_id`, 0069) that we served: a live call is on Live calls, a `rejected` one is never a row, and one filed under no campaign is in no campaign's log. It sits in the list at the time it **arrived**. `campaign_id` is the matched contact's campaign at arrival (`campaign_source = 'contact'`); `matched_campaign_id` keeps its meaning for the sticky agent, the contact's callback and the outcome catalog. Existing matched calls were backfilled inside the migration, as 0027 and 0032 backfilled this table (one row per inbound call; only matched rows are written), with the batched form for a table past about a million rows run **before** the deploy (the migration's header and `DEPLOYMENT.md` 12; 0069 adds its columns `IF NOT EXISTS` and files only what is still unfiled, and its CHECK is validated by 0070), the same batch being the repair after a rollback; unmatched calls were not given a campaign (CL10) | default, pending Manas |
| CL17 | **Where the setting lives, and who may change it** (P3, built 2026-11-22). Its own table, `agency_call_settings`, one optional row per (tenant, account); no row is the setting off. Not a column of `agency_softphone_settings` (the rule governs inbound calls as much as the soft phone, and an account may have inbound on with the soft phone off), and not of `account_settings` (the operator's row: limits and toggles a super admin sets, read through a 60-second cache and written with a concurrency version; this is an account admin's switch that every dial reads fresh). A new permission, **`agency.calls.manage`**, floored at `account_admin` as `agency.softphone.manage` and `agency.inbound.write` are; no floor was widened. The routes (`GET` / `PUT /proxy/agency/call-settings`) are behind neither the soft phone's flag nor inbound's. Agents learn the setting from what they already read: the soft phone's bootstrap (`campaign_required`) and the inbound call (`campaign_required`) | default, pending Manas |
| CL18 | **What stands in the way of switching it on** (P3, built 2026-11-22). Every **live** inbound route of the account (not deleted), with or without numbers: a route with none answers nothing today, but would the moment a number is attached, with no second check. The switch-on and every inbound route write take one transaction advisory lock on the account before they check each other, so a route cannot be saved without a campaign in between. Switching off, and saving "on" over "on", are never refused. A route's campaign for unknown callers is one of the account's campaigns that is **not archived**, in any status: a call is filed under it whatever it is doing, as a matched caller's is. Agents on no running campaign are listed for information and block nothing. **Amended after review (2026-11-22):** (a) a route whose campaign can no longer be chosen counts as having none: it was **archived** since, or is no longer a campaign of the account. It stands in the way of the switch and is listed on the page, and the route is not saved again, whatever the change, until another campaign is chosen (the check runs on the campaign the route will hold, not only on a changed one). A stopped or completed campaign still counts as chosen. (b) **While inbound is off for the account (`agency_inbound_enabled`) its routes are not counted**, by the switch or by the page: the route editor answers `403 inbound_disabled` then, so a leftover route could not be given a campaign and the setting could never go on. Nothing is lost: a call that arrives filed under no campaign is still refused its outcome (`campaign_required`), and once inbound is on again the page names the route and its next save needs a campaign. The lock is pinned by tests that hold it on a second connection | default, pending Manas |
| CL19 | **Calls that began before the switch finish as they are** (P3, built 2026-11-22). The row keeps when the setting was last switched on (`require_campaign_since`). An inbound call that **arrived** before it takes its outcome with no campaign, as before; one that arrives at or after it is refused `campaign_required` until it is filed. A soft-phone call is checked once, at the dial: a call already placed with no campaign is hung up and written up as any other. The lapse of an owed outcome (`no_disposition`) is never held back by the setting. Nothing is written to any existing call (CL10) | default, pending Manas |
| CL20 | **The outcomes of a call filed under a campaign its caller is not on** (P3, built 2026-11-22). They are that campaign's (`catalog_source: 'campaign'` on the panel), **without the outcomes that ask for a promise to pay**: there is no contact to keep a promise on, the soft phone's `promise_needs_contact`, decided once on the server so the panel never offers what the save would refuse; a catalog left empty by that is the built-in one. Everything about the **customer** stays with the match: the sticky agent, the contact's settlement and callback, the promise, and the Do Not Call scope (CL6) read `matched_campaign_id`, never `campaign_id`. The outcome is stored with `disposition_source = 'route'`: 0027's CHECK says `campaign` exactly when the caller matched a campaign's list, and it is kept as it is so an older image's lapse write stays valid after a rollback; which campaign the call is under is on the row (`campaign_id`, `campaign_source`) | default, pending Manas |
| CL21 | **Stopping or archiving a campaign that receives unknown callers asks first, and does not refuse** (P3, built 2026-11-22). The Stop and Archive confirmations name the inbound numbers ("This campaign receives unknown callers on Sales line. Choose another campaign for that route first.", `GET /proxy/agency/campaigns/:id/inbound-default-routes`, `agency.supervise`). The supervisor may go on: Stop is the off switch and is never held behind another page, and a call goes on being filed under the campaign whatever its status. A stopped campaign cannot be chosen on the soft phone, so the route should be given another; an archived one is refused the next time the route is saved (CL18) | default, pending Manas |

The console's agent station does not say "campaign" (AS15: its word is "work"), so the proposal's "Log to (required)" and "Choose a campaign to log this call to." are **"Save this call to (required)"** and **"Choose the work to save this call to."** there; "You are not in any work that is running, so you cannot dial. Ask your supervisor to add you to one."; "This call was not saved to any work. Choose one to call again."; and at the end of an inbound call **"Filed under: General enquiries (this number is not on any list)"** with **Change**, then "What you can choose changed to match Gold Loan Renewals." The agent app already says "Log to" and "campaign", so it uses the proposal's words as they are. Settings › Calls, the route editor and the Stop and Archive warnings are an admin's and use the proposal's words. Two links differ from the mock-up: "Open Team" is **Open campaigns** (agents are added to a campaign on its own Team tab, not in Settings › Team), and "See them in All calls" waits for P5, which builds that page | default, pending Manas |
| CL23 | **Who may file an unknown caller's call under another campaign, and when** (P3, built 2026-11-22). At the end of the call only: it ended after it was answered and its outcome is not saved (`campaign_changeable`). The agent who took it may choose a campaign they may log a soft-phone call to (running or paused, in the account, one they are staffed on: one rule, `findTaggableCampaign`), stored `campaign_source = 'agent'`; someone with `agency.supervise` any running or paused campaign of the account, on behalf, `'supervisor'`. Either with the outcome (`campaign_id` on `POST …/disposition`, filed in the outcome's transaction, the code checked against the new campaign's outcomes) or before it (`POST …/campaign`, which answers the call with the new outcomes; audited `agency_inbound_call.campaign_changed`). A matched caller's campaign is refused (`inbound_campaign_fixed`); a campaign the actor may not log to is refused (`inbound_campaign_not_allowed`). A **missed** call from an unknown caller is filed by its route alone and is not moved: the Missed list names that campaign, and Call back logs the soft-phone call to it when the agent may. **A Change that lands under a Save (review, 2026-11-22):** the outcome's code is judged against the catalog of the campaign the call was under when it was read, so the save holds only while the call is still under that campaign (`saveDisposition`'s `expectedCampaignId`). When a supervisor's Change got in between, nothing is written and the code is judged once more against the campaign the call is under now: saved there, or refused `inbound_disposition_invalid` (`unknown_code`, with that campaign's codes), the refusal both clients already answer by showing the new outcomes. No new wire code | default, pending Manas |

The Insights rollup was not given columns: it is a pure function of `agency_call_attempts`, recomputed from a statement trigger on that table, and holds the dialler's figures alone; an inbound call is not an attempt, and a second trigger and a second table's buckets would be a new rollup, not a column. Counted by `campaign_id`, never `matched_campaign_id` or `disposition_source`. | CL26 | **What the inbound counts are** (P4, built 2026-11-22). `received`: every inbound row of the campaign's log that arrived in the window (half-open on `arrived_at`). `answered`: an agent took it (the Call log's "Connected" on an inbound row). `missed`: it left a Missed row, open or handled since (the Call log's "Missed"). `talk_seconds`: the calls' talk time. **`open_hours` is added to the spec's five**: the calls that arrived while the line was open (received less those answered with the closed message), the denominator of **Answer rate, open hours**, which is the inbound summary's own definition (`inboundOpenHoursSql`, shared with it) under the page's one floor of 25. On an inbound call, **Reached a person is answered**: nobody rings us by machine, and an inbound call has no voicemail outcome an agent writes | default, pending Manas |

It is the campaign's open Missed rows by the Call log's To follow up rule (CL14), which ignores the dates, so the figure and the chip are the same number whatever range is chosen, and the figure links to the chip. It can therefore be larger than the window's missed calls ("2 (5 to follow up)"): the table's note says "To follow up counts every missed call of this campaign still owed a call back now, whatever the dates", and the report says "when this report was made". The alternative, the window's missed calls still open, would disagree with the chip beside it and hide a caller owed a call since last week **Amended after review, 2026-11-22:** the Inbound column also shows while the campaign has calls still to follow up, even when the range (or the report's window) holds no inbound call and inbound is off: a quiet week must not hide callers owed a call back | default, pending Manas |

| CL30 | **One row, one call, one type, in figures** (P4, built 2026-11-22). **OPEN QUESTION FOR MANAS: this departs from the proposal's literal words in one row.** The proposal says "a dial stopped because the customer rang us counts once, as Inbound". As built, that is true of Connected, Reached a person, Talk time and the outcomes, and **not of the Calls row**: there the stopped dial is counted under Dialler and the customer's call under Inbound, two calls. Why: the stopped dial was placed and rang, the Call log lists it as a Dialler row, and it is in the dialler's Dials and in the connect rate's first dials today; taking it out of the Calls row would make the table's Dialler column disagree with the Dials figure and the Dialler chip beside it, and taking it out of Dials would move the dialler's connect rate, which P4 may not. If Manas wants the literal reading, the choice is between showing a Dialler count that differs from Dials by the stopped dials, or changing what a dial is for the dialler's own rate. The rest of the ruling: the Dialler column's Calls is the **Dials** figure (placed dials, by when they were dialled), so it is the number its own link opens on the Call log's Dialler chip. A **dial stopped because the customer rang us** was placed and rang, so it stays a dial (the standing rule, `docs/insights.md` "System dispositions"; removing it would move the dialler's connect rate, which P4 may not); it is no connect, no conversation, no person reached and no outcome, and the customer's conversation is counted once, under Inbound. So "counts once, as Inbound" is true of Connected, Reached a person, Talk time and the outcomes; in Calls the stopped dial and the customer's call are the two rows the Call log lists. A missed call and its call back are two calls (an inbound row and a soft-phone row); a call back that never dialled is none (SR2); marking a Missed row handled changes `open_follow_ups` and nothing else. The Call log's Dialler chip under plain dates also lists attempts that were never dialled, which no figure counts; under the figure's link (`placed=1`, the dialled window) the two are equal | default, pending Manas |

The console no longer reads `my-attempts` at all.)* Stricter would be a Call back that needs no number; that is a change to the soft phone's dial, not made here | default, pending Manas |

When the Missed badge's live count says a number the list did not read, the console and the app read what is owed again, so the two badges agree; a supervisor's console badge counts the account's rows and is not followed.)* Each callback says "Due 4:00 PM" or "Late by 2 hours" (`agencyCallbackDueLabel`: minutes, hours, days) | default, pending Manas |

| CL37 | **One index, measured first** (P5, built 2026-11-22). `0072`: `idx_agency_attempts_account_created (tenant_id, account_id, created_at DESC, id DESC)`. Without it an account's page of attempts was a scan of the table and a sort. Nothing was added for the other branches: the soft-phone calls and the inbound calls had account indexes, an agent's open Missed rows theirs, and "No campaign" and an agent's answered calls are filters over the account's range. An agent's attempts are the account's range filtered to their sessions and bounded below by their first session, so somebody who never dialled reads nothing (the bound drops no row of theirs: `reserved_agent_id` is written only when an attempt is inserted and references a session row that already exists, an attempt's `created_at` and a session's `joined_at` are both the database's `now()` at their insert and never written again, and a rejoin reuses the session row without moving `joined_at` or its account); with a campaign named (checked to be the account's first) they are the campaign Call log's own agent filter, and the answered inbound calls are read on the campaign's index. Not added, and the two reads that grow with the account's history because of it: an index by session and time on attempts (a second index on the busiest write path; an agent who dials rarely walks the account's range since their first session), and an index by answerer on inbound calls (somebody who has answered few walks the account's inbound range). Neither is slower than `my-attempts`, which My calls read until now and which scans the attempts table | default, pending Manas |

The attempts list, the soft-phone calls list, their filter bars (day, outcome, campaign, number, status) and their detail sheets are removed with the segments; a card carries the notes. *(2026-11-22, review: so the app has no read-only page for one soft-phone call any more: its call fields, its override note and who saved its outcome are not shown in the app.)* The Missed calls list is one tap from To follow up, unchanged. Call back from a card reads the Missed row (by its id, since the review of 2026-11-22) when pressed and is the Missed list's own Call back from there. An app build from before P5 keeps its three segments on the old routes, which are still served, and so still shows numbers in full | default, pending Manas |
| CL39 | **Which inbound calls are in a number's history** (P6, built 2026-11-22). Every call **from** the number (`agency_inbound_calls.from_e164`, an exact match on the E.164 the search already looks for: however the **reader** types it, +91, a leading 0, spaces or bare digits, the search looks for the one E.164; what a call is stored under is what the **carrier** sent, to which the arrival, `plivoNumberToE164`, only adds a missing `+`, so a caller sent in full with its country code is found, and one sent in national format or with a trunk 0 would be stored as another number or as withheld and not found: P6 leaves the arrival as it is, the live Plivo checks, `inbound.md` §12 P0, are where the carrier's form is confirmed, and `search-number-agreement.test.ts` pins today's arrival) in an account the caller can see (CH2, the dialer flag as the boundary), that has **ended** and that **we served**: the campaign Call log's own row rule (CL16, `inboundCampaignRowConditions`) **without its campaign term**. So a call **filed under no campaign is a row** here, where no campaign's Call log has it: the history is about the customer, and a customer who rang us rang us whatever the call was filed under. It reads "No campaign". A call that could not be answered (`failed`) is a row, as in the Call log. **Not rows:** a `rejected` call (turned away at the answer for the line's own wiring, never served: the customer history says what happened with the customer, and nothing did); a **live** call, which is in the summary's live calls (`open.live_calls`, channel `inbound`) until it ends, since it has no outcome to list and its row would change under the reader (a call not yet admitted, `arrived`, is in neither: it is refused or admitted within the answer); a call with a **withheld** number (nothing to match); a call with **no account** (refused before it had one: scoped to nobody, CH2). **Inbound's own flag hides nothing**: a history keeps the inbound calls it holds with `agency_inbound_enabled` off, as a campaign's Call log does (SR5); the family stays behind the dialer flag alone. The row is the Call log's inbound row (`INBOUND_LOG_SELECT_SQL`, `projectInboundLogRow`) at `kind: 'inbound_call'`, placed by when the call **arrived** | default, pending Manas |
| CL40 | **How the history's filters and figures read an inbound call** (P6, built 2026-11-22). `channel` gains `inbound`. Each call-only filter means on an inbound call what its own column says (CL13): `outcomes` keeps it only for `connected`, which is a call an agent answered (no inbound call matches another attempt outcome), as `connected_only` does; the agent is who **answered**; the outcome code is the call's own; `campaign_ids` is the campaign it is **filed under** (a call filed under none drops out, as an untagged soft-phone call does); the dates are when it arrived. **Counts stay apart** (CL7): `totals.calls`, `connected` and `last_contacted` are still the calls we **placed**, and the calls received are `totals.inbound { calls, answered }`, shown as "Calls received". The filter options add an answered call's agent, its outcome code, `connected`, and the campaign a call is filed under. A **callback booked on an inbound call** is a `callback` entry at the call's arrival (`source.channel: 'inbound'`), judged like every callback by the next call we placed to the number. *(2026-11-22, review: the summary's open callback also names its booker when it was booked on an inbound call, `latestBookings`' inbound arm: the contact `settleFromInbound` settled.)* The audit's `result_count` (CH10) counts the inbound entries with the rest; nothing else about the audit changed. The page keeps its own words for the channels ("Campaign call", "Soft-phone call") and adds "Inbound call": a history's campaign call includes an agent's own dial on a contact, so the Call log's "Dialler" would be wrong there | default, pending Manas |
| CL41 | **One index, measured first** (P6, built 2026-11-22). `0073`: `idx_agency_inbound_calls_from (tenant_id, account_id, from_e164, arrived_at DESC, id DESC) WHERE from_e164 IS NOT NULL`, walked per visible account in the timeline's keyset order as the soft-phone calls' destination index is (CH9). Without it the only index by caller held the answered calls alone (0033, the sticky agent's), so a number's missed calls were found by reading every ended call of the account. 0033's index is a subset of it and is **kept, with whether to drop it left open for Manas** ([`call-log.md`](call-log.md) §9): on seeded, analysed calls, answered and missed, the sticky read is still planned on 0033's (smaller, no filter), and on calls all answered on 0073's, so it is not known to be unused; dropping it needs a measurement on real data and is its own change, with its own rollback to think through (an image from before 0073 has only 0033's). Unlike roster rows (CH14) the number's inbound calls are not capped: they are found by the caller, like the soft-phone calls dialled to the number, and the count reads every one of them through the index | default, pending Manas |
| CL42 | **Who sees what of an inbound call in the history** (P6, built 2026-11-22). No floor moved: the routes are still `agency.customer_history.read` (CH1) with the search's audit (CH10). Under CH8: the **notes** need `agency.campaigns.read` in the call's account, or the call is the caller's own, which for an inbound call is one **they answered** (`notes_hidden` otherwise); the **Call page** needs `agency.supervise` there **and** a campaign (the inbound Call page is a campaign's, so a call filed under none has no page for anyone); an agent never gets one, and the app opens none. **No media reaches anyone through the history**: the entry has no media leg, no recording link and no voicemail link or key, only `recording` (its state) and `voicemail_seconds`; the players are the Call page's and the Missed list's, under their own rules. **The number is not masked**, as on the rest of the history (AS9, §3): the search is an exact match on a full number the reader typed (CH4), so a row tells them no digit they did not have; what the row adds is the line the customer rang, which is ours. The console's history reads every time with AM / PM in capitals since P6 (`formatWhen`), as the Call log does | default, pending Manas |

## Campaign templates (CT)

Reusable campaign settings at the organisation and at each account (ClickUp "Reusable Default
Campaign Settings at Tenant or Account Level"). Designed 2026-11-21; Manas answered the design's
five questions on 2026-11-22: override by section (CT2), API creates get the template (CT8),
named templates (CT10), the soft phone follows the account's template (CT11), no locked sections
(CT12). The build's and the review's own calls (CT13–CT21, among them CT18's `Asia/Kolkata`
built-in time zone and CT9's amended record) were accepted on 2026-11-22 ("The route taken for open
decision looks good to me."). The fresh-context review added CT22 and CT23 on 2026-11-22. Every
ruling is decided; the full table, CT1–CT23, is in [`campaign-templates.md`](campaign-templates.md)
§2. The two the review added:

| ID | Decision | Status |
|---|---|---|
| CT22 | A template never holds roster-card fact keys: its script may use only the legacy six keys a script with no card takes. Copying from a campaign (Save as template, Copy from a campaign, New template › A copy of a campaign) keeps What agents see but leaves out a script that uses the card's facts, and says so | **Decided** (2026-11-22) |
| CT23 | The task's outcome "categories" are the outcome's behaviour, which the outcome editor already sets per outcome: Success (`is_success`), Call back (`requires_datetime`), Ends the contact (`terminal`) and Do Not Call (`suppress`). There is no separate category field; a template's outcomes carry these as a campaign's do | **Decided** (2026-11-22) |

## Campaign setup (CS)

The rulings CS1–CS21 (four steps shared by New campaign and Settings, purpose presets, one
sentence per button, the agent's view beside the buttons, no languages yet, bad rows counted and
left out, Excel or CSV, the phone column found not asked, the card picked at upload, agents
picked in the builder, hours as choices, one "doesn't pick up" rule, Advanced folded, only what
exists, Start from the builder, Settings anchors kept, and with campaign templates: Start from
above the steps, source chips where each setting is edited, a template's script on Purpose &
buttons, starting points not called templates, the template editor on the same steps) are in
[`campaign-setup.md`](campaign-setup.md) §1. Asked for by Manas on 2026-11-22.

## Carrier account modes (CR)

Rulings CR1–CR15 (2026-11-25; some decided, the rest pending Manas) are in [`carriers.md`](carriers.md) §3: which carrier accounts a call may be placed on (the service's own, a tenant's own, or both), VoiceLink on a tenant's own account, numbers added by hand and pinned to a credential, and what adding a carrier means.
